# BayanHealth Migration Decision Log

Purpose: Record architecture/product/security decisions that are binding for the team.  
Rule: Architecture, product, and security changes require an ADR recorded as part of the same coherent change. Lack of prior named-person approval does not block bounded repository work when the governing specification defines the behavior; governed mutations remain subject to current, unused, operation-specific point-of-action authorization.

---

## Decision Template

- Date:
- Decision ID: `ADR-YYYYMMDD-XX`
- Title:
- Status: `Proposed | Accepted | Superseded | Rejected`
- Owner:
- Context:
- Decision:
- Alternatives considered:
- Consequences:
  - Positive:
  - Trade-offs:
  - Follow-up actions:
- Related files/tickets/PRs:

---

## Accepted Decisions

### ADR-20260416-01: AWS-native serverless rewrite
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Decision: Rebuild as AWS-native serverless architecture in a new implementation repo.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Clean architecture, less legacy carryover.
  - Trade-off: Requires complete API/data migration.

### ADR-20260416-02: Team ownership model
- Status: **Superseded by ADR-20260630-01** (team restructured for 30-day MVP push)
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Decision:
  - Engineer A owns frontend implementation.
  - Engineer B owns backend, cloud infra, AI, and is merge/deploy gatekeeper.

### ADR-20260416-03: Deployment model and environments
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Decision:
  - One AWS account.
  - Environments: `dev`, `staging`, `prod`.
  - Region: `ap-southeast-1` only. _(Regional pivot and rationale: **ADR-20260514-01**.)_
  - Frontend hosted on Amplify (**Terraform-managed app/branches**: **ADR-20260514-03**).
  - IaC with Terraform (**full provisioning scope: ADR-20260514-03**).
  - GitHub Actions with manual promotion approvals; these are protected-environment, operation-specific authorizations at promotion time, not prerequisites for bounded repository implementation.

### ADR-20260416-04: Realtime scope
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Decision:
  - Option A scope + realtime chat.
  - Best-effort realtime.
  - HTTP fallback enabled.
  - Chat follows write-before-emit persistence.

### ADR-20260416-05: AI/CDS behavior and logging
- Status: Accepted (model/inference lines updated **2026-05-25** via **ADR-20260525-01**)
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Decision:
  - Sync target p95 <= 5s.
  - Timeout returns queued/partial; async continuation.
  - CDS inference: external provider per **ADR-20260525-01** and **`architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`** (AI Architecture PRD); **not** Amazon Bedrock.
  - Model fallback chains per PRD (SOAP: 72B → 70B medical → template; patient: 7B → template).
  - The original “full raw prompt/response logging” clause is superseded and constrained by ADR-20260525-01 pre-prompt de-identification and ADR-20260723-01 minimal PHI-safe audit requirements. Operational telemetry excludes raw prompts, responses/generated content, tokens, Assessment text, patient identifiers, normalized input, and unbounded exceptions; retain only explicitly governed minimized artifacts where a later policy permits them.
  - Any governed minimized artifact access follows the later de-identification, retention, assignment, and audited break-glass rules; the older raw-log visibility statement does not revive full prompt/response retention.

### ADR-20260416-06: Data and retention policy
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Decision:
  - DynamoDB rewrite from scratch.
  - Core + audit/AI table strategy.
  - Default retention: 120 days.
  - Overrides for AI logs, payment proofs, medical documents.
  - Legal hold owner: admin.

### ADR-20260416-07: Security and observability baseline
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Decision:
  - WAF enabled in v1.
  - Public serverless endpoints with baseline security controls.
  - CloudWatch log retention 30 days.

### ADR-20260416-08: Backend runtime language
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Need to select a runtime for all Lambda handlers to enable scaffolding, dependency management, and CI setup.
- Decision: Use TypeScript on Node.js 24.x for all Lambda handlers (upgraded from 20.x, 2026-06-10).
- Alternatives considered:
  - Python 3.12: Strong AWS SDK support, but weaker type safety and no FE type sharing.
  - Go: Fastest cold start, but smaller ecosystem for rapid prototyping.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Type safety, shared types with FE (React/TypeScript), fast cold starts, broad tooling.
  - Trade-offs: Team must be proficient in TypeScript.
  - Follow-up actions: Set up `tsconfig.json`, ESLint, and build tooling in `backend/`.
- Related files: `backend/`, `infra/modules/lambda/`

### ADR-20260416-09: Contract source-of-truth format
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Blueprint requires contract references per ticket and communication of contract changes between FE and BE.
- Decision: Use OpenAPI 3.1 YAML as the contract source-of-truth in `contracts/`.
- Alternatives considered:
  - JSON Schema standalone: Less tooling for REST API documentation.
  - TypeSpec: Newer, less mature ecosystem.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Industry standard, enables code generation, request validation, and documentation.
  - Trade-offs: Some 3.1 tooling is still maturing; can fall back to 3.0 if blockers arise.
  - Follow-up actions: Create `contracts/openapi.yaml` and `contracts/README.md`.
- Related files: `contracts/`

### ADR-20260416-12: Idempotency enforcement pattern
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Blueprint requires write-before-emit for chat and safe writes for bookings/payments. Retries must not create duplicates.
- Decision: Use DynamoDB conditional writes with `idempotencyKey` attribute.
  - Client provides `Idempotency-Key` header.
  - Backend writes with `attribute_not_exists(idempotencyKey)` condition.
  - TTL of 24 hours auto-expires idempotency records.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Redis/ElastiCache: Additional cost and infrastructure.
  - Separate idempotency table: More complexity, same DynamoDB cost.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Atomic, low-latency, no additional infra, self-cleaning via TTL.
  - Trade-offs: TTL window must be chosen carefully per use case.
  - Follow-up actions: Add `idempotencyKey` and `idempotencyTTL` to data model; document in contracts.
- Related files: `contracts/`, `backend/handlers/`

### ADR-20260416-13: Cognito RBAC representation
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Blueprint requires patient/doctor/admin role model with JWT-based RBAC middleware.
- Decision: Use Cognito groups (`patient`, `doctor`, `admin`, `moderator`) mapped to `cognito:groups` claim in JWT.
  - Middleware checks group membership for authorization.
  - Assignment checks (doctor-patient scoping) are handled in application logic.
  - `moderator` added 2026-06-15 (Slice 2) for doctor KYC review queue APIs; **removed 2026-09-07 — superseded by ADR-20260907-01**, which reduces the group set to `patient` / `doctor` / `admin`.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Custom attributes: Harder to manage, not array-friendly for multi-role users.
  - External identity provider: Overkill for v1.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Native Cognito feature, direct JWT claim access, supports multi-role users.
  - Trade-offs: Cannot encode complex hierarchies without additional application logic.
  - Follow-up actions: Create groups in Cognito Terraform module; implement auth middleware.
- Related files: `infra/modules/cognito/`, `backend/lib/auth.ts`

### ADR-20260514-01: Primary AWS region (`ap-southeast-1`)
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Pivot to Singapore (`ap-southeast-1`) before real users/data exist. Bedrock is no longer the CDS anchor (ADR-20260525-01), and Singapore is the better default latency fit for Philippines-based patients/doctors.
- Decision: Single-region deployment SHALL use **`ap-southeast-1`** across compute, data plane, Amplify-hosted FE, Cognito user pools/regional endpoints, logging, Terraform defaults, and environment naming/tags.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: One region simplifies networking, quotas, KMS keys, IAM conditions, DR assumptions, and improves Southeast Asia / Philippines API latency.
  - Trade-offs: Existing pre-user infrastructure is treated as disposable/migratable; Terraform backend must be bootstrapped/migrated to Singapore.
  - Follow-up actions: CDS inference is external (HTTPS); no in-region Bedrock requirement (**ADR-20260525-01**).
- Supersedes: prior region clause introduced during the Bedrock-oriented phase.
- Related files: `README.md`, `infra/`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`

### ADR-20260525-01: CDS inference per AI Architecture PRD (Together.ai; no Bedrock)
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Date: 2026-05-25
- Context: Bedrock model access (`qwen.qwen3-next-80b-a3b`) blocked in-account; product AI design is already defined in **`architecture/BayanHealth AI Architecture PRD v1.0.pdf`** (Qwen2.5-72B SOAP, OpenBioLLM-70B fallback, Qwen2.5-7B patient cards, template fallbacks). MVP scale ~50 patients and ~5 doctors/month; ~USD 50/mo inference budget; de-identified prompts only; Philippine DPA with US transit acceptable.
- Decision:
  - **Authoritative AI design:** AI Architecture PRD + **`architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`** (engineering binding).
  - **No Amazon Bedrock** for CDS in v1; **no planned Bedrock cutover**.
  - **Provider (MVP):** Together.ai via OpenAI-compatible HTTPS from Lambda; API key in Secrets Manager (`TOGETHER_API_KEY`).
  - **SOAP (Layer 3):** `Qwen/Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct` → `OpenBioLLM/OpenBioLLM-70B` → deterministic solver template fill.
  - **Patient cards (Layer 5):** `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct` → English solver template.
  - **Client:** provider-agnostic `backend/src/lib/cds/` (`profiles.ts`, `client.ts`).
  - **Pre-prompt de-identification** and post-output validation per PRD before any model call / display.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Amazon Bedrock (Qwen3 80B): rejected — access blocked; superseded by PRD-aligned external stack.
  - OpenRouter as primary: deferred; optional latency spike only.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Unblocks Week 5 CDS slice; matches PRD; low cost at MVP volume; fallback chain improves resilience.
  - Trade-offs: Cross-border inference; vendor dependency; measure p95 from `ap-southeast-1` to Together.ai.
  - Follow-up actions: Wire Secrets Manager + CDS Lambda; implement de-id filter and SOAP validators; CloudWatch metrics on fallback depth.
- Supersedes: **ADR-20260514-02** (Bedrock Qwen3 80B); amends **ADR-20260416-05** (fallback chain + inference provider).
- Related files: `architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`, `backend/src/lib/cds/`, `architecture/BayanHealth AI Architecture PRD v1.0.pdf`

### ADR-20260514-03: Terraform-only cloud provisioning
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Console- or wizard-provisioned resources drift from `dev`/`staging`/`prod` and bypass review; Amplify and the rest of the stack should share one operational model.
- Decision:
  - All **long-lived AWS resources** for BayanHealth MVP (including **Amplify** frontend hosting, Cognito, API Gateway, Lambda, DynamoDB, S3, KMS, WAF, CloudWatch, Step Functions, IAM, and Secrets Manager for inference API keys) SHALL be defined in **Terraform** under `infra/`, merged via PR.
  - Exceptional **short-lived** debugging or break-glass actions in the console MUST be **reverted or imported into Terraform** immediately and noted in the PR or `architecture/DECISIONS.md` decision log if policy-relevant.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Terraform for data plane only with Amplify Console for hosting: rejected to avoid split operational model and drift.
  - AWS CDK / CloudFormation: deferred; team standard is Terraform (**ADR-20260416-03**).
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Single source of truth, reproducible environments, plan/apply in CI, fewer region and naming mismatches.
  - Trade-offs: Up-front effort to model Amplify and edge cases in HCL; provider limitations may require small workarounds documented in `infra/README.md`.
  - Follow-up actions: Configure **remote state** (S3 + DynamoDB lock) before shared `apply`; add modules incrementally (Cognito, API, data stores, Amplify app).
- Related files: `infra/`, `.github/workflows/`
- Expands: IaC commitment in **ADR-20260416-03** with explicit full-stack scope.

### ADR-20260618-01: Payment gateway — PayRex integration
- Date: 2026-06-18
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: BayanHealth needs a production payment gateway that supports Philippine payment methods (GCash, PayMaya, online banking, credit/debit cards) with a hold/capture pattern matching the existing booking lifecycle (`pending_payment → confirmed → in_progress → completed`). The prior ledger simulation was sufficient for backend slices 1–10 but is not a real payment processor.
- Decision:
  - **Provider (MVP):** PayRex — Philippine-native gateway supporting GCash, PayMaya, BPI/BDO/Metrobank/UnionBank online banking, and credit/debit cards.
  - **Adapter pattern:** `PaymentGatewayAdapter` interface in `backend/src/lib/payments.ts` abstracts hold/capture/refund/getStatus. `LedgerAdapter` remains as the simulated fallback; `PayRexAdapter` is the production implementation in `backend/src/lib/payrex-adapter.ts`.
  - **Circuit breaker:** `CircuitBreakerAdapter` in `backend/src/lib/circuit-breaker.ts` wraps `PayRexAdapter`. On 5 consecutive failures the circuit opens and all payment operations automatically fall back to `LedgerAdapter` for the 5-minute recovery window. State is container-persistent (intentional — avoids hammering a degraded PayRex API on every invocation).
  - **Credentials:** PayRex API key, secret key, and webhook secret stored in AWS Secrets Manager (`payrex-credentials-{env}`). Loaded with a 6-hour cache via `backend/src/lib/secrets.ts`. Never logged.
  - **Provider selection:** `PAYMENTS_GATEWAY_PROVIDER` env var (`'ledger'` | `'payrex'`). Defaults to `'ledger'`. Set to `'payrex'` per environment when credentials are configured. Gradual rollout controlled by `migration-control.ts` at the adapter layer.
  - **Webhook endpoint:** `POST /v1/webhooks/payrex` — no JWT; HMAC-SHA256 signature verification using PayRex-Timestamp + PayRex-Signature headers. Idempotent via PayRex event ID. Defined in `backend/src/handlers/payrex-webhooks.ts` and `infra/modules/http_api/payrex-webhook.tf`.
  - **PCI DSS posture:** PayRex-hosted payment forms only. No card data touches BayanHealth infrastructure. `providerReference` (session/transaction ID) is stored; no PANs, CVVs, or account numbers.
  - **Monitoring:** CloudWatch custom metrics (`PayRex/*`) emitted per operation; alarms on success rate < 95% and p95 response > 5s. Dashboard in `infra/modules/http_api/monitoring.tf`.
- Alternatives considered:
  - PayMongo: Strong PH coverage but no hold/capture pattern; capture requires workaround.
  - Stripe: Excellent API but GCash/PayMaya support is via third-party redirect, not native.
  - Ledger-only (permanent simulation): Rejected — not a real payment processor; no patient funds settlement.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Real PH payment method support; automatic ledger fallback preserves uptime during PayRex outages; existing API surface and booking lifecycle unchanged.
  - Trade-offs: External vendor dependency; PayRex sandbox must be used in dev/staging; PHP-only currency (hard requirement matches PH market).
  - Follow-up actions: Configure `payrex-credentials-{dev,staging,prod}` in Secrets Manager; set `PAYMENTS_GATEWAY_PROVIDER=payrex` per environment in Terraform; test full hold→webhook→capture flow in staging before production rollout.
- Related files: `backend/src/lib/payments.ts`, `backend/src/lib/payrex-adapter.ts`, `backend/src/lib/payrex-client.ts`, `backend/src/lib/circuit-breaker.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/payrex-webhooks.ts`, `infra/modules/http_api/payrex-webhook.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/monitoring.tf`, `.kiro/specs/payrex-integration/`

### ADR-20260618-02: Break-glass DynamoDB key redesign (M11/M12)
- Date: 2026-06-18
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Security scan `scan-59cc01e5` identified two vulnerabilities in the break-glass grant data model. M11: grants were keyed by `adminUserId` (`pk: ADMIN#BREAK_GLASS#<adminUserId>`), meaning only the owning admin could list and revoke their own grants — a compromised admin could create grants that no other admin could cancel. M12: grants carried no scoping fields, giving the admin unrestricted access to **all** clinical AI data across all consultations and patients for the full grant duration.
- Decision:
  - **Shared partition key:** Change `pk` from `ADMIN#BREAK_GLASS#<adminUserId>` to the constant `pk: 'BREAK_GLASS'`. Sort key remains `sk: GRANT#<expiresAt>#<grantId>`. This places all active grants in a single partition, enabling any admin to list system-wide grants and any admin to revoke any grant.
  - **Cross-admin revocation endpoint:** `DELETE /v1/admin/break-glass/grants/{grantId}` enforces no ownership check — any admin with a valid JWT can revoke any grant.
  - **Resource scoping fields:** Grant records now carry `consultationId` and `patientId` fields (optional). When present, the CDS access check verifies the accessed consultation matches the grant scope.
  - **Key builder:** `breakGlassGrantSharedKey()` in `lib/dynamo.ts` returns `{ pk: 'BREAK_GLASS' }`. Legacy `breakGlassGrantPrimaryKey(adminUserId)` is retained for backwards-compatible reading during migration.
  - **Existing grants:** Grants created under the old key structure remain valid until their TTL expires; the new read path uses the shared partition.
- Alternatives considered:
  - GSI on `adminUserId`: Would allow cross-admin listing but not revocation without also changing the primary key.
  - Separate `BREAK_GLASS_GRANT` table: Simpler isolation but adds operational complexity and loses single-table transaction guarantees.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Any admin can now revoke any active grant system-wide; resource-scoped grants prevent over-privileged break-glass access; closes M11 and M12.
  - Trade-offs: Grant items must be migrated or left to TTL-expire under the old key. The new key concentrates all grants in one partition (acceptable — break-glass events are rare).
  - Follow-up actions: Monitor for stale grants under old key prefix via a one-time DynamoDB scan during next maintenance window.
- Related findings: M11, M12 (scan-59cc01e5)
- Related files: `backend/src/lib/break-glass.ts`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/admin.ts`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`

### ADR-20260624-01: Solver content store for CDS grounding (Phase 1 RAG)
- Date: 2026-06-24
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Patient communication cards (Layer 5) hallucinated when the LLM (Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-Turbo) paraphrased lifestyle templates without grounded source passages. SOAP generation (Layer 3) already uses `cpgPassages` and `solverTemplate` effectively. The patient card pipeline needed the same grounding treatment. Additionally, Together.ai retired serverless access to the original model slugs (Qwen/Qwen2.5-72B-Instruct, OpenBioLLM/OpenBioLLM-70B, Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct); all profiles now use `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-Turbo` with increased timeout budgets (15s SOAP, 10s patient card, 20s sync budget) pending model tier upgrades.
- Decision:
  - **Solver content entity:** New `solver_content` entity in `app_core` (pk: `SOLVER#<solverId>`, sk: `CONTENT#<layer>`). Stores pre-extracted lifestyle passages, return precautions, and patient education content per solver and language.
  - **Tiered retrieval:** Embedded content registry in `backend/src/lib/cds/solver-content.ts` provides instant fallback (~0ms); DynamoDB lookup is attempted first to allow content updates without Lambda redeploy.
  - **Prompt grounding:** Patient card prompt now receives `solverPassages` (cited lifestyle guidelines) and `returnPrecautions`. System prompt enforces "rephrase ONLY from provided context" with citation traceability.
  - **Phase 2 (future):** Vector similarity search via OpenSearch Serverless for cross-condition retrieval when knowledge base grows beyond pre-mapped solvers. No code changes needed to the prompt layer — additional passages are appended to the same `solverPassages` array.
  - **Phase 3 (future):** Admin content management UI for doctor/medical officer curation of solver passages without engineering intervention.
  - **Model profiles updated:** All CDS profiles now use `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-Turbo` (Together.ai serverless). Timeouts increased from 4.5s to 15s (model) / 20s (sync budget) to accommodate serverless cold starts.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Real-time PDF parsing with embedding at query time: Rejected — adds 2-3s latency per call and requires an embedding model.
  - Pure DynamoDB without embedded fallback: Requires seeding before first use; embedded registry provides zero-config baseline.
  - Vector-only approach from day one: Over-engineered for 5 solvers; DynamoDB point-reads are faster and simpler.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Eliminates hallucination in patient cards; grounded content with citations; content updatable without code deploy; same pattern as SOAP layer; scalable path to full RAG.
  - Trade-offs: Embedded content requires code update when adding new solvers (acceptable at MVP scale of ~5-10 solvers); DynamoDB item size limit (~400KB) constrains per-solver content volume.
  - Follow-up actions: Seed DynamoDB with all solver content from CDS Solver Library PDF; expand solver registry as new conditions are onboarded; evaluate vector store when solver count exceeds 20.
- Related files: `backend/src/lib/cds/solver-content.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/patient-card.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/cds.ts`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`

### ADR-20260618-03: WebSocket connection token exchange (M9)
- Date: 2026-06-18
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: Security scan `scan-59cc01e5` identified M9: the WebSocket `$connect` handler required the full Cognito JWT as a `?token=<JWT>` query parameter. API Gateway writes query parameters to access logs, CloudWatch, CDN/WAF/proxy logs — exposing full bearer credentials to anyone with log read access. This is a well-known WebSocket authentication anti-pattern.
- Decision:
  - **Short-lived token endpoint:** Add `POST /v1/ws-token` (HTTP API, JWT-authenticated). Issues a single-use, 30-second TTL opaque token stored in DynamoDB under `pk: WS_TOKEN#<tokenHash>`. Returns `{ token: "<opaque>" }` to the client.
  - **`$connect` handler change:** The WebSocket `$connect` handler reads `?wsToken=<opaque>` from the query string, looks up the token in DynamoDB, validates it has not expired and has not been used, marks it consumed (delete), and proceeds with the connection. The Cognito JWT is never sent in a WebSocket query parameter.
  - **Token TTL:** 30 seconds (`Ttl.WsToken = 30` in `lib/dynamo.ts`). DynamoDB TTL auto-expires unused tokens.
  - **Entity type:** `EntityType.WsToken = 'ws_token'` in `lib/dynamo.ts`.
  - **Key shape:** `wsTokenPrimaryKey(tokenHash)` returns `{ pk: 'WS_TOKEN#<tokenHash>', sk: 'WS_TOKEN#<tokenHash>' }`.
  - **No replay:** Token is deleted on first use; a second `$connect` with the same token is rejected with 401.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Signed cookies via API Gateway: More complex; requires CloudFront in front of API GW WebSocket stage.
  - `Authorization` header at `$connect`: API Gateway WebSocket does not support custom headers at `$connect` time.
  - Longer TTL (e.g. 5 minutes): Wider replay window; 30 seconds is tight enough for the client to immediately use the token.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: JWT bearer credentials no longer appear in any log stream. The short-lived token has no value outside the 30-second window. Closes M9.
  - Trade-offs: Additional round-trip (HTTP token request → WebSocket connect). Client must obtain the token immediately before opening the WebSocket connection.
  - Follow-up actions: Frontend WebSocket connection flow must be updated to call `POST /v1/ws-token` first and pass `wsToken` query param.
- Related findings: M9 (scan-59cc01e5)
- Related files: `backend/src/handlers/chat-ws.ts`, `backend/src/lib/ws-connections.ts`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`

### ADR-20260629-01: Pre-production cost optimization (CDS inference + observability right-sizing)
- Date: 2026-06-29
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Backend/Cloud/AI Engineer
- Context: As the platform moves from development to a testing phase, a codebase cost sweep identified avoidable spend. The dominant variable cost driver is Together.ai CDS inference (PayRex is disabled in dev); secondary costs are CloudWatch log storage, S3 retention, and Lambda over-provisioning. None of these required architectural change — only right-sizing and a few cost guards.
- Decision:
  - **CDS fallback de-duplication:** `completeWithChain` / `completeWithChainSafe` now collapse the profile chain to one entry per distinct resolved model (`dedupeChainByModel` in `lib/cds/client.ts`). When the SOAP primary and fallback resolve to the same model slug, the fallback is skipped and the deterministic template path handles failures. Eliminates double-billing a failed model with no resilience benefit. Distinct fallback models are preserved.
  - **Configurable token budget:** SOAP/patient `maxTokens` are overridable via `CDS_SOAP_MAX_TOKENS` / `CDS_PATIENT_MAX_TOKENS` (`lib/cds/profiles.ts`). Dev sets `CDS_SOAP_MAX_TOKENS=2048` (code default remains 4096 for staging/prod).
  - **CDS inference cache (opt-in):** New `cds_inference_cache` entity (`CDS_CACHE#<sha256>`) caches identical, PII-checked completions with a short TTL. Gated by `CDS_CACHE_ENABLED` (default **false**; enabled only in dev) and `CDS_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS` (default 3600). Cache read/write failures are swallowed so caching never reduces inference reliability. Only completions that already passed input + output PII checks are stored, so cache keys/values never contain PII. See ADR-20260629-01 entry in `DATA_MODEL.md`.
  - **Log retention right-sizing:** Non-audit Lambda + API Gateway access log groups now use `var.lambda_log_retention_days` (default 30). Dev overrides to 7. PayRex **audit** logs remain at `var.log_retention_days` (90) for compliance.
  - **S3 lifecycle right-sizing (dev):** `data_stores` module exposes `ai_raw_logs_expiration_days` (default 120), `media_expiration_days` (default 365), and `media_enable_glacier_transition` (default true). Dev overrides to 30 / 90 / false respectively. GLACIER_IR transition is disabled in dev because its 90-day minimum storage charge makes it more expensive than Standard for short-lived dev objects.
  - **Lambda right-sizing:** Lightweight handlers (`health`, `otl`, `prescription-verify`, `intake-queue`) reduced from 256 MB to 128 MB across all environments.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Making the SOAP fallback a genuinely cheaper/smaller model instead of de-duplicating: deferred — no clearly cheaper reliable Together slug than the current 7B-Turbo, and the deterministic template already covers failures. De-dup is defensive and config-independent.
  - Caching at the handler/draft layer rather than the inference client: rejected — the client chain is the single choke point for both SOAP and patient-card, and keeping the cache there preserves the existing PII guarantees.
  - Enabling the inference cache in production: rejected — caching clinical drafts across consultations is a safety risk; the cache is dev/testing only.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Lower Together.ai spend (no double-billed fallback, smaller dev token budget, cache hits on replayed test scenarios), lower CloudWatch/S3 storage cost, smaller Lambda GB-second footprint. All production-affecting defaults are unchanged; cost savings are dev-scoped except the model de-dup and Lambda right-sizing (both safe everywhere).
  - Trade-offs: A new opt-in entity type coexists in `app_core`. Reduced Lambda memory slightly lowers CPU for the affected lightweight handlers (acceptable given their trivial workloads).
  - Follow-up actions (completed 2026-06-29): Lambda packaging is now a single parallel esbuild pass (`backend/scripts/package-lambdas.mjs`, `npm run package:lambdas`); CI and deploy workflows cache `backend/dist` (keyed on backend source hash) so deploy reuses CI-built artifacts on a cache hit; the Amplify build is triggered earlier so its build overlaps with verification/smoke, and the poll window is tightened (30s interval, 15-minute ceiling) to cut idle runner minutes.
  - Follow-up actions (completed 2026-06-29, second pass): (a) the notifications reminder EventBridge rule (`rate(1 minute)`, 24/7 in every environment regardless of real traffic) is now gated by `reminder_schedule_enabled` — disabled entirely in dev, reduced to `rate(5 minutes)` in staging, unchanged (`rate(1 minute)`, enabled) in prod; (b) the PayRex CloudWatch dashboard (`aws_cloudwatch_dashboard.payrex_payment_health`) is now gated on `payrex_enabled == "true"` so it isn't billed in environments where PayRex is off (dev); (c) the four PayRex webhook alarms in `payrex-webhook.tf` (`payrex_webhook_failure_rate`, `payrex_webhook_invalid_signatures`, `payrex_webhook_duration`, `payrex_webhook_errors`) were unconditionally created (only their `alarm_actions` were gated) — now gated the same way as the dashboard, for consistency with the already-gated alarms in `monitoring.tf`.
- Related files: `backend/src/lib/cds/client.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/profiles.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/cache.ts`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `backend/scripts/package-lambdas.mjs`, `backend/package.json`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/deploy-dev.yml`, `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/variables.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/monitoring.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/payrex-webhook.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/outputs.tf`, `infra/modules/notifications/main.tf`, `infra/modules/notifications/variables.tf`, `infra/modules/data_stores/main.tf`, `infra/modules/data_stores/variables.tf`, `infra/environments/dev/main.tf`, `infra/environments/staging/main.tf`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`

### ADR-20260630-01: Team restructure for 30-day MVP push
- Date: 2026-06-30
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: The two-person model (ADR-20260416-02: Engineer A frontend, Engineer B backend/cloud/AI) is no longer sufficient to ship the MVP on the compressed timeline. The team is expanding to four roles and committing to ship the MVP within the next 30 days. Frontend work is split between net-new feature delivery and hardening/design-mapping of existing surfaces, and a dedicated UI/UX designer is added to own the Stitch scratchpad and branding.
- Decision:
  - **Dev A — Lead Engineer (Backend + Cloud + AI + DevOps):** owns Terraform/AWS infra, Lambda domain logic, DynamoDB schema/access patterns, WebSocket handlers, CDS + safety backend, AI logging/retention/break-glass, security, observability, and the CI/CD release pipeline. Remains **merge/deploy gatekeeper** (formerly Engineer B).
  - **Dev B — Frontend Developer (new features):** implements net-new frontend features against the AWS APIs, contract-first against `contracts/openapi.yaml` (formerly Engineer A).
  - **Dev C — Frontend Developer (existing features + design mapping):** hardens and improves existing frontend features and maps the **Stitch scratchpad** to the live UI.
  - **Dev D — UI/UX Designer:** owns the Stitch scratchpad, branding, and webapp design. Ideally produces design artifacts/specs and does **not** commit code.
  - **Delivery target:** ship the MVP for controlled live testing within 30 days of this ADR.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Keep the two-person model: rejected — insufficient frontend throughput for the timeline.
  - Single combined frontend role: rejected — separating new-feature build from existing-surface hardening + design mapping reduces context-switching and merge contention.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: clearer ownership lanes, parallel frontend streams, dedicated design ownership, preserved single-gatekeeper merge discipline.
  - Trade-offs: more PR coordination across two frontend devs; the gatekeeper (Dev A) remains a potential review bottleneck under the compressed schedule.
  - Follow-up actions: refresh ownership/role references in `README.md`, `architecture/30_60_90_PLAN.md`, and the steering docs (`product.md`, `structure.md`).
- Related files: `README.md`, `architecture/30_60_90_PLAN.md`, `.kiro/steering/product.md`, `.kiro/steering/structure.md`

### ADR-20260630-02: Drug reference data layer in `app_core` (drug-data-dynamodb)
- Date: 2026-06-30
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: The CDS pipeline needs a canonical drug reference (generic ingredients, marketed products, clinical warnings) to ground prescription guidance in SOAP Plan generation and to validate prescribed drug names. Rather than a new table or external service, the data is mapped into the existing `app_core` single-table using sparse population of the shared `gsi1`/`gsi2`/`gsi3` indexes — no new GSIs or tables required.
- Decision:
  - **Three new entity types** in `app_core`: `generic_drug`, `marketed_product`, `clinical_warning` (registered in `lib/dynamo.ts` `EntityType`/`SchemaVersion`).
  - **Single partition per drug** (`pk = DRUG#<drugId>`) groups the metadata item (`sk = METADATA`), products (`sk = PRODUCT#<productId>`), and warnings (`sk = WARNING#<warningId>` or `sk = PRODUCT_WARNING#<productId>#<warningId>`) so a drug and all its children fetch in one Query.
  - **Sparse GSI reuse:** `gsi1` (`RXCUI#<rxCui>`) point lookup by RxNorm CUI; `gsi2` (`DRUG_CLASS#<class>`) therapeutic-class listing; `gsi3` (`DRUG_NAME#<firstLetter>` / `<nameLower>#<drugId>`) prefix name search. `gsi2` attributes are omitted when `therapeuticClass` is empty.
  - **RxCui uniqueness** enforced via a transactional sentinel-item write (`pk = sk = RXCUI#<rxCui>` + `attribute_not_exists`).
  - **Soft-delete with cascade:** `isActive=false` propagates to all children via TransactWriteItems batched at ≤100 items; TTL (365 days) handles eventual physical deletion. Optimistic locking on `updatedAt` → `STATE_CONFLICT` (409).
  - **New `lambda-drugs` handler** (`handlers/drugs.ts`) serves 7 routes (admin-write CRUD + doctor/admin read/search) using the multi-route-per-Lambda pattern with platform RBAC, idempotency, and response envelopes.
  - **CDS consumes drug data via an inlined library** (`lib/drug-data.ts` → `cdsLookupByName`, `cdsValidateDrugExists`), not HTTP — p95 < 10ms; functions never throw (return `{ found: false }` on error/no-match).
  - **Bulk seed CLI** (`scripts/seed-drugs.ts`) uses `BatchWriteItem` (≤25/batch) with exponential-backoff retry and a stdout summary report.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Separate drug table or external drug API: rejected — single-table point-reads are faster/simpler at MVP scale and avoid extra infra and a network hop on the CDS hot path.
  - New dedicated GSIs: rejected — sparse population of the existing three GSIs covers all required access patterns at near-zero added cost.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: grounded, validated drug references for CDS; no new tables/GSIs; consistent with existing single-table conventions; clear path to scale via seeding.
  - Trade-offs: three more entity types coexist in `app_core`; GSI key-space is now shared across clinical and drug entities (mitigated by distinct prefixes); sentinel-item uniqueness adds a write per drug create.
  - Follow-up actions: author OpenAPI paths/schemas for the drug routes in `contracts/openapi.yaml`; seed `app_core` from the CDS Solver Library / DynamoDB Drug Data Spec; add a `smoke:drugs-*` script; deploy `lambda-drugs` to dev+staging.
- Related files: `backend/src/handlers/drugs.ts`, `backend/src/lib/drug-data.ts`, `backend/src/lib/drug-validation.ts`, `backend/scripts/seed-drugs.ts`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/soap.ts`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/variables.tf`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`, `.kiro/specs/drug-data-dynamodb/`

### ADR-20260703-01: Assessment-first, post-consult-only CDS generation (PRD v3.2 harness-only pivot)
- Date: 2026-07-03
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: `architecture/QA feedback v1.pdf` ("BayanHealth AI Clinical Copilot PRD v3.2 — Post-Consult, Assessment-First, Harness-Only") supersedes the AI Architecture PRD v1.0's implicit timing model. The corrected behavior: AI may organize Subjective/Objective support **before or during** the consult, but Assessment, Plan, Rx, final ICD, medical certificate, lab/imaging request, and patient education remain **locked until the physician selects or types an Assessment**, post-consult. This is a correction to the prior model where SOAP (including Assessment/Plan) could be generated as a single one-shot draft during or immediately after the consult. The synthetic test fixture `bayanhealth_prd_v3_synthetic_intake_test_cases.csv` (40 cases) already encodes this gated behavior and was committed ahead of this ADR — this entry retroactively documents and ratifies that direction as binding.
- Decision:
  - **Global invariant:** No physician-confirmed Assessment = no Plan, no Rx, no final ICD, no medcert, no lab/imaging request, and no patient education.
  - **Assessment field starts blank.** The doctor must select from a diagnosis-name-only picker (search/scroll/manual entry) or type a diagnosis manually. No AI confidence scores, solver names, or "AI diagnosis" wording are ever shown to the doctor — backend may silently rerank candidates, but rank mechanics are never surfaced in the UI.
  - **CDS draft model changes:** the current `CdsDraftLayer` (`'soap' | 'patient-card'`) treats Assessment as one field within a single-shot SOAP draft. This must be restructured so Assessment is a distinct, doctor-gated step — either a new draft layer or a decoupled generation phase — separate from Plan/Rx/document drafting, which only unlock after Assessment is confirmed.
  - **Protected endpoint contract:** every post-consult generation endpoint (Plan, Rx, medcert, patient card) must require `consultation_id`, `assessment_version`, `physician_actor_id`, and a signed `gate_token`. If the Assessment is edited or cleared, the token is revoked and dependent outputs are marked stale, requiring regeneration or manual doctor action.
  - **Backend enforces the gate, not the frontend.** A frontend unlock is never proof that downstream generation is allowed — every protected endpoint must independently recheck consultation state, Assessment version, and gate token server-side.
  - **Red-flag router runs twice:** once before candidate/diagnosis display, and again before any post-Assessment generation. Emergency disposition suppresses routine suggestions and all care-plan generation, and cannot be bypassed through normal UI or retry.
  - **Deterministic-first, harness-only strategy carried forward:** no additional model fine-tuning/distillation is in scope for this pilot. Reliability comes from schemas, deterministic gates, validators, citation binding, and physician approval — not from making the LLM "smarter." This is consistent with, and extends, ADR-20260525-01's provider/fallback-chain decision; it does not change the inference provider.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Keep the existing one-shot SOAP generation (Assessment as a field, not a gate): rejected — this is the exact behavior PRD v3.2 identifies as the core correction, citing risk of AI-suggested diagnosis/ICD biasing the physician before independent clinical judgment is exercised.
  - Enforce the gate only in the frontend (hide locked fields via UI state): rejected — PRD v3.2 §5 explicitly requires backend re-validation on every protected endpoint; a client-side-only gate is trivially bypassable and does not meet the safety bar for a clinical product.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: matches the corrected clinical-safety model; keeps the physician as the actor of record for every diagnosis; reduces anchoring bias risk from AI-suggested assessments; existing harness components (guardrails, audit, de-identification) are reused, not replaced.
  - Trade-offs: requires restructuring `lib/cds/drafts.ts` and `handlers/cds.ts` to support a distinct Assessment-confirmation step and stale-artifact invalidation on Assessment change; requires new diagnosis-card catalog (first 20 full solvers + 80 reference-only cards per PRD §8) and a red-flag router that does not yet exist in `lib/cds/guardrails.ts`; frontend Assessment field, diagnosis picker, and CPG Preview components must be built from scratch (none exist today — current `AssessmentTextEditor.tsx` is free-text, not a gated picker).
  - Follow-up actions: update `architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md` §1 (six-layer pipeline) to reflect Assessment as a separate, doctor-gated step (done alongside this ADR); update `architecture/scenarios/consultation/During Consultation.md`, which currently describes AI drafting SOAP "in background" during the live consult — this scenario is stale relative to this ADR and needs rewriting or deprecation; design and implement the protected-endpoint gate-token contract in `backend/src/lib/cds/`; seed the first 20 diagnosis solver cards.
- Related files: `architecture/QA feedback v1.pdf`, `architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`, `architecture/bayanhealth_prd_v3_synthetic_intake_test_cases.csv`, `backend/src/lib/cds/drafts.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/guardrails.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/cds.ts`, `architecture/scenarios/consultation/During Consultation.md`

### ADR-20260710-01: Approved MVP infrastructure budget — Low tier (cost-optimized pilot)
- Date: 2026-07-10
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: A three-tier monthly infrastructure budget proposal (Low / Medium / High) was prepared for and reviewed by the C-Suite, scoped to the AWS-native serverless stack in `ap-southeast-1`. The C-Suite **approved the Low tier** for the pilot. The Low tier is not a bare-bones cut — it is the full security + AI stack running under a fixed set of cost controls. This ADR ratifies the approved budget envelope, the cost-optimization stack it assumes, and the scope decisions that follow from it (most notably a reduction of telemetry from 12 events to 7, and a WAF + GuardDuty + AWS Config security baseline that explicitly defers Security Hub, Macie, and external audit tooling to later tiers). This supersedes the informal "~USD 50/mo inference envelope" and "USD 60–100 target" figures in prior docs.
- Pilot volume basis: 3,500–4,000 consults/month, 5–10 doctors, ~8 min average consult.
- Decision:
  - **Approved monthly envelope (Low tier):** **₱10,369–19,215 (~USD 170–315)** all-in across frontend hosting, backend integration, AI inference, CI/CD, database & storage, observability, and cybersecurity. AWS Budgets + anomaly-detection alarms SHALL be configured to this envelope once real usage data exists.
  - **Cost-optimization stack (assumed active at all tiers, mandatory at Low):**
    - **Caching** — CDS inference cache pattern (`CDS_CACHE_ENABLED`, ADR-20260629-01) and cache-safe reuse of deterministic red-flag / drug-validator lookups.
    - **RAG grounding** — solver content store (ADR-20260624-01) to shrink prompts and cut hallucination-driven regenerations.
    - **LLM token optimization + response-length caps** — `CDS_SOAP_MAX_TOKENS` / `CDS_PATIENT_MAX_TOKENS`, `dedupeChainByModel`, `CDS_SYNC_BUDGET_MS`.
    - **Rate limiting** — API Gateway throttling / usage plans to cap abuse-driven invocation spikes ($0 direct cost, protective).
    - **S3 lifecycle policies** — environment-tiered expiration + GLACIER_IR transition (`ai_raw_logs_expiration_days`, `media_expiration_days`, `media_enable_glacier_transition`), per ADR-20260629-01.
  - **Telemetry scope reduced from 12 events to 7 (curated for future healthcare/teleconsult model training).** The 7 events persisted as DynamoDB telemetry entities are:
    1. Intake/booking submission (symptoms, triage answers, service type, demographic bracket)
    2. Consult session start/end (duration, channel, specialty)
    3. AI draft/document generation (model/profile, latency, fallback depth)
    4. Doctor-edit/diff classification (AI draft vs. doctor-final; useful/incomplete/wrong/unsafe)
    5. CDS guardrail/red-flag disposition (which rule fired, disposition)
    6. Follow-up/re-consult (return visit for same complaint, escalation)
    7. Prescription/QR verification (which prescriptions are verified/dispensed)
    The remaining prior "CEO-12" events — **signup, payment, doctor assignment, cancellation, refund, generic errors, latency/drop-off** — are demoted to lightweight **CloudWatch log lines**, not DynamoDB telemetry entities. They remain observable for product/ops but are not stored as training-grade telemetry. This cuts telemetry write/storage volume ~40% with no loss of ML-relevant signal.
  - **Security baseline (Low tier):** **WAF + GuardDuty + AWS Config.** WAF is already in scope (ADR-20260416-07); **GuardDuty and AWS Config are added to the Terraform-managed baseline** under `infra/` (ADR-20260514-03). **Security Hub, Amazon Macie, and any external pentest / SOC2-HIPAA-readiness audit retainer are explicitly OUT of scope at Low** and deferred to the Medium/High tiers.
  - **AI inference posture:** the approved envelope funds **real Together.ai inference at the optimized cost (~₱244–915 / USD 4–15/mo)** at pilot volume. The **enablement timing** of the real `TOGETHER_API_KEY` in staging/prod still follows the Week 4 go/no-go gate (`30_60_90_PLAN.md` Phase 1); this ADR removes the *budget* blocker, not the *readiness* gate. Model tier stays on the efficient 7B-Turbo class — upgrade to 70B/235B is a High-tier decision, evidence-gated on doctor-acceptance-rate data.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Medium tier now (adds Security Hub, X-Ray/canaries, full 12-event telemetry, provisioned concurrency): rejected for the pilot — cost per consult is only justified once volume/finding-density grows.
  - High tier now (adds Macie, external audit, model upgrade, cross-region DR): rejected — premium capability with no pilot-stage justification.
  - Keep 12-event telemetry at Low: rejected — 5 of the 12 carry little ML-training value and inflate DynamoDB write/storage cost; logging them to CloudWatch preserves ops visibility at a fraction of the cost.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: predictable ~USD 170–315/mo pilot spend; per-consult cost ₱2.76–5.12; security baseline (WAF/GuardDuty/Config) covers the highest-signal-per-dollar controls; telemetry still captures every ML-relevant signal for future model training.
  - Trade-offs: no findings-aggregation layer (Security Hub) or continuous PHI scanning (Macie) at Low — mitigated by GuardDuty/Config coverage + the existing manual security re-scan cadence (`scan-59cc01e5` tooling). **Macie + an external audit must re-enter the budget once real PHI flows in prod at volume** — a "when", not an "if".
  - Follow-up actions: (a) add GuardDuty + AWS Config to Terraform baseline under `infra/`; (b) build the telemetry ingestion endpoint for the **7** approved events (not 12) and route the demoted 5 to CloudWatch logs; (c) configure AWS Budgets + anomaly alarms to the approved envelope; (d) revisit tier at Phase 2 (pilot expansion / PayRex live) against real cost-per-consult data.
- Related files: `architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`, `architecture/30_60_90_PLAN.md`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`, `.kiro/steering/tech.md`, `infra/`
  - Note (2026-08-04): the `architecture/MVP Sprint/WEEK_*_SPRINT.md` documents previously cited here were removed as superseded planning artifacts. `architecture/30_60_90_PLAN.md` and `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md` are the surviving plan-of-record references for the Week 4 go/no-go gate named above.

### ADR-20260715-01: Consultation document finalization, release, and electronic signatures
- Date: 2026-07-15
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: The post-consultation UI created independent blank drafts for the same document type, discarded the drawn signature, and labeled sequential document finalization as an atomic “release” even though no release resource or transaction existed. The API exposed `finalized` documents to patients but did not define whether finalization itself constituted release or what evidence an electronic signature must retain.
- Decision:
  - **Finalized means released:** transitioning a consultation document from `draft` to `finalized` is the patient-release event. No second release state or aggregate release endpoint is introduced for the MVP.
  - **Independent writes:** each document is finalized independently. Multi-document UI convenience actions are explicitly non-atomic and must report the result of every document; they must not claim all-or-nothing release.
  - **Signature required:** doctor-finalized clinician documents (`soap_note`, `prescription`, `medical_certificate`, `visit_summary`, and `lab_request`) require an electronic signature in the same finalization request. System-finalized `recommendation` and imported `lab_result` documents are exempt.
  - **Signature evidence:** the client sends normalized vector strokes plus signer name and explicit review acknowledgment. The backend validates size/shape, binds the authenticated assigned doctor, stamps UTC `signedAt`, and stores a SHA-256 digest of the canonical drawing. PNG/base64 data URLs are prohibited because they can exceed the 24 KiB document-content limit and duplicate binary data in DynamoDB.
  - **Canonical drafts:** the frontend lists consultation documents once and coordinates all authoring/signature surfaces around the newest finalized document, otherwise the newest draft, for each document type. It must hydrate existing content before allowing creates and must not create blank duplicate drafts on remount.
  - **Read lifecycle:** assigned doctors and owning patients may read permitted documents after a session is ended or completed. Create/update/finalize writes remain limited to active or in-progress sessions.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Separate `released` state and bulk release transaction: deferred; prescription verification adds a second transactional entity and the MVP does not require all document types to release together.
  - Store canvas PNG data URLs in document content: rejected due to size, duplication, and unsafe client-controlled binary payloads.
  - Store only typed signer name: rejected because it does not preserve the captured drawn mark.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: signatures are durable and attributable; patient visibility has one unambiguous transition; authored content and finalized IDs stay connected; completed consultations remain readable.
  - Trade-offs: “finalize remaining” may partially succeed and requires per-document retry/reporting; normalized vector signatures are evidence records rather than raster images and must be rendered when exported.
  - Follow-up actions: render vector signatures in future PDF exports; consider an atomic release bundle only if pilot workflows require all document types to publish together.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/consultation-documents.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/consultation-documents.ts`, `backend/src/lib/prescription-verification.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/`

### ADR-20260723-01: Assessment-first CDS authorization, publication safety, and cutover
- Date: 2026-07-23
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Clinical safety reviewer: Medical Director / Clinical Lead (named individual pending)
- Context: ADR-20260703-01 establishes the assessment-first, post-consult gate but does not define the durable authorization, concurrency, async continuation, publication, retention, or migration model needed to enforce it safely. The approved assessment-first specification closes those gaps. It also intentionally separates clinical finalization from patient release for assessment-gated protected artifacts, which conflicts with ADR-20260715-01's general MVP rule that finalization itself is release. Current code and `contracts/openapi.yaml` remain legacy behavior and are not implementation evidence for this decision.
- Decision:
  - **Scope and precedence:** This decision extends ADR-20260703-01. For the seven assessment-gated protected output types—Plan, prescription, final ICD, medical certificate, lab request, imaging request, and patient education—it narrowly amends ADR-20260715-01: clinical finalization and patient release are separate idempotent, revision-fenced transitions. ADR-20260715-01's “finalized means released” rule remains in force for consultation documents outside this protected set until another accepted ADR changes it.
  - **Release safety:** Finalization records the assigned Physician's completed clinical review and required signature evidence but does not create patient visibility. Release separately revalidates canonical ended Session state, current assignment and confirmation ownership, effective artifact freshness, policy activation, gate and artifact revisions, and release authorization. Each transition atomically commits domain state, scoped idempotency response, and durable audit intent. A stale or legacy artifact lacking complete gate provenance is historical-only and cannot be finalized or released.
  - **Canonical authority:** One consultation-scoped `SESSION#CURRENT` projection in `app_core`, read by strongly consistent point read or transaction, is authoritative for Session status and assignment revision. Its closed lifecycle is `active | ended`; only `ended` establishes post-consult state. The canonical writer updates the primary Session, projection, assignment/session revisions, and applicable safety fences in one bounded transaction. GSI results, permissive Queries, booking status, duplicated legacy records, and `completed` aliases cannot authorize protected actions.
  - **Clinical-input fencing:** One `CLINICAL_INPUT_HEAD` owns source membership and revisions, canonicalization version, normalized digest, `Clinical_Input_Revision`, monotonic `Clinical_Input_Source_Fence`, and readiness. Every authoritative source mutation atomically advances the source fence and Gate revision, marks the head dirty, sets the owned lock, revokes the current token-family epoch, and writes idempotency/audit intent. External mutable sources must first be trusted-ingested as immutable versioned `app_core` projections. Publication condition-checks the head, closing read-before/read-after races.
  - **Assessment and token-family recovery:** Assessment confirmation, update, re-attestation, and clear are optimistic-concurrency transitions bound to exact editable, Assessment, assignment, gate, and input revisions. Every Assessment version begins with immutable `Token_Family_Epoch` 1. Revocation permanently closes only that epoch; no revoked record can be reopened. Governed recovery creates the next epoch only through an eligible routine token-issuance transition, exact clinical-episode acknowledgment, or a new Assessment-version transition. Reassignment revokes the current epoch and requires the newly assigned Physician to update, re-attest, or clear the Assessment.
  - **Gate tokens and exact replay:** Gate tokens are short-lived ES256 credentials signed by a per-environment Terraform-managed AWS KMS `ECC_NIST_P256` key. Tokens bind Consultation, Physician, Assessment version, token-family epoch, assignment revision, clinical-input revision/source fence, Gate revision, policy activation and component versions, jti, issuer, audience, and time bounds. A valid signature is necessary but never sufficient; every protected action reconstructs and revalidates authoritative state. Only the scoped token-issuance idempotency record may retain the compact token as envelope-encrypted ciphertext under a dedicated replay key and scope-bound encryption context. Application-enforced ciphertext expiry, no later than token expiry plus approved skew, denies replay independently of eventual DynamoDB TTL deletion.
  - **Durable scoped idempotency:** Safety-sensitive CDS writes extend ADR-20260416-12 with an actor/operation/Consultation/key-scoped `Idempotency_Reservation` and the closed states `reserved`, `running`, `provider_invocation_started`, `completed`, `failed_terminal`, and `recovery_required`. A reservation and bounded lease exist before side effects. Provider dispatch is durably marked before invocation. Outcome-unknown provider work enters `recovery_required` and is never automatically repeated. The terminal domain transition, deterministic publication/job reference, response, and audit intent commit atomically. The existing completed-response helper is insufficient for these operations.
  - **Async continuation:** A client Gate token authorizes admission only. Admission atomically creates the job, reservation, and non-exportable server-side `Continuation_Grant` bound to the admitted authorization snapshot and an approved deadline. Original client-token expiry after admission alone does not invalidate the grant, but jti/epoch revocation, Assessment, assignment, input source fence, Gate, lock, episode, policy, lease, or deadline change rejects start, resume, or completion. There is no client continuation endpoint. Only a fenced publication transaction can mark a job completed; cancellation is an idempotent terminal transition that cannot erase a committed publication.
  - **Publication and staleness:** Synchronous and asynchronous publication condition-check canonical Session, assignment/confirmation ownership, Assessment version, clinical-input head and source fence, Gate revision, exact epoch/jti, policy bundle, locks, acknowledgment, routing state, and deterministic artifact identity in the committing transaction. Current views compute effective staleness from authoritative revisions and revocation watermarks; correctness never depends on unbounded item rewrites. Historical views label stale provenance explicitly.
  - **Deterministic safety and immutable policy:** Candidate and protected-generation red-flag evaluations use deterministic immutable rules. Non-routine or failed outcomes fail closed, set reason-owned durable locks, and revoke the current epoch. Environment activation selects one immutable, content-addressed compatible bundle of ruleset, diagnosis catalog, prompt, deterministic template, output schema, grounding, and model-profile versions through a revision-fenced activation record. In-flight work remains bound to its admitted bundle and cannot silently mix versions.
  - **Audit outbox:** Every safety-relevant transaction writes deterministic, minimal, PHI-safe audit intent to a hash-sharded `app_core` outbox. A stream consumer delivers idempotently with bounded retry/backoff and encrypted dead-letter handling. Delivery failure does not roll back valid clinical state, but backlog age/count is reconciled and alarmed. Raw tokens, Assessment text, patient identifiers, normalized input, prompts, generated content, and unbounded exceptions are excluded from operational telemetry.
  - **Retention and legal hold:** Feature records carry an approved retention class, `legalHold`, and optional `ttl`. Applying legal hold conditionally removes `ttl`; releasing a hold restores only the approved retention expiry. DynamoDB TTL is eventual cleanup and is never an authorization, token-expiry, cryptographic-expiry, or legal-hold control. Exact retention periods, grant/job deadlines, replay windows, backup/restore behavior, and deletion ownership must be approved in the feature retention matrix before implementation tasks may reference them. This clarifies ADR-20260416-06 for assessment-first CDS records.
  - **Migration and cutover:** Protected generation defaults disabled. Before enablement, all legacy routes, writers, jobs, draft/document types, approval hooks, frontend flows, and automation paths are classified as `historical_read_only`, `convertible_pre_assessment`, `reject_pending_protected`, or `quarantine_unknown`. Cutover uses a stable high-water checkpoint, resumable conditional migration, source-to-class count/checksum equality, zero pending protected jobs, a bounded dual-read period, and irreversible retirement of legacy protected writers. Unknown or mismatched records block enablement. Rollback disables issuance/generation and may restore compatible readers, but never restores one-shot SOAP Assessment/Plan, client continuation, patient-education bypass, or legacy protected writers.
  - **Infrastructure and source-of-truth constraints:** All long-lived KMS, IAM, streams, dead-letter, alarms, dashboards, and kill-switch resources are Terraform-managed in `ap-southeast-1`. `contracts/openapi.yaml` remains contract-first, and `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts` remains the implementation source of truth only after the approved `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md` source inventory, no-scan access patterns, transaction manifests, action/byte budgets, and retention classes are complete.
  - **Implementation gate:** This accepted decision is binding but does not by itself authorize implementation. Handler, frontend, Terraform, migration, and enablement work remains blocked until the OpenAPI contract, CDS data model/manifests, finite limits/retention matrix, threat model/IAM review, migration inventory/playbook, observability/runbooks, traceability evidence, named clinical owner, and durable approval links satisfy the assessment-first specification's prerequisite gates.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Keep “finalized means released” for protected artifacts: rejected because a combined transition cannot preserve a separately reviewable clinical-signoff state or independently revalidate patient-release authorization after gate, assignment, input, or policy changes.
  - Reopen a revoked token family after recovery: rejected because mutation of revocation history weakens auditability and creates ambiguous replay behavior; immutable epochs with governed next-epoch creation preserve both safety and liveness.
  - Use a client token for async continuation: rejected because client-token lifetime and possession cannot safely authorize later worker execution; a bounded server-side grant with full revalidation is required.
  - Keep completed-response-only idempotency: rejected because it cannot serialize side effects or distinguish safe retry from an outcome-unknown provider invocation.
  - Authorize from GSI/session Queries or direct external-source rereads: rejected because eventual consistency and read/commit races cannot establish a publication-safe clinical snapshot.
  - Perform synchronous stale updates across all artifacts: rejected because artifact history is unbounded while DynamoDB transactions are bounded; authoritative revision predicates provide immediate invalidation.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: closes the known token-recovery, external-source publication-race, immediate-staleness, reassignment, async replay, and legacy-bypass hazards while preserving Together.ai and deterministic fallback behind the same server gate.
  - Trade-offs: adds state, transactional manifests, KMS signing/replay encryption, reconciliation, migration, and operational burden; separately released protected artifacts require frontend and contract changes and a migration from current immediate patient visibility.
  - Follow-up actions: acceptance recorded on 2026-07-23; assign the named Medical Director / Clinical Lead; update `contracts/openapi.yaml` contract-first; approve `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`; approve finite limits and retention; complete threat/IAM, migration, observability, runbook, and traceability artifacts; then create implementation tasks. The assessment-first design prerequisite table now cites this ADR as Requirement 15.2 evidence.
- Amends: ADR-20260703-01; ADR-20260715-01 only for assessment-gated protected artifacts; ADR-20260416-12 for safety-sensitive CDS idempotency; ADR-20260416-06 for optional TTL under legal hold and application-enforced expiry.
- Related files: `.kiro/specs/assessment-first-cds-gating/requirements.md`, `.kiro/specs/assessment-first-cds-gating/design.md`, `architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`, `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `backend/src/lib/idempotency.ts`, `backend/src/lib/otl.ts`, `backend/src/lib/consultation-documents.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/`, `backend/src/handlers/cds.ts`, `infra/`

### ADR-20260723-02: Assessment-first CDS MVP governance readiness amendment
- Date: 2026-07-23
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Dev A — Lead Engineer
- Amends: ADR-20260723-01 governance and implementation-gate clauses only; all authorization, publication-safety, deterministic routing, migration-safety, and fail-closed requirements remain binding.
- Context: ADR-20260723-01 correctly established the assessment-first CDS safety architecture but also required separate contract, data, security, operations, and named-clinical-owner approvals plus durable approval links before task creation and implementation. For this MVP, Dev A has sole implementation and governance authority. Clinical QA and product direction remain required before clinical canary or production enablement, but they are not development prerequisites.
- Decision:
  - Dev A may accept the assessment-first contract, documented data model and defaults, architecture, security/operations baseline, migration plan, and implementation readiness. Separate contract, data, security, operations, frontend, design, or executive approval gates are not required for `tasks.md`, implementation, dev deployment, or technical validation.
  - A complete executable `tasks.md` and implementation may proceed after the OpenAPI operation/schema/error inventory is complete and passes repository contract validation, and after the data model, transaction boundaries, ownership, finite defaults/maxima, retention behavior, deadlines, and cancellation mappings needed by the implementation are documented and locked by Dev A.
  - Pull-request links, commit links, a personally named clinical owner, and other durable approval-link artifacts are not prerequisites to task creation or development. Normal source control and validation practices still apply.
  - Dev A may accept the migration plan for implementation readiness. This acceptance does not assert that migration was executed, rehearsed, reconciled, or approved for cutover; those facts require actual environment evidence.
  - Before clinical canary or production enablement, the CEO acting as Medical Director / Clinical Lead must complete clinical QA and exercise product-direction authority over clinical behavior. No personal name is required in governance text.
  - Technical gates are unchanged: the OpenAPI contract must remain complete and pass validation; data/defaults must remain documented; deterministic red-flag, server-side gate, stale-state, denied-path, least-privilege, reconciliation, rollback, and safety-test requirements may not be weakened.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Removes organizational and evidence-link blockers from development while retaining a clear technical readiness boundary and independent clinical enablement gate.
  - Trade-offs: Dev A carries sole accountability for MVP implementation/governance acceptance and must distinguish readiness decisions from later runtime, migration, clinical, and production evidence.
  - Follow-up actions: Apply this boundary to Requirement 15, the design readiness section, the review matrix, and the migration/cutover playbook; do not rewrite ADR-20260723-01 as though its original text never existed.
- Related files: `.kiro/specs/assessment-first-cds-gating/requirements.md`, `.kiro/specs/assessment-first-cds-gating/design.md`, `architecture/REVIEW_AND_APPROVAL_MATRIX.md`, `architecture/CDS_MIGRATION_CUTOVER.md`

### ADR-20260723-03: Conservative non-production CDS migration rehearsal configuration
- Date: 2026-07-23
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Dev A — Lead Engineer
- Context: The assessment-first migration tooling intentionally left execution-sensitive in-flight, export-retention, parallelism, lease, segment, and dual-read values unset. Task 10.5 cannot execute even a non-production rehearsal until Dev A records finite values. These values govern dev/staging rehearsal only and are not production cutover approval or environment authorization.
- Decision:
  - `inFlightWaitSeconds = 300`.
  - `exportRetentionDays = 30`.
  - `parallelism = 2`.
  - `leaseSeconds = 60`.
  - `segmentCount = 4`.
  - `dualReadDays = 7`.
  - The accepted configuration is represented by `APPROVED_NON_PRODUCTION_MIGRATION_CONFIG` in `backend/src/lib/cds/migration-tooling.ts`; missing, non-integer, or non-positive values fail closed before rehearsal.
  - Immutable DynamoDB point-in-time exports and S3 object-version manifests remain the only permitted high water. Live `Scan` and maximum-key capture remain prohibited.
  - This decision authorizes deterministic local/synthetic rehearsal and defines values for an authorized dev/staging rehearsal. It does not authorize AWS mutation, claim deployed route/IAM state, or approve production values.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Task 10.5 has finite, reproducible, conservative execution bounds and a seven-day non-production dual-read interval.
  - Trade-offs: A full environment rehearsal still requires an identified non-production target, verified access, explicit change authorization, immutable export capability, and independently captured evidence.
  - Follow-up actions: Run local/synthetic rehearsal first; execute dev or staging export/migration actions only through an authorized window; record environment-specific counts/checksums and rollback evidence without PHI. Select production values through a separate Dev A decision.
- Related files: `architecture/CDS_MIGRATION_CUTOVER.md`, `backend/src/lib/cds/migration-tooling.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/migration-rehearsal.ts`, `.kiro/specs/assessment-first-cds-gating/tasks.md`

### ADR-20260720-01: Team transition and product-design operating model
- Date: 2026-07-20
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Dev A — Lead Engineer
- Context: The MVP is moving from feature assembly to production hardening and pilot readiness. The previous split between Dev B (new frontend features) and Dev C (existing-surface work) no longer reflects staffing. The product also needs a clear owner for its Figma-based brand refresh and implementation handoff.
- Decision:
  - Dev B is offboarded and has no active delivery, review, or approval responsibility.
  - Dev C is the sole frontend developer, responsible for new features, existing-surface hardening, live API integration, end-to-end quality, and implementation of approved Figma designs.
  - Dev D owns the revised BayanHealth Figma brand system, component and workflow specifications, implementation annotations, accessibility-aware UX, and design QA. Dev D is non-code by default.
  - Dev A remains lead engineer for backend, cloud, AI, DevOps, contracts, security, and operational work; Dev A is the final merge/deploy gatekeeper.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: One clear frontend delivery owner and one explicit design-to-code handoff path.
  - Trade-offs: Frontend sequencing must respect Dev C capacity; Figma acceptance must be explicit before visual rework begins.
  - Follow-up actions: Update active governance/docs; use approved Figma tokens and handoff annotations for frontend work; keep the review matrix current.

### ADR-20260720-02: Google Meet as the MVP video-consultation provider
- Date: 2026-07-20
- Status: Accepted architecture; implementation gated
- Owner: Dev A — Lead Engineer
- Context: BayanHealth needs a video-consultation path without taking on native WebRTC, SFU, TURN, or browser credential complexity. The chosen provider must not weaken BayanHealth authorization, clinical lifecycle, or PHI controls.
- Decision:
  - Google Meet replaces the deferred native WebRTC direction for the MVP video transport.
  - BayanHealth remains the system of record and authorization boundary for booking assignment, consent, session eligibility, clinical completion, document release, chat fallback, and audit.
  - The backend alone may call Google Meet APIs. Browser code must never receive Google OAuth client secrets, refresh tokens, or direct provider-control capability.
  - No recordings, transcripts, calendar synchronization, guest management, or Meet add-ons are in MVP scope.
  - Before any route or infrastructure implementation, Dev A must decide and validate the organizer identity model (approved platform-owned Workspace identity or per-doctor OAuth), provider capabilities, Google Workspace administration, privacy/data-processing terms, retention, and patient/doctor notices.
  - Contract-first implementation is mandatory after those gates: OpenAPI routes precede Lambda, Terraform, and frontend work.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Reduces media-infrastructure scope while retaining a provider-neutral clinical lifecycle and chat fallback.
  - Trade-offs: Adds Google OAuth/Workspace and privacy dependency; a Meet provider event does not prove clinical completion.
  - Follow-up actions: Complete the gates in `GOOGLE_MEET_INTEGRATION.md`, publish the contract, model least-privilege Terraform resources, and add E2E failure/fallback coverage.

### ADR-20260728-01: Evidence-driven rapid delivery for assessment-first CDS

- Date: 2026-07-28
- Status: Accepted; amended 2026-07-31
- Owner: Product and Engineering
- Context: The assessment-first CDS implementation plan mixed bounded repository work with full-repository security remediation, environment campaigns, named approvals, clinical review, canary observation, and production rollout. Repeated attempts to finish Task 10 therefore expanded into unrelated subsystems or stopped while waiting for Dev A or CEO approval. This conflicts with the rapid-delivery intent of the QA feedback v1 / PRD v3.2 baseline, whose core human gate is physician confirmation inside the clinical workflow.
- Decision:
  - Implementation, local validation, documentation, and non-destructive evidence generation proceed without waiting for Dev A, CEO, Clinical Lead, or other named approval.
  - A spec task must be bounded to repository-controlled work with a deterministic command, artifact, or reviewable document. Missing prior named-person approval is not a valid reason to withhold such work.
  - Every mutation classified by the governing specification requires its own current, unused, operation-specific point-of-action authorization. Authorization is bound to the exact operation, target, environment, and window; it cannot be reused or generalized. Missing, expired, used, or mismatched authorization requires zero mutation and a recorded `DEFER`.
  - A governing specification may narrowly pre-authorize emergency disablement, credential/token revocation, or containment necessary to stop active harm. The actor must use the smallest action, preserve evidence, record actor/time/scope/result, and reconcile runtime, Terraform, and governance state afterward. Emergency authority never permits enablement, promotion, migration, or unrelated remediation.
  - Technical failures block only the task and scope they directly affect. Full-repository scan findings unrelated to assessment-first CDS are recorded and routed to separate remediation work; they do not expand the CDS task automatically.
  - Repository security tests are distinct from approved penetration testing. Environment load/fault campaigns, migration execution, staging qualification or promotion, clinical review, canary authorization/observation, and production/GA enablement are operational follow-ups and do not keep implementation tasks open.
  - Clinical review remains important input for clinical content and production use, but it is role-based evidence rather than a named-person approval prerequisite. Protected-environment mutations remain subject to the operation-specific authorization rule above.
  - Task status uses one evidence model: `complete` for its bounded deliverable, `blocked` only for an in-scope technical failure, and `deferred operational` for work requiring a target environment, observation window, or external actor. Deferred operational work is never represented as an unfinished coding task.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: autonomous progress, less repeated approval prompting, bounded scans and tests, clearer separation of implementation from rollout, and exact mutation authority.
  - Trade-offs: launch readiness must be read from the operational follow-up register rather than inferred from implementation completion; operators must track authorization use and emergency reconciliation evidence.
  - Follow-up actions: amend the assessment-first CDS requirements, design, tasks, traceability, readiness register, review matrix, handoff, and steering guidance.
- Supersedes: approval-gating and task-withholding clauses of ADR-20260630-01 and ADR-20260723-02 for assessment-first CDS implementation; does not remove protected-branch controls or operation-specific authorization for governed mutations.
- Related files: `.kiro/specs/assessment-first-cds-gating/`, `.kiro/steering/`, `architecture/CDS_TASK_10_READINESS.md`, `architecture/REVIEW_AND_APPROVAL_MATRIX.md`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`

### ADR-20260726-01: Task 10.3 least-privilege application boundary hardening
- Date: 2026-07-26
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Dev A
- Context: Task 10.3 triage of the retained repository security findings identified authorization, browser-state, audit, CORS, and IAM boundaries that permitted client-controlled privilege or unnecessarily enlarged the effect of a compromised client or Lambda.
- Decision:
  - Public Cognito confirmation always assigns only the `patient` group; privileged roles require audited administrative/KYC promotion.
  - Manual booking assignment is admin-only and requires an explicit server-validated doctor identifier; automated matching remains a separate canonical server-side writer.
  - Booking price and currency are server-owned. WebSocket connection tokens are consumed atomically, and PayRex webhook/fallback transitions are ownership- and state-fenced.
  - Break-glass access is consultation-scoped and fail-closed on durable audit failure. Privileged authorization intent is recorded before mutation and completion is audited before replay success is stored.
  - Auth tokens and intake PHI remain memory-only in the browser, with restrictive browser security headers. API CORS uses exact environment-specific origins.
  - Cognito administrative permissions are isolated to the admin Lambda execution role rather than inherited by unrelated handlers.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Retain doctor self-assignment with additional booking-status checks: rejected because status does not prove matching authorization or patient consent.
  - Persist encrypted browser sessions or intake drafts in local storage: rejected because same-origin script compromise would still expose decrypted state and shared-device residue.
