  - Keep the shared Lambda role and rely only on handler authorization: rejected because application compromise would retain unnecessary Cognito control-plane privilege.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Client metadata, request bodies, browser persistence, and unrelated Lambda compromise can no longer directly cross the affected privilege and PHI boundaries.
  - Trade-offs: Browser refresh clears in-progress auth/intake state; administrative assignment and role-promotion workflows must be used explicitly; deployments must provide exact staging and production origins.
  - Follow-up actions: Maintain Task 10.3 regression coverage, regenerate OpenAPI types after contract changes, and require a fresh full-repository security scan before closing retained findings.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/handlers/bookings.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/post-confirmation.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/admin.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/chat-ws.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/payrex-webhooks.ts`, `backend/src/lib/break-glass.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/stores/useAuthStore.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/hooks/useIntakeForm.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/next.config.ts`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `architecture/CDS_SECURITY_VALIDATION_TASK_10_3.md`

### ADR-20260804-01: Patient-education knowledge base — keyed retrieval, no vector store (V1)

- Date: 2026-08-04
- Status: Accepted; register naming clarified by ADR-20260824-01 — the bilingual requirement is satisfied by exactly two registers, English and Taglish, where "Taglish" is the accurate display name for the content stored in the `*_fil` columns and tagged `filipino`; no third register added
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A); clinical content owned by the CEO (general physician)
- Context: V1 needs a patient post-education knowledge base. The corpus will be authored by the CEO and delivered as a CSV of common conditions with ICD-10 codes and patient-facing education content. The obvious reflex is "RAG", which implies embeddings, a vector store, and similarity search. That reflex is wrong for this system for two reasons.

  First, CDS is assessment-first (ADR-20260703-01): patient education is a Protected_Output that unlocks only **after** the physician confirms an Assessment. At retrieval time the confirmed diagnosis is already known, so retrieval is a lookup by key, not a search for a probable match. Semantic search would be solving a problem the gating model has already eliminated.

  Second, `soap-multi-transaction-cds` requires grounding assembly to be deterministic — an identical Grounding_Bundle digest and identical ordered passage set for identical inputs. A keyed lookup satisfies that by construction. An approximate-nearest-neighbour index does not, and would put the retrieval layer in permanent tension with the spec it must satisfy.

  `approved_cpg_passage` is already one of the three closed source classes accepted by the spec's retrieval service, so this corpus slots into the existing design rather than extending it.
- Decision:
  - **Storage**: the corpus lives in `app_core` as immutable, content-addressed items, modelled on the drug reference layer (ADR-20260630-02) — new entity types registered in `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, sparse reuse of `gsi1`/`gsi2`/`gsi3`, no new table and no new GSI.
  - **Retrieval**: keyed lookup by confirmed ICD-10 code, with condition name as a secondary key. `GetItem`/`Query` only; **no `Scan` path**.
  - **No embeddings, no vector store, no similarity search in V1.** No Amazon Bedrock (ADR-20260525-01 stands). Together.ai offers embedding models; they are deliberately unused here.
  - **Bilingual is a first-class requirement**, not a later enhancement. Every topic carries paired English and Filipino content. A topic reaches `approved` only when **both** languages are present and reviewed; a single-language topic is never eligible for grounding. Retrofitting a second language later would require re-approving every row, so the schema requires it from the first ingest.
  - **Review status is load-bearing**: `draft | approved | retired`. The spec's grounding assembly excludes unapproved review statuses, so this field gates clinical visibility. The CEO is the clinical approver of record; reviewer identity and review date are stored per topic.
  - **Citation metadata is mandatory per passage.** The spec drops uncited elements silently, so a passage without a source cannot be bound and would degrade output invisibly.
  - **Corpus revisions are explicit**: an `educationCorpusRevision` counter in the style of `policyActivationRevision`, so any historical generation is attributable to the exact corpus that produced it. Approved content is immutable; corrections create a new revision rather than mutating a row.
  - **Ingestion is an admin-only offline script** following the `seed-cds-demo-policy.ts` precedent — CSV in, validated and content-digested, immutable put. There is **no runtime upload endpoint** and no PDF parsing path. A PDF corpus is converted to CSV once, by hand, before ingest.
  - **PHI exclusion**: the corpus is general disease education and must contain zero PHI. Ingest runs the existing `deidentify.ts` gate over every field and refuses the row on any violation.
  - **Retention**: reference data with an explicit no-TTL declaration, consistent with how the drug layer is described in `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Vector store plus embeddings (OpenSearch Serverless, pgvector, or a managed vector DB): rejected for V1. It cannot guarantee the deterministic-digest property the SOAP spec requires, adds a new long-lived AWS resource and its cost against the ADR-20260710-01 Low-tier envelope, and solves a retrieval problem that assessment-first gating has already removed.
  - Free-text semantic retrieval over symptoms rather than confirmed diagnosis: rejected as a V1 scope error. This is the legitimate future use for embeddings, and the keyed design does not block adding it later behind the same `RetrievalService` seam.
  - Storing the corpus as flat files in S3 and reading at generation time: rejected — no review-status query path, no atomic revision semantics, and it would sit outside the single-table access-pattern discipline.
  - Runtime admin upload endpoint: rejected for V1 — it widens the authenticated write surface for a corpus that changes rarely and requires clinical approval per row.
  - English-only V1 with Filipino added later: rejected. Intake already preserves Taglish patient wording, so English-only education is a visible product gap, and adding a language later invalidates every prior clinical approval.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: deterministic and reproducible grounding; no new AWS resource and no incremental infrastructure cost; reuses an established access pattern and ingestion idiom; clinical approval and bilingual completeness are enforced structurally rather than by convention.
  - Trade-offs: no fuzzy matching, so a confirmed diagnosis absent from the corpus yields no education content rather than an approximate neighbour — the correct failure for clinical content, but it makes corpus coverage a product dependency. The bilingual gate slows initial corpus approval because both languages must land together.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) register the entity types, key prefixes, and retention class in `dynamo.ts` and record them in `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`; (b) build the CSV ingestion script with digesting and the PHI gate; (c) wire the corpus as an `approved_cpg_passage` source class in the retrieval service; (d) revisit embeddings only when retrieval by free-text symptom becomes a requirement.
- Related files: `architecture/AI_INFERENCE.md`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/solver-content.ts`, `backend/src/lib/drug-data.ts`, `backend/scripts/seed-cds-demo-policy.ts`, `.kiro/specs/soap-multi-transaction-cds/`

### ADR-20260804-02: Dev audit DLQ purge and environment-aware alarm missing-data posture

- Date: 2026-08-04
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Five CDS alarms in `dev` had been in `ALARM` continuously, every one reporting *"no datapoints were received for 1 period and 1 missing datapoint was treated as [Breaching]"*. That was read as known noise. It was not: `bayanhealth-dev-cds-audit-dlq` genuinely held 37 messages.

  Investigation found the actual defect. `AuditOutboxService.markDelivered` referenced the DynamoDB reserved keyword `ttl` bare in an `UpdateExpression`, so **every invocation** of `bayanhealth-dev-cds-audit-outbox` failed with `ValidationException` from 2026-07-31 onward — roughly 4,320 errors per day against 4,320 invocations. The unit suite reported 703/703 passing throughout because the Document Client is mocked and never validates the expression. The 37 DLQ items were `DDBStreamBatchInfo` records with `condition: RetryAttemptsExhausted`, containing stream batch coordinates rather than audit payloads, so they carried no PHI and were not themselves replayable once the 24-hour stream retention elapsed.

  Two decisions follow: what to do with the 37 items, and how alarms should treat missing data in an environment that is idle by design.
- Decision:
  - **The 37 dev audit DLQ messages were purged** on 2026-08-04 by Dev A via `sqs purge-queue` against `arn:aws:sqs:ap-southeast-1:472206752892:bayanhealth-dev-cds-audit-dlq`, depth 37 before and 0 after. Authorized as a point-of-action decision on the explicit basis that `dev` is **strictly synthetic** — fake patient and doctor data used for e2e flow, demo, and test runs — so no real clinical audit record was discarded. The messages held batch coordinates, not audit content, and the referenced stream positions had already expired, so redrive was not available. This authorization is specific to this queue, this environment, and this occurrence; it does not generalize to `staging` or `prod`, where an audit DLQ item must be reconciled rather than discarded.
  - **The underlying `ttl` defect is fixed in code** across seven sites in `audit-outbox.ts` and `async-jobs.ts`, with a source-level regression guard (`reserved-keyword-expressions.test.ts`) because mocked unit tests structurally cannot observe this class of failure. Purging without fixing would simply refill the queue.
  - **Alarm missing-data posture is now environment-aware.** `local.idle_tolerant_missing_data` in `infra/modules/cds_operations/monitoring.tf` resolves to `notBreaching` in `dev` and `breaching` in `staging` and `prod`, and is applied to the four audit-outbox backlog alarms and the audit DLQ depth alarm — the five whose metrics only publish under activity. **This bullet is superseded for `dev` by ADR-20260806-05**: the Terraform change was never applied, and on reconsideration it will not be. Live `dev` keeps `treat_missing_data = "breaching"` and the five standing `ALARM`s are accepted noise. `staging` and `prod` are unaffected.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Redrive the 37 messages instead of purging: rejected as unavailable. They are stream batch pointers and the underlying stream records had aged out of the 24-hour retention window.
  - Purge without fixing the Lambda: rejected. The outbox was failing 100% of invocations, so the queue would refill and the alarm signal would remain untrustworthy.
  - Keep `breaching` everywhere: rejected for `dev`. An alarm that fires permanently cannot be used to exercise alarming — which is the stated purpose of monitoring in this environment — and it actively erodes trust in the signal. This incident is the evidence: a real 37-message backlog was dismissed because it looked identical to the standing false alarm.
  - Switch to `notBreaching` in every environment: rejected. In a protected environment, silence on audit backlog depth is itself a suspicious condition and must fail safe.
  - Raise the DLQ alarm threshold above zero: rejected. Any audit DLQ item is significant; the threshold is not the problem, the missing-data treatment was.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: an `ALARM` in `dev` now carries information. The audit outbox can mark intents delivered again once the fix is deployed. The regression guard closes a defect class that mocked tests cannot reach.
  - Trade-offs: `dev` no longer alarms on a genuinely stalled metric pipeline, so a silent-failure mode there is detectable only by inspection. Accepted, because `dev` is not an environment whose availability is being asserted.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) **deploy the `ttl` fix to `dev`** — the code fix is inert until the Lambda is updated, and the demo on 2026-08-07 exercises this path; (b) apply the Terraform monitoring change to `dev` under its own authorization; (c) confirm the audit outbox error rate returns to zero and the DLQ stays empty after deployment; (d) treat any `staging` or `prod` audit DLQ item as a reconciliation obligation, never a purge candidate.
- Related files: `backend/src/lib/cds/audit-outbox.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/async-jobs.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/reserved-keyword-expressions.test.ts`, `infra/modules/cds_operations/monitoring.tf`, `architecture/CDS_SECURITY_OPERATIONS.md`, `architecture/CDS_TASK_10_READINESS.md`

### ADR-20260804-03: Dev seeded-account password floor lowered to 12 with explicit complexity

- Date: 2026-08-04
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A) — decision taken by Dev A after the trade-off was presented
- Context: `TF_VAR_seed_account_password` sets the permanent password on the seeded dev demo, role, and smoke Cognito accounts, and the dev smoke test authenticates against those same accounts. The value must therefore be identical across `DEV_SEED_ACCOUNT_PASSWORD`, `SMOKE_USER_PASSWORD`, and `SMOKE_DOCTOR_PASSWORD`.

  The Terraform validation required at least 16 characters, enforced in both `infra/environments/dev/variables.tf` and `infra/modules/cognito/variables.tf`. This is why the working value had previously been changed to `TempPass123!2026` — exactly 16 characters — and why `TempPass123!` had stopped working. On 2026-08-04 the dev secret was set back to `TempPass123!` (12 characters), which blocked `terraform apply` in the dev deploy workflow with `Invalid value for variable`, in turn blocking deployment of the audit-outbox `ttl` fix and the CSP nonce fix three days before the 2026-08-07 demo.
- Decision:
  - The length floor for the **dev seeded-account password only** is lowered from 16 to 12 characters.
  - Explicit character-class requirements are added in compensation: at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, one digit, and one symbol. `TempPass123!` satisfies all four.
  - The properties that actually carry the security weight are **unchanged**: no source default, `nullable = false` at the dev root, no committed literal anywhere in Terraform or workflow files, and the value must arrive through the approved secret channel. The two Task 10.3 regression tests that assert `TempPass123!` appears in neither the module source nor the dev source remain green, because this change adds no literal.
  - Scope is dev only. `staging` and `prod` do not consume this variable, and the module validation remains conditioned on `var.environment == "dev"`.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Use `TempPass123!2026` (16 characters): recommended by the implementer and rejected by the owner. It required no code change and was the value the smoke script already documents as its default.
  - Use a different ≥16 value retaining the memorable stem, such as `TempPass123!Dev1`: rejected by the owner for the same reason.
  - Leave the floor at 16 and block the demo deploy: rejected — the deploy carries two live defect fixes and the demo date is fixed.
  - Lower the floor with no compensating control: rejected by the implementer. A bare reduction would leave a weaker rule with nothing recorded in its place; the character-class requirement keeps the validation meaningful and self-documenting.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the dev deploy is unblocked, the seeded-account and smoke passwords agree, and the value is one the team can recall during a live demo. The complexity requirement is now explicit in code rather than implied by length.
  - Trade-offs: a 12-character dev credential is weaker than a 16-character one. Accepted on the basis that these accounts are synthetic, exist only in dev, hold no real patient data, and are reachable only through the dev Cognito pool. Cognito's own pool policy independently enforces a 10-character minimum with uppercase, lowercase, and numeric requirements. **This reduction must not be propagated to `staging` or `prod`**, where seeded accounts do not exist and any real credential remains subject to the unmodified controls.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) rotate the dev seeded-account password if the value is ever shared outside the team or shown on screen during the demo; (b) revisit the floor when a shared GitHub organization and secret-management process exist, since the convenience argument for a memorable value disappears once retrieval is automated.
- Related files: `infra/environments/dev/variables.tf`, `infra/modules/cognito/variables.tf`, `infra/modules/cognito/seed.tf`, `.github/workflows/deploy-dev.yml`, `backend/scripts/smoke-bookings-dev.sh`

### ADR-20260805-01: Patient-authorized intake endpoints so intake happens during booking

- Date: 2026-08-05
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Intake writes were reachable only through a doctor-issued one-time link (`/v1/intake/{token}`). The booking wizard, however, places intake before payment, so a patient creating a booking had no authorized way to fill the form that the wizard was asking them to fill. The wizard's intake read returned `404` and the step could not be completed. This ADR was referenced throughout the implementation but was never recorded in this log; it is written here retroactively so the log matches the code.
- Decision:
  - Two patient-owned routes were added contract-first: `PUT /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/intake` (save one step) and `POST /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/intake/submissions` (finalise).
  - Both are restricted to the booking's owning patient, consume no one-time link, and are allowed only while the booking is `pending_payment`, `payment_submitted`, `confirmed`, or `in_progress`.
  - Both write the identical `intake_form` item the OTL path writes, so doctor read, review, and acknowledgement are unchanged and there is one intake representation rather than two.
  - Ownership mismatch returns `404`, consistent with the booking endpoints, so booking existence is not disclosed.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Have the patient consume an OTL during their own booking: rejected. It would mean minting a single-use, 15-minute credential for the person who already holds a session, and the link's purpose is delegation to an unauthenticated recipient.
  - Accept intake only after the doctor issues a link: rejected. It contradicts the booking flow the product defines, where intake precedes payment.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the booking wizard's intake step works for an authenticated patient, and the doctor-facing surface is untouched.
  - Trade-offs: intake is now writable from two entry points, so the status and booking-state guards must stay in agreement between them. The shared item shape and the shared editable-status set are what keep that manageable.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/intake.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/intake.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/intake-patient.test.ts`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/api/patientIntake.ts`

### ADR-20260805-02: Patient-authorized doctor directory; no fabricated clinician attributes

- Date: 2026-08-05
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Three defects in the patient booking surface shared one root cause — the frontend was reading doctor identity from places a patient cannot reach, and filling the gap with invented values.

  1. **Doctor search and the doctor detail page called `GET /v1/admin/users`**, which requires the `admin` role. Every patient request returned `403`, so patient-side doctor search could not work at all.
  2. **`GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}` returns only `doctorId`**, no name. The booking wizard's assigned-doctor card therefore rendered hard-coded defaults: `"Dr. Placeholder"`, `"Medical Center"`, `"~ 30 km away"`, a 4-of-5 star rating, and `"English · Filipino"`. The platform holds none of those attributes for any doctor.
  3. **The Finding step was unreachable.** The wizard mapped only `payment_submitted` to it, but that status is written solely by the legacy payment-proof path. The wired payment path (`POST /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/payment/hold`) transitions `pending_payment` straight to `confirmed`, so the wizard jumped Payment to Confirm. The step's own content was simulated regardless: a 3-second `setInterval` walked a scripted "Doctor has been notified / is reading your request / accepted" sequence with no reference to backend state.
- Decision:
  - Add two contract-first routes on the existing doctor-KYC Lambda, readable by **any authenticated role**: `GET /v1/doctors` (approved directory, paginated) and `GET /v1/doctors/{doctorId}`.
  - Both project through a single `DoctorPublicSummary` shape — `doctorId`, `fullName`, `specialty?`, `onDemandAvailable?`. `email`, `licenseNumber`, `phoneNumber`, `verificationStatus`, and every review field are excluded. Widening the audience must not widen the payload, so a property test asserts the projection never carries a sensitive key.
  - Only consultation-approved doctors are addressable. Listing reuses the `KYC#approved` gsi3 partition that already backs the moderator queue, so approval is the single gate rather than a post-hoc filter. A doctor who does not exist and one who is not approved both return `404`, so neither existence nor approval state leaks.
  - The frontend resolves the assigned doctor's summary once in `PatientBookingDetail` and passes it down, so both the Finding and Confirmation steps show the same real name.
  - `doctorRating`, `doctorHospital`, and `doctorDistance` are removed from the `Booking` type rather than defaulted. The simulated matching interval is removed; the Finding step renders `useFinding`'s real polled state.
  - Wizard step mapping now reads matching state from `confirmed` + `doctorId` rather than from `payment_submitted`.
  - The Confirmation step no longer says the doctor is "reviewing your payment"; on the wired path the funds are already held and the booking is already confirmed.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Grant patients read access to `GET /v1/admin/users`: rejected outright. It exposes the full user directory with emails and Cognito status, and it inverts the least-privilege boundary established in ADR-20260726-01.
  - Embed a doctor name in the booking response: rejected. It duplicates profile data into every booking item and goes stale when a doctor updates their profile. A separate, cacheable read keeps one source of truth.
  - Keep the placeholder ratings and hospital and populate them later: rejected. The 2026-08-07 audience is two physicians assessing clinical credibility; a fabricated peer credential is worse than an absent one, and placeholder data has a habit of surviving into production.
  - Insert an artificial delay so the Finding step is always visible: rejected. Matching runs synchronously inside the payment hold (`finalizeBookingAfterPaymentConfirm`), so when a doctor is available there is genuinely nothing to wait for. The step now appears when it means something — no doctor assigned yet — and is skipped when it does not.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: patient doctor search works; the assigned-doctor card shows a real name and specialty; the Finding step is reachable and honest; no invented clinician attribute reaches a screen.
  - Trade-offs: a doctor with no `specialty` on their profile renders "Specialty not listed", and one whose summary has not resolved renders "Resolving doctor…". Both are visibly incomplete, which is the intended behaviour — an unresolved value must look unresolved.
  - Trade-offs: the assigned-doctor card costs one extra request per booking detail view. Mitigated by a 10-minute `staleTime`, since an approved doctor's name and specialty rarely change.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) deploy the doctor-kyc Lambda and apply the two new API Gateway routes to `dev` under their own authorization; (b) confirm the seeded dev doctor accounts have an approved profile with `fullName` and `specialty` populated, otherwise the directory is empty and the card shows the unresolved state during the demo.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/doctor-kyc.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/doctor-kyc.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/doctor-directory.test.ts`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/api/doctors.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/consultation/FindingStep.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/consultation/ConfirmationStep.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/PatientBookingDetail.tsx`

### ADR-20260805-03: Client-side session guard for memory-only auth state

- Date: 2026-08-05
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: A signed-in user who reloaded a page found the session gone and no redirect — the app sat on a loading skeleton indefinitely.

  The cause is a lifetime mismatch introduced deliberately by ADR-20260726-01. Auth tokens live in memory only, while the `bayan-auth` cookie carries just `{ roles, expiresAt }` so `proxy.ts` middleware can make a server-side routing decision. On reload the cookie survives and the tokens do not. The server guard therefore still answers "allow", the page renders, and every data query stays disabled because there is no token to send. Nothing in the system noticed the contradiction, because both halves were behaving as designed.
- Decision:
  - Add a `SessionGuard` client component, mounted once in the root layout. On a non-public path it checks the in-memory store and, when there is no valid session, clears the marker cookie and redirects to `/signIn?next=<path>`.
  - `api.ts` gains an `onUnauthorized` listener registry and fires it on any `401`. `SessionGuard` subscribes, so a server-side session loss produces the same clear-and-redirect. The registry keeps `api.ts` free of store and router imports.
  - `SignInForm` honours `?next=` through `resolveSignInDestination`, which accepts only same-origin absolute paths. A property test asserts no input can make it resolve off-origin, so the parameter cannot become an open redirect.
  - Public paths are read from the existing `isPublicPath` in `lib/route-guard.ts`, so the landing page, OTL intake links, and prescription verification are never bounced.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Persist tokens to `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`: rejected. That is precisely what ADR-20260726-01 removed, and a bearer token in web storage is readable by any script that gets injected.
  - Refresh tokens silently on reload: rejected for now. The refresh token is also memory-only, so there is nothing to refresh from; introducing a persisted refresh token reopens the same storage question and needs its own decision.
  - Have middleware clear the cookie: rejected. The server cannot see whether the client still holds tokens, so it cannot distinguish a reload from a normal navigation.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: session loss now fails fast into sign-in and returns the user to where they were, instead of hanging on a skeleton.
  - Trade-offs: a page reload always costs a re-authentication. That is inherent to memory-only tokens, and this change makes the cost visible and recoverable rather than presenting it as a broken page.
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/components/auth/SessionGuard.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/layout.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/api.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/authentication/components/forms/SignInForm.tsx`

### ADR-20260805-04: On-demand consultation request pool behind a per-environment flag

- Date: 2026-08-05
- Status: Accepted — implemented; **enabled in `dev` only** as of ADR-20260808-02, still disabled in `staging` and `prod`, unqualified everywhere. **Superseded on identity disclosure by ADR-20260808-02** — the "withhold every identifying field until acceptance" decision below no longer holds; `patientName` is now disclosed pre-acceptance. Everything else in this ADR stands. The patient-facing waiting screen for a pooled request is ADR-20260808-03.
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A) — direction from the CEO, rollout shape chosen by Dev A
- Context: The CEO wants the Grab/Angkas shape: a patient completes intake and pays, doctors see a pool of open requests with the time and a sense of why, a doctor accepts, and the consultation proceeds. The current model instead auto-assigns one doctor server-side inside the payment hold (`finalizeBookingAfterPaymentConfirm` → `matchDoctorForBooking`), so there is no pool and no acceptance step.

  A tension was raised: the intake form also collects a desired schedule, which reads as contradicting an on-demand flow. The codebase is already partly modelled for both — `bookingMode` is `scheduled | on_demand`, `matcher.ts` branches on it, and `intakeQueueStatus` is already a doctor-side accept/reject signal — so the gap is the pool and the claim, not the concept.

  The constraint is timing. This lands two days before the 2026-08-07 demo, whose entire purpose is two physicians and the CEO evaluating the end-to-end booking, consult, and AI flow. A change that plausibly breaks the scheduled path is worse than no change at all.
- Decision:
  - Implement the pool, and gate every part of it behind `ON_DEMAND_POOL_ENABLED`, defaulting to `"false"` in all environments. Enabling it is an explicit per-environment act with its own authorization.
  - Two routes on the existing intake-queue Lambda: `GET /v1/doctors/me/request-pool` and `POST /v1/doctors/me/request-pool/{bookingId}/accept`. Both require the `doctor` role and consult-approved KYC. Both return `404` when the flag is off, so a disabled feature is indistinguishable from an absent one.
  - **No new keys, indexes, or entity types.** A pooled request is a `confirmed` booking with `bookingMode = on_demand` and no `doctorId`, which the gsi3 `STATUS#confirmed` partition (access pattern B4) already addresses. `DATA_MODEL.md` is unchanged and there is no migration.
  - **Acceptance is a single conditional write.** `attribute_not_exists(doctorId) AND status = confirmed AND bookingMode = on_demand` makes the claim an atomic compare-and-set, so concurrent accepts resolve to exactly one winner and the rest receive a non-retryable `409 REQUEST_ALREADY_CLAIMED`. A read-then-write would let a second doctor overwrite the first.
  - **The pool is PHI-minimised.** `OnDemandRequest` carries `bookingId`, `serviceType`, `channel`, `scheduledAt`, `requestedAt`, price, whether intake is submitted, and a whitespace-collapsed chief-complaint excerpt capped at 160 characters. There is no `patientId`, name, date of birth, or full intake body: a doctor who has not accepted is not yet this patient's clinician. Identity becomes available through the existing booking and intake endpoints on acceptance. A property test asserts no generated `patientId` can appear in a pool entry.
  - **Accepting does not start the consultation.** It assigns the doctor and enqueues the intake item, mirroring auto-matching exactly. The session and chat still begin through one-time-link activation, so the consultation lifecycle and the `in_progress` chat precondition are untouched.
  - The matcher bypass fires only for an unassigned `on_demand` booking with the flag enabled. Scheduled bookings, already-assigned bookings, and every booking with the flag off take the original path.
  - Frontend: a `RequestPool` component on the doctor dashboard that renders nothing when the route answers `404`, scoped to its own `QueryClient` rather than adding a provider to the shared doctor layout.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Ship the full model unflagged for the demo: rejected. It changes the payment-hold path that every booking traverses, two days before the event the path exists to demonstrate.
  - Design only, implement nothing: considered seriously. Rejected because the design questions that actually carry risk — the acceptance race and how much PHI a pre-acceptance doctor may see — are only answerable by writing the conditional write and the projection. A document would have deferred both.
  - Introduce a new booking status such as `awaiting_acceptance`: rejected. `BookingStatus` is referenced across the contract, the cancellable and reschedulable sets, notifications, and the CDS session lifecycle. Reusing `confirmed` + absent `doctorId` expresses the same state with no enum change and no migration.
  - Reuse `payment_submitted` to mean "paid, awaiting acceptance": rejected. It means "proof submitted, awaiting review" on the legacy path, and overloading it would make both meanings unreadable.
  - Have acceptance create the chat session: rejected for now. Chat requires `status = in_progress` and an active session, both produced by OTL activation. Making acceptance create them would move the start of the consultation, which is a separate decision.
  - Expose full intake to the pool so doctors can triage properly: rejected. The bounded excerpt is the smallest disclosure that still supports the accept/decline judgement. If physicians report it is insufficient, widen it deliberately with its own decision rather than defaulting to full disclosure.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the model exists, is race-safe, and can be demonstrated by flipping one variable. The scheduled path is provably unaffected — tests pin the bypass boundary in all four flag/mode combinations.
  - Trade-offs: two routes exist in every environment's API Gateway inventory while returning `404`, which keeps the route inventory uniform but means the contract describes behaviour that is off by default. The Terraform variable and the handler guard are the only places that state changes.
  - Trade-offs: pool listing filters after the index key condition, so a page can be empty while a cursor remains. Callers must follow the cursor rather than stopping on an empty page; the contract says so explicitly.
  - Trade-offs: listing performs one intake read per pooled request. Acceptable at MVP volume and bounded by the page limit; if the pool grows, denormalise the excerpt onto the booking at intake submission.
  - **Not qualified.** Implemented and flag-gated is not enabled, and enabled in `dev` would not be staging qualification, canary authorization, or production readiness. The patient-side "Consult now vs Schedule later" choice and the conditional schedule field are **not** implemented — the pool currently only serves bookings that are already `on_demand`.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) decide whether to enable in `dev` for the demo, under its own point-of-action authorization, and confirm a seeded on-demand booking exists to populate the pool; (b) implement the patient-side mode choice and conditional schedule field, which is what actually resolves the schedule/on-demand contradiction; (c) decide whether `payment/hold` should stop confirming an on-demand booking so acceptance is what confirms it — deliberately left alone here to avoid touching the payment state machine before the demo; (d) revisit the excerpt bound after physician feedback.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/on-demand-pool.ts`, `backend/src/lib/intake-queue.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/intake-queue.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/on-demand-pool.test.ts`, `backend/src/lib/on-demand-pool-bypass.test.ts`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/variables.tf`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/lib/api/requestPool.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/RequestPool.tsx`

### ADR-20260806-01: Scheduled consultation lifecycle wiring, and structured red-flag screening in intake

- Date: 2026-08-06
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: A trace of the scheduled end-to-end flow found it intact from booking through doctor confirmation and then stopped completely. Four defects compounded, and none of them was visible from the backend alone because every backend route involved was correct, deployed, and fully gated.

  1. **No UI could start a consultation.** `POST /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/otl` had zero frontend callers — `issueOtl` appeared only in generated types. The doctor's "Proceed to Teleconsult" button flipped local state and made no request. Chat was complete on both sides, including the `ws-token` exchange and HTTP fallback, and entirely unreachable.
  2. **The one-time link is single-use, and the only chat surface was keyed by it.** Whoever opened the link consumed it; the other participant received `409` with nowhere to go. Two-party chat was impossible even with a hand-minted token.
  3. **`consultationId` was invisible to clients.** OTL activation writes it onto the booking item, but `toApiBooking` did not project it and the contract's `Booking` schema did not declare it. The post-consult workspace is keyed by `consultationId`, so only the party that activated the link ever learned it.
  4. **Nothing in the deployed runtime wrote CDS clinical input.** `mutateClinicalSource`, `ingestTrustedExternalSource`, and `normalizeClinicalInput` had no handler or route caller. Every downstream stage loads a normalized snapshot as its safety evidence and fails closed with `503 RED_FLAG_EVALUATION_FAILED` when none exists, so candidate ranking, gate-token issuance, and protected generation were unreachable for any consultation created by the application. The only path through was running `scripts/prepare-cds-demo-consultation.ts` by hand, per consultation.

  Investigating (4) surfaced a fifth issue that changes what the bridge had to be. `matchesRule` in the deterministic red-flag router resolves a rule's field against the normalized snapshot and treats an **absent** field as "no match". The seeded ruleset routes on `chestPain`, `dyspnea`, and `feverDays` — none of which the intake form collected. Ingesting intake as-is would therefore have produced a router that returns ROUTINE for every consultation regardless of what the patient reported, while looking fully operational.
- Decision:
  - **Expose `consultationId` on `Booking`** in the contract and in `toApiBooking`.
  - **Add a booking-keyed consultation room** at `/consultation/room/{bookingId}`, backed by the previously uncalled `GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/state`. Both participants occupy the same conversation; `/consultation/[token]` remains the public single-use link path.
  - **The doctor starts the consultation and consumes the link themselves** (`useStartConsultation`: issue, then activate, then enter the room). With a durable room this is strictly better than sending the token, because a single-use link handed to one party locks the other out. If a session already exists the hook joins instead of minting.
  - **The room carries the only "End consultation" control**, calling `POST /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/complete` and routing the doctor to the post-consult workspace with `?consultationId=`.
  - **Add structured red-flag screening to intake** as `details.safetyScreen` — `chestPain`, `dyspnea`, `feverDays` — asked as explicit three-state questions (yes / no / unanswered), never inferred from free text. Every layer preserves the third state: the sanitizer drops uninterpretable input rather than coercing it, the mapper omits unanswered fields rather than sending `false`, the ingested content carries a `screeningCompleteness` record, and the doctor's panel renders "not asked" distinctly from "No".
  - **Bridge intake into CDS clinical input** on consult completion (`backend/src/lib/cds/intake-clinical-input.ts`), through the approved Trusted Clinical Ingestion Adapter entry point followed by `normalizeClinicalInput`, so every fence, digest, and revision the gate later verifies is produced exactly as production produces it. Only `submitted` or `acknowledged` intake is ingestible; a `draft` is refused. The source id is derived from the booking so re-ingestion revises one source instead of appending toward the source cap.
  - **Replace the doctor's fabricated subjective panel** with the real submitted intake from the already-doctor-readable `GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/intake`.
  - **Unify the OLDCART key vocabulary.** The patient form emitted `character`, `aggravating`, `alleviating`, and `priorTreatment` while the backend `OldcartField` constants named `characteristics`, `aggreviating_and_alleviating_factors`, and `treatments_tried`. The stored map is open, so nothing rejected the mismatch: four of seven fields read back empty for any consumer using the constants. `OldcartField` now covers what the form actually collects — including `radiation` and `timing`, which had no counterpart — and aggravating and alleviating stay separate keys so the patient's answers round-trip exactly.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Derive the red-flag answers from OLDCART free text: rejected outright. "Tightness in my chest" contains no occurrence of "chest pain", so string matching produces silent false negatives on exactly the presentations that matter most.
  - Default an unanswered screening question to `false`: rejected. It yields the same ROUTINE outcome as omission, so it buys nothing, and it destroys the distinction between "screened negative" and "never asked" — which is the only thing that makes the gap auditable.
  - Clamp an out-of-range `feverDays` into range: rejected. A clamped value is an invented clinical duration. Dropping it reads as unanswered, which is true.
  - Add a fourth intake wizard step for screening: rejected. The step enum drives the wizard, progress indicator, and submit validation on both sides; nesting the screen inside the existing `details` section achieves the same collection with no flow change.
  - Send the one-time link to the patient instead of the doctor consuming it: rejected for the primary path, because a single-use link means the first opener locks the second out of a token-scoped page. Kept available for a future unauthenticated recipient.
  - Ingest intake from the CDS Lambda rather than the payments Lambda: rejected as unnecessary. `handleCompleteConsult` already calls `endCanonicalSessionAndInitializeAssessment`, so the payments Lambda already writes CDS aggregates on this exact trigger; the bridge is the same class of write behind the same gate and needs no new IAM grant.
  - Trigger ingestion at intake submission rather than at completion: rejected. The Clinical_Input_Head does not exist until the canonical session ends, so there is nothing to advance before completion.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the scheduled flow now runs booking → intake → payment → doctor confirm → consultation → completion → post-consult workspace from the application, with no manual script. The red-flag router acts on real patient answers rather than absent fields. The doctor reads the patient's actual intake.
  - Positive: `screeningCompleteness` makes "unscreened" a first-class, auditable state rather than something inferred from a missing key.
  - Trade-offs: intake ingestion is best-effort on the completion path, matching the existing session-end call — payment capture has already succeeded and must not be rolled back. A skip is logged with a PHI-safe reason code, because a skipped ingestion means the consultation will still fail closed downstream and that must not be silent.
  - Trade-offs: the OLDCART vocabulary change orphans any `aggreviating_and_alleviating_factors` values already stored in `dev`. Accepted — `dev` is strictly synthetic, and no other environment has intake data.
  - Trade-offs: screening covers exactly the three fields the seeded ruleset routes on. A future ruleset that adds a field will silently not fire until intake collects it. That coupling is inherent to a field-matching router; the mitigation is that adding a rule now requires adding the question, and `screeningCompleteness` shows when it was not asked.
  - **Not qualified.** This is local evidence on an uncommitted tree. Reaching the post-consult workspace still requires the policy `generationMode` to be `enabled` or `canary` on `CDS#POLICY#<env> / ACTIVE`, and the doctor's KYC to be `approved` — `GET /v1/doctors/me/intake-queue` and OTL issuance both refuse otherwise.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) confirm the seeded `dev` doctor has an approved KYC profile with `fullName` and `specialty`, or the dashboard, directory, and consultation start all refuse; (b) the doctor has no route back to a completed consultation's workspace — the intake queue returns only `confirmed` bookings, so a "recent consultations" list is still needed; (c) wire the delivered patient-education corpus, which is the remaining gap in the post-consult chain; (d) no Terraform change was required — all five routes involved were already deployed.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/booking.ts`, `backend/src/lib/intake.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/intake-clinical-input.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/payments.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/api/consultSession.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/session/ConsultationRoom.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/consultation/room/[bookingId]/page.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/hooks/useStartConsultation.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/homepage/ReadyIntakeContent.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/consultation/intake/SafetyScreenFields.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/intakeMapper.ts`

### ADR-20260806-02: Dev doctor-approval index repair, and removal of fabricated data from the patient and doctor routes

- Date: 2026-08-06
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Two pre-demo prerequisites, one of which turned out to be a live blocker, plus a full audit of fabricated data on the surfaces the 2026-08-07 audience will use.

  **The approved doctor was invisible to the product.** `testdoctor@example.com` held a profile with `verificationStatus: "approved"` and **no `gsi3pk`/`gsi3sk`**. Approval status lives on the item, but every "who is approved?" query reads the gsi3 `KYC#approved` partition, which was empty:
  - `listApprovedDoctorIds` in `lib/matcher.ts` — so `matchDoctorForBooking` returned null and **no doctor was ever auto-assigned to any booking**. A paid booking sat `confirmed` and unassigned, never entered a doctor's intake queue, and could never be started.
  - `listKycApplicationsByStatus('approved')` — so `GET /v1/doctors` returned an empty directory and the assigned-doctor card never resolved a name.

  Current `reviewDoctorKyc` writes both index keys on approval, so this is legacy dev data drift, not a live code defect. It is nonetheless silent: nothing detects a profile that is approved on the item and absent from the index. The CDS policy prerequisite needed no work — `CDS#POLICY#dev / ACTIVE` was already `generationMode: "enabled"` at revision 2.

  **The fabricated-data audit found fifteen reachable findings.** Ranked by what a reviewer could act on:
  - *Clinical.* `useProfile` returned a `setTimeout` stub with weight `"70"`, height `"175"`, `allergens: ["Pollen"]`, and three invented relatives carrying `["Penicillin"]`, `["Sulfa","Shellfish"]`, and `["Latex","Peanuts"]`. `PersonDataSection.populateSelfData()` writes those into the intake form, which is submitted to `PUT /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/intake` — so **a fabricated allergy list could enter a real clinical record** and read to a physician as the patient's own answer. `PatientDetails` rendered height `"150" cm`, weight `"60" kg`, and five invented allergens with no props at all, in the post-consult workspace where the Assessment is written. `AudioMarkers` carried a scripted chest-pain transcript. `prescriptionData.ts` held a ~35-drug client-side formulary.
  - *Identity.* Four invented appointments ("Juan Carlos 9:00 AM" …) on every doctor route via the header. A copy-able fake patient phone `"63+ 9182758492"`, an emergency number, and `"PATIENT ADDRESS"` shown identically for every booking. `"Dr. Juan"` for every doctor id on the create-booking route. The sidebar reverse-engineered the doctor's name from their email local part.
  - *Commercial.* Ten fabricated fields per doctor card — a "Featured" badge, `"Cardiologist"`, `"St. Luke's Medical Center · BGC"`, `~ 30 km away`, `"English · Filipino"`, four-and-a-half hardcoded stars, rating `"4.9"`, `312` reviews, `14 yrs` — every one of which fired on every render because the mapper never populated them. A local price table quoting `teleconsult: ₱400` while the server prices the same booking at 50000 centavos (₱500), so the summary and the payment step disagreed about money. `sampleAds` under a "Sponsored | Partners" heading. A hardcoded six-slot time window the patient picked a consultation time from.
- Decision:
  - **Add `scripts/seed-dev-doctor-profile.ts`** (`npm run script:seed-dev-doctor-profile`) to reconcile a doctor profile to approved-and-indexed, building the index keys with the production builders (`doctorProfilePrimaryKey`, `kycQueueIndexKey`) so the written shape cannot drift from what the readers expect. Dev-guarded, idempotent, and refuses to create a profile without an explicit `--full-name`. Ran with `--repair-only` against dev; `KYC#approved` now resolves the doctor.
  - **Replace fabricated data with the real source wherever one exists.** The doctor's own name and availability from `GET /v1/doctors/me/kyc`; today's schedule from `GET /v1/doctors/me/intake-queue`; the booking-request and post-consult patient panels from `GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/intake`; the create-booking doctor and slot times from `GET /v1/doctors/{doctorId}` and `.../schedules`; the doctor card's specialty from `DoctorPublicSummary`.
  - **Where no source exists, remove rather than re-source.** The platform holds no patient phone, address, emergency contact, doctor rating, review count, hospital, distance, or years of experience, and has no advertisement or patient-profile endpoint. Those fields are deleted. `useProfile` now returns an empty profile seeded only with the signed-in email, and exposes `isPersisted: false` so no caller presents a save that did not happen.
  - **Thread `bookingId` alongside `consultationId`** into the post-consult workspace, because the patient's intake is addressed by booking id. The consultation room supplies both on handover.
  - **Do not parse structured vitals out of prose.** Height, weight, and diet are folded into `details.medicalHistory` as free text by the intake mapper, so the post-consult panel shows that block verbatim rather than re-deriving numbers from it.
  - **Delete superseded legacy UI whose only data source was fabricated**: the prescription authoring chain (`PrescriptionForm` → `PrescriptionMaker` → `Deliverables`, already forbidden from the production route by `assessmentFirst.security.test.ts` and unreachable), `mockData.ts`, `ObjectiveContent`, `OldcartItem`, `adsData.ts`, `DoctorInvoice`, `BookingDoctorHeader`, `BookingDoctorDetails`.
  - **Add `src/app/fabricated-data-scan.test.ts`**, a source-level guard asserting the removed literals do not reappear anywhere under the patient and doctor route trees. It strips comments before scanning, using a string-aware pass rather than a regex so a `https://` inside a literal is not mistaken for a line comment.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Repair the dev index by hand in the console: rejected. The same drift would recur with no record of what was done, and a script makes the correct shape reproducible.
  - Add a runtime consistency check that flags approved-but-unindexed profiles: considered and deferred. It is the right long-term guard, but it belongs with the admin/moderator surface rather than being bolted onto a read path two days before a demo.
  - Keep the fabricated doctor ratings and hospitals as "obvious placeholders": rejected. The audience is two physicians assessing clinical credibility, and the data layer already states the platform does not hold those fields. A fabricated peer credential is worse than a visibly absent one.
  - Keep the local price table so the patient sees a figure before booking: rejected. The figure was wrong — it disagreed with what the server actually charges — and a wrong number about money is worse than deferring the number to the payment step, which shows the real `amountCents`.
  - Parse height and weight out of `medicalHistory` prose: rejected. The intake mapper's own documentation gives the reason: re-deriving individual fields from prose invents data.
  - A general "no hardcoded strings" lint rule instead of a literal scan: rejected as unmaintainable — it would fight legitimate UI copy and configuration lists, and the distinction that matters is whether a value *purports to be real user or clinical data*, which no lint rule can judge.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: no fabricated clinical value can reach a submitted intake or a physician's Assessment screen. The doctor dashboard, header, sidebar, booking flow, and doctor cards all render backend data or an explicit unresolved state. Auto-assignment works in dev, which unblocks the whole scheduled flow.
  - Positive: the scan makes reintroduction a test failure rather than a discovery in front of a reviewer.
  - Trade-offs: several screens are visibly emptier. Doctor cards show name, specialty, and real availability only; the booking summary defers the fee; the patient profile starts blank. That is the honest surface area of what the platform currently stores, and each empty field is a real gap now made visible rather than hidden.
  - Trade-offs: `useProfile` edits live in a per-tab memory map and are lost on reload. Persisting them needs a patient-profile endpoint, which does not exist; `isPersisted: false` marks the gap rather than papering over it.
  - Trade-offs: the doctor board's name slot shows a booking reference (`Ref A1B2C3`), not a person. The intake queue exposes no patient name and there is no name source for the doctor surface.
  - Trade-offs: the doctor shell now needs a `QueryClient`, so one was added to the doctor layout and to the two render-smoke harnesses that mount route components without their layout.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) decide whether to add a patient-profile endpoint, without which the profile screen cannot persist; (b) add a consistency check or moderator-visible warning for approved-but-unindexed doctor profiles; (c) the doctor still has no route back to a completed consultation's workspace, since the intake queue returns only `confirmed` bookings; (d) `postConsultationDate.ts` retains an ICD suggestion list, kept because two assessment-first preservation tests import it and the production route is guarded against the chain that consumes it.
- Related files: `backend/scripts/seed-dev-doctor-profile.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/fabricated-data-scan.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/hooks/useProfile.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/hooks/useMyDoctorProfile.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/history/DoctorHistory.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/homepage/BookingRequestContent.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/sidebar/SidebarTeleconsultStatus.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/doctor/DoctorList.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/doctor/DoctorBookingLoader.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/BookingSummary.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/PatientDetails.tsx`

### ADR-20260806-03: Patient-education corpus wired — keyed ICD-10 retrieval, corpus-grounded generation and fallback

- Date: 2026-08-06
- Status: Accepted — ingested and live in `dev`; not qualified beyond `dev`
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A); corpus authored and approved by Marco Paolo Perpetua, General Physician (2026-08-06)
- Context: ADR-20260804-01 decided the shape of the patient-education knowledge base — keyed ICD-10 retrieval in `app_core`, bilingual required for approval, mandatory citation, no embeddings and no vector store in V1 — and `PATIENT_EDUCATION_CORPUS_SCHEMA.md` issued the 18-column schema. The corpus was delivered on 2026-08-06: 24 conditions, all 18 columns populated, 0 blockers against its own validation gate.

  Until now nothing consumed it. `patient-education-generations` was contracted, routed, and gated, but its grounding was a synthesized one-liner — `Approved immutable grounding bundle for ${diagnosis}.` — and its deterministic fallback was a fixed two-sentence template (`"Follow the care plan reviewed by your physician."`). The only real bilingual content in the repo was five solvers in `solver-content.ts`, reachable solely from the terminally retired `handlePatientCards`.

  **The join was the hard part.** The confirmed Assessment carries a free-text `diagnosis` (max 256 chars) and the diagnosis catalog has **no ICD-10 field** — `ImmutableDiagnosisCatalogEntry` holds `diagnosisName` and `manualAliases` only. So there was no code to key on, and "keyed ICD-10 retrieval" needed an explicit join rather than an assumed one.
- Decision:
  - **Two point-read entities, no new GSI.** `PATIENT_EDU#<ICD10> / APPROVED` holds the article; `PATIENT_EDU#ALIAS#<normalizedKey> / APPROVED` holds an exact-match pointer to a code. Retrieval is at most two `GetItem` calls. `DATA_MODEL.md` documents both with access patterns PE1/PE2.
  - **Ingest writes one alias per known name** for each condition — English name, Filipino name, and the ICD-10 code itself. Lookup normalizes the confirmed diagnosis with the same pure function the ingest used (`normalizeEducationKey`: case-folded, accent-stripped, punctuation-reduced, space-collapsed) and does a single exact Get. Two strings either normalize to the same key or they do not; **nothing is matched approximately**, which is what keeps this consistent with the no-semantic-retrieval decision.
  - **A diagnosis with no alias yields no education content**, and generation falls back to the deterministic template. This is the corpus author's own stated position, adopted verbatim: a condition with no approved article "silently has no patient education, which is safer than showing an approximate neighbour."
  - **Both the model prompt and the deterministic fallback are corpus-grounded.** `groundingPassagesFor` emits six passages carrying English and Filipino side by side, so the model rephrases from reviewed bilingual text rather than translating clinical advice itself. `patientEducationTemplate` builds the fallback payload from the same article. Grounding the fallback matters as much as grounding the prompt: the deterministic path runs precisely when the provider is unavailable, times out, or fails a safety gate, which is when the output still has to be clinically usable.
  - **Fail closed on incomplete content.** Both the ingest and the read-time validator reject a row missing either language of any of the six sections, or a citation, reviewer, or review date. A partial article still renders, and half-translated clinical advice is not approved content.
  - **Contract extended** so the mandatory citation is a first-class field: `CdsPatientEducationPayload` gains `citation`, `icd10Code`, `titleFilipino`, `corpusVersion`, and a `bilingual` language value; `CdsPatientEducationSection` gains a per-section `language`.
  - **Ingest is an operator script** (`npm run script:ingest-patient-education`), dev-guarded, idempotent, with a `--dry-run` and a per-row rejection report. Run against `dev`: 24 articles, 71 alias keys, 0 rejected.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Fuzzy or embedding-based matching from diagnosis text to condition: rejected. ADR-20260804-01 ruled out a vector store for V1, and the failure mode is the dangerous one — a near match returns a *different condition's* self-care and red flags with full apparent authority.
  - Add an ICD-10 field to the diagnosis catalog and key off that: cleaner long-term, rejected for now. The catalog is content-addressed and policy-activated, so changing its entry shape re-digests every entry and forces a policy re-activation — a governed change, not a two-day one. The alias index achieves keyed retrieval without touching approved policy content.
  - Match on `catalogEntryId`: rejected. It is optional on a confirmed Assessment (`'unmapped'` when absent) and is a content digest, not a clinical code, so it carries no ICD relationship.
  - Store one item per language: rejected. Bilingual is an approval condition, so the two languages must travel together or the article is not approved; splitting them would let a half-approved article be read.
  - Merge English and Filipino into one rendered blob: rejected. Tagging each section with its language lets a patient-facing surface show either without a machine translation step.
  - Ship an admin HTTP ingest route now: deferred. ADR-20260804-01 specified admin-only CSV ingest, and a dev-guarded operator script satisfies the authorization intent without adding a route, a contract operation, and a Terraform change two days before the demo. The route remains outstanding — see follow-ups.
  - Reuse the `SOLVER#` solver-content entity: rejected. It is keyed by solver id for Layer 5 patient cards, which is a different retrieval key and a retired path.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: patient-education generation is grounded in clinician-approved, cited, bilingual content for all 24 corpus conditions, on both the model path and the fallback. Retrieval is two point reads with no scan, no GSI, and no vector infrastructure.
  - Positive: the citation is structural rather than prose, so a released artifact is traceable to its guideline, reviewer, and review date.
  - Trade-offs: coverage is 24 conditions. Anything outside the corpus gets the generic deterministic template. That is the intended safe behaviour, but it means a demo diagnosis must be inside the corpus to show real content — and it will be, only if the confirmed diagnosis text normalizes to one of the indexed names.
  - Trade-offs: the alias index must be re-ingested whenever a condition name changes, and a stale alias would point at the old code. Ingest is idempotent and re-runnable, but nothing detects an orphaned alias.
  - Trade-offs: a row removed from the CSV is not deleted from the table. Deletion is left as a deliberate separate operation rather than inferred from absence.
  - **Not qualified.** Ingested in `dev` only. The corpus itself carries 50 open warnings from its own validation — 24 rows cite no Philippine primary source, and 26 English fields average more than 25 words per sentence against a grade-6 target. Neither blocked clinical approval; both are recorded in `architecture/corpus_validation_report.md` and remain the CEO's call.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) add the admin-only HTTP ingest route ADR-20260804-01 specified, so corpus updates do not require an operator with table credentials; (b) **there is still no patient-facing surface for released patient education** — `outputs/current` is doctor and admin-break-glass only, so a released article has no reader; (c) obtain Philippine primary sources for the 24 rows that cite only international guidelines; (d) the approval record lives in a Word document footer, which is editable and not a durable audit trail — capture sign-off somewhere immutable if the audit process requires it.
- Related files: `architecture/patient_education_corpus_v1.csv`, `architecture/HANDOVER.md`, `architecture/corpus_validation_report.md`, `architecture/PATIENT_EDUCATION_CORPUS_SCHEMA.md`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/patient-education.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/patient-education.test.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/protected-generation.ts`, `backend/scripts/ingest-patient-education-corpus.ts`, `contracts/openapi.yaml`

### ADR-20260806-04: Patient-readable released patient education, and the doctor's route back to a completed consultation

- Date: 2026-08-06
- Status: Accepted — implemented locally; not qualified in any environment
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Two gaps left the post-consult chain open at both ends. The larger one sat on the patient side.

  **A released article had no reader.** Physician release already wrote `patientVisibilityState = 'patient_visible'` and `patientVisible = true` onto both the artifact and its `CDS#OUTPUT_CURRENT` pointer, conditionally and revision-fenced, in `backend/src/lib/cds/protected-artifact-lifecycle.ts`. **Nothing read either flag.** Every route under `/v1/cds/` is doctor-only, with an admin break-glass path on history, so the two fields that exist specifically to express "the patient may see this" were write-only. ADR-20260806-03 then ingested the corpus and grounded both the model prompt and the deterministic fallback in clinician-approved bilingual content — 24 conditions, 71 alias keys — and that content still could not reach a patient. Release was a state transition with no consumer, which meant the physician's release action had no observable effect outside the doctor's own view.

  **A completed consultation vanished from the doctor's side.** `GET /v1/doctors/me/intake-queue` returns only `confirmed` bookings, so the moment a consultation completed it disappeared from every doctor-side surface. The post-consult workspace was reachable only through the redirect the room's "End consultation" button fires (ADR-20260806-01). Close that tab and the Assessment became unreachable — which matters precisely because assessment-first gating means Plan, Rx, final ICD, medical certificate, lab/imaging requests, and patient education unlock only after the physician confirms an Assessment post-consult (ADR-20260703-01). The release step this ADR gives a reader is itself downstream of an Assessment the doctor could no longer navigate back to.
- Decision:
  - **One new patient-readable CDS route.** `GET /v1/cds/consultations/{consultationId}/patient-education/released`, `operationId: getReleasedPatientEducation`, returning `CdsReleasedPatientEducation`. It is the only patient-readable operation under `/v1/cds/`.
  - **It does not go through `handleCdsContractRoute`.** That function's rule is "assigned doctor, or admin holding a break-glass grant on history". It is the authorization boundary for twenty-four gated operations and is heavily tested. Adding a patient branch to it would widen a doctor-only gate so that one read could work, and every future reader of that function would have to re-derive which branch applies to whom. Authorization for this route is therefore written out separately and narrowly, and the shared boundary is left untouched.
  - **Participant only.** The caller must be the consultation's patient or its assigned physician. Participants are read from the canonical session projection with `ConsistentRead: true`, so an assignment change cannot be read off a stale replica by an actor who has just been removed.
  - **Released and patient-visible only.** A generated artifact, a finalized-but-unreleased one, and a stale one are all treated as absent. **Both** visibility flags are required, not either: a single flag could be the result of a partial write, and neither alone is sufficient evidence that a physician released the article. The read follows the `CDS#OUTPUT_CURRENT#patient_education` pointer, so it always resolves the current revision rather than a historical one, and short-circuits on the pointer's mirrored `patientVisible` so an unreleased artifact costs one read rather than two.
  - **One concealed outcome for every negative case.** Unknown consultation, malformed consultation id, non-participant caller, and nothing-released all return the same `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` with the same message, so the response discloses neither existence nor participation nor release state. Responses carry `Cache-Control: no-store`. The frontend client maps `404` to `null`, and the card renders nothing — deliberately no empty state, because a panel reading "your doctor hasn't shared anything" invites a patient to chase a clinician over a step that may never apply to their consultation.
  - **Explicit three-field projection.** The stored artifact also carries the gate token family epoch, revision fences, the normalized-input digest, ruleset and policy activation versions, the generation eligibility digest, and the physician actor id. Returning the item would hand the entire authorization envelope to the least-privileged reader in the system. The response is `consultationId`, `releasedAt`, and `payload`, nothing else; the schema is `additionalProperties: false`, and a property test asserts no gate-authorization envelope field — and no envelope *value* — survives the projection.
  - **Patient surface on the booking detail route.** `PatientEducationCard` mounts in `PatientBookingDetail`, keyed by the `consultationId` that ADR-20260806-01 projected onto `Booking`. It shows reviewer-authored English and Filipino behind a language toggle — not a machine translation — and the citation that ADR-20260804-01 makes mandatory, so a patient can see what the advice rests on. Warning signs render as one list in the selected language rather than two, since a duplicated urgent-care list is harder to act on.
  - **Terraform gets a `patient_cds_route_keys` local** in `infra/modules/cds_operations/routes.tf`, unioned into `active_cds_route_keys`, keeping Terraform/OpenAPI route parity. It is kept as its own list rather than folded into the assessment-first set, which is doctor-only by design — a reader glancing at that set should not find a patient route inside it.
  - **A doctor-side completed-consultations list** on the doctor homepage reads the already-deployed `GET /v1/bookings`, keeps the `completed` bookings, and links to the post-consult workspace with both `consultationId` and `bookingId` — the same two parameters the room's redirect supplies, because the patient's intake is addressed by booking id. **No contract, backend, or Terraform change was needed.** Because `GET /v1/bookings` paginates by opaque cursor across all statuses rather than by status, the read walks a bounded five cursor pages and stops as soon as it has something to show, so a dashboard mount cannot fan out request count over a long booking history. A completed booking with **no** `consultationId` — one that completed without a session ever being activated — renders without a link and says so, rather than routing to a workspace that cannot resolve. No patient name is rendered, because the `Booking` contract exposes `patientId` and no name and the platform holds no patient-name source for the doctor surface; an entry is identified by service type and booking reference, consistent with ADR-20260806-02.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Add a patient branch to `handleCdsContractRoute`: rejected. It is the single authorization boundary for twenty-four doctor-only operations, and the change would be a widening of that boundary rather than an addition beside it. One narrow read is not worth making the shared gate's rule conditional on the caller's role.
  - Let the patient read `GET /v1/cds/consultations/{consultationId}/outputs/current`: rejected. That operation returns the full protected-artifact envelope for any output type, including Plan, prescription, and final ICD, and its payload carries the gate machinery. Making it patient-readable would expose six other protected output types and the authorization envelope in order to deliver one article.
  - Return the artifact item and let the frontend pick fields: rejected outright. The projection is the control. A field the API returns is a field the client, the network, and any log in between have seen, regardless of what the UI renders.
  - Require only `patientVisible`, or only `patientVisibilityState`: rejected. They are written together by the same conditional update, so requiring both costs nothing and makes a partial write fail closed instead of releasing an article on one flag.
  - Eventually-consistent read of the participants: rejected. The check decides whether a patient may read clinical content, and a stale replica could admit an actor whose assignment was just removed. One strongly consistent point read is the correct price.
  - Render an empty state when nothing is released: rejected. Most consultations will never have released education, and a persistent "nothing shared yet" card converts a non-event into an apparent omission by the clinician.
  - Distinguish `403` for a non-participant from `404` for nothing released: rejected. A distinguishable `403` confirms that the consultation exists, which is exactly the disclosure the CDS surface avoids elsewhere.
  - Add a status filter to `GET /v1/bookings` for the doctor's completed list: rejected for now. It is a contract change and a Terraform-adjacent one, and a bounded cursor walk over an already-deployed route achieves the same panel with no new surface. Revisit if a doctor's booking history grows past the bound.
  - Add a dedicated completed-consultations endpoint: rejected as premature. `GET /v1/bookings` already returns exactly the doctor's assigned bookings with the status and the `consultationId` needed to link.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: release is now a state transition with an observable effect. A patient reads clinician-approved, cited, bilingual education for their own consultation, and only after their physician released it.
  - Positive: the doctor-only CDS gate is unchanged. The patient path's authorization is short enough to read in full, and its two rules are stated in one place.
  - Positive: a doctor can return to any completed consultation's workspace, so the Assessment — and therefore every assessment-gated output including the education this ADR publishes — survives a closed tab.
  - Trade-offs: CDS authorization now lives in two places rather than one. Accepted deliberately: the alternative was one place with a role-conditional rule, and the two paths have disjoint audiences, disjoint outputs, and disjoint failure modes. The narrow path grants read on exactly one output type in exactly one lifecycle state.
  - Trade-offs: every negative outcome is a bare `404`, so a patient with a genuine problem and a patient whose doctor released nothing see the same thing, and support cannot distinguish them from the response alone. That is the intended cost of non-disclosure.
  - Trade-offs: the completed-consultations panel costs up to five requests on a cold mount, and a doctor whose completed consultations sit beyond 100 bookings back will not see them. Bounded on purpose; an unbounded walk would read the whole booking history on every mount.
  - Trade-offs: a booking that completed without an activated session shows as a record with no workspace link. Visibly incomplete, which is true — there is no consultation id and therefore no workspace.
  - **Not qualified.** This is local evidence on an uncommitted working tree. It is not staging qualification, not canary authorization or observation, and not production enablement. The new route is defined in the contract and in the Terraform route inventory; it has not been applied to any environment, so the patient surface is unexercised against a deployed API. The corpus warnings recorded in ADR-20260806-03 are unchanged by this work.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) apply the new API Gateway route to `dev` under its own authorization and exercise the patient read end to end against a genuinely released artifact; (b) confirm a demo consultation reaches `released` — a finalized-but-unreleased artifact is correctly invisible, which will look like a broken surface to anyone who has not read this entry; (c) the admin-only HTTP ingest route ADR-20260804-01 specified still does not exist, and the corpus approval record still lives in an editable Word-document footer; (d) revisit the five-page cursor bound if the doctor's booking volume grows.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/cds/released-patient-education.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/released-patient-education.test.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/cds.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/protected-artifact-lifecycle.ts`, `infra/modules/cds_operations/routes.tf`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/api/patientEducation.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/patientEducation/PatientEducationCard.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/PatientBookingDetail.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/consultations/CompletedConsultations.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/doctor/(homepage)/page.tsx`

### ADR-20260806-05: Server-enforced slot overlap rejection, removal of the unsourced SearchDoctor fields, and keeping the dev CDS alarm breaching posture

- Date: 2026-08-06
- Status: Accepted — implemented locally; not qualified in any environment
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Three items, two code defects and one operational posture that was about to be silently changed by a Terraform apply nobody had authorized.

  **Double-booking was only prevented on the client.** `POST /v1/doctors/{doctorId}/schedules` guarded a create on the freshly generated `slotId` alone. A slot's identity is that random id, not its time, so two `09:00`+30 slots on the same date were both accepted and both published to patients. `PUT` was worse: it could move `startTime` or stretch `durationMinutes` straight onto time the doctor had already committed, with no check at all. The only thing standing between a doctor who regenerated a shift and a patient-facing list with every time listed twice was `slotPlan.ts` in the frontend — a screen, which is to say nothing, since the endpoint is reachable without it. `security-wave4-preservation.test.ts` had asserted the *status* guard on `PUT` for some time; nothing asserted anything about time.

  **`SearchDoctor` still declared twelve fields the platform does not hold.** ADR-20260806-02 removed the *renders* — the fabricated `"St. Luke's Medical Center · BGC"`, the `4.9` rating, `312` reviews, `14 yrs`, `~ 30 km away` — but left the type surface intact: `role`, `location`, `hospital`, `rating`, `reviewCount`, `experience`, `price`, `avatarUrl`, `personality`, `languages`, `isFeatured`, `isTopRated`, and an exported `Location` interface carrying `distanceKm`. `mapDoctorToSearchDoctor` populates none of them, and `DoctorPublicSummary` exposes only `doctorId`, `fullName`, `specialty`, and `onDemandAvailable`. An optional field on a view model is a standing invitation to supply a plausible default for it, and `doctor.rating || "4.9"` typechecks cleanly and reads as deliberate — which is exactly how the fabricated values got there the first time.

  **The doctor detail header disclosed an internal identifier as content.** It rendered `ID: {doctorId.slice(-6).toUpperCase()}` beneath the doctor's name.
