From the doctor's side this looked exactly like a dead button, because functionally it was one: the only thing the click did was replace the current page with itself.

  This is a case of a shared endpoint's contract changing at one call site (ADR-20260809-05, deliberately, to open pre-consult chat) without every caller of the endpoint being re-examined against the new contract. `fetchBookingConsultationState`'s own doc comment already stated the new rule plainly — "a successful response no longer means the consultation is live" — but nothing enforced that every reader of its return value actually branched on that, and this hook was the one that had not.
- Decision:
  - **Branch on `existing?.session`, not on `existing` truthiness.** A present `session` is the only fact that means "already running"; a present `booking` with no `session` is the pre-consult phase and must fall through to the real `POST /start` call.
  - **State the coupling in the doc comment**, since this exact failure mode — a shared read's contract changing while a caller's boolean check does not — is invisible in a diff of either file alone.
  - **No change to `fetchBookingConsultationState` or the `/state` route.** The contract from ADR-20260809-05 is correct and is what makes pre-consult chat possible; the bug was entirely in a caller that had not been updated to match it.
  - **A dedicated test file**, since this hook had none. Pins both branches directly — pre-consult (`session` absent) must call `/start`; an active session (`session` present) must not — plus the network-failure fallback and the `409` error path, and is written so that reverting the one-line fix fails two of the four cases (verified by temporarily reverting it before writing this ADR).
- Alternatives considered:
  - Add a separate "is pre-consult" flag to the response instead of relying on `session`'s presence: rejected. `session` presence already carries that information unambiguously; a second flag saying the same thing would be one more place for the two facts to drift apart from each other.
  - Fix it by having `/state` return `404`/`409` for the pre-consult case instead: rejected, and rejecting it again here on purpose — that is the exact rule ADR-20260809-05 replaced, and reverting it would silently re-close pre-consult chat as a side effect of an unrelated fix.
  - Have `useStartConsultation` call `/start` unconditionally, dropping the "join instead" optimization entirely: rejected. The backend already treats a second `/start` as a safe join (idempotent by design), but skipping the check on the client means every resumed consultation pays a redundant round trip, and the explicit branch documents intent that an always-call would not.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: pressing Start consultation now actually starts one — verified by a test that fails without the fix and passes with it.
  - Positive: both call sites (the room's own button, the dashboard drawer's button) share the one hook, so one change fixes both without touching either component.
  - Positive: the doc comment on both the hook and `fetchBookingConsultationState` now cross-reference the coupling, which is the thing that broke silently.
  - Trade-offs: none identified beyond the general risk named above — any future caller of `fetchBookingConsultationState` still has to remember to check `.session` rather than truthiness, and nothing in the type system currently forces that (the field is merely optional, not a discriminated union that would make the wrong check a type error).
  - **Not qualified.** Frontend typecheck, `next build`, lint 0 new errors (52 pre-existing warnings, unchanged), 696 tests across 102 files (4 new, all in a fresh `useStartConsultation.test.tsx`). No backend or infrastructure change.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) re-verify live that Start consultation creates a session and the room upgrades to the in-progress view for both participants; (b) once that is confirmed, verify End consultation actually reaches the post-consult Assessment workspace, which was already implemented but has never been exercised past this blocker; (c) consider narrowing `BookingConsultationState` to a discriminated union (`{ phase: "pre_consult"; booking } | { phase: "live"; booking; session }`) so a caller checking the wrong field is a compile error rather than a silent runtime no-op — this is the second bug in two days caused by a boolean check standing in for a field that can independently be true or false (the first was the `usePatientBoard` query-key mismatch in ADR-20260809-08).
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/hooks/useStartConsultation.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/hooks/useStartConsultation.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/api/consultSession.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/session/ConsultationRoom.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/homepage/DoctorDashboardDrawer.tsx`

### ADR-20260810-01: `catalogVersion` drift on repeated demo-policy seeding broke every post-consult CDS read

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: Accepted — backend-only; not applied until `Deploy Dev` runs. Not qualified.
- Owner: Dev A (sole engineer)
- Context: Live testing on the post-consult workspace reported two failures that looked unrelated: clicking "Start fresh evaluation" returned `RED_FLAG_EVALUATION_FAILED` ("The server rejected this action. Refresh current state before retrying."), and saving a manual diagnosis entry ("Migraine") returned `STATE_CONFLICT` ("Authoritative state was refreshed; review it before retrying.").

  Direct inspection of the dev `app_core` table found one root cause behind both: the active policy's `ACTIVE.catalogVersion` (`demo-catalog-v1785867520686`) did not match the stored `diagnosis_catalog` component's own `content.catalogVersion` (`demo-catalog-v1785533067375`), even though both pointed at the same `catalogDigest`.

  `backend/scripts/seed-cds-demo-policy.ts` derived `catalogVersion` as `demo-catalog-v${Date.now()}` — a fresh string on every run. `catalogDigest` is computed from `entries` alone (`createImmutableDiagnosisCatalog` in `catalog-policy.ts`), and the script's `DEMO_CATALOG_ENTRIES` is a hardcoded constant that never changes between runs. So a second run of the script always produced the **same digest** with a **different version string**.

  `storePolicyComponent` treats a repeat write at an already-stored digest as a safe idempotent no-op — correct behavior for content-addressed storage, since the same digest is defined to mean the same content. But the component it declined to overwrite still carried the **first** run's version string, while `activatePolicyBundle`, a few lines later in the same script run, unconditionally recorded **this** run's fresh version string onto the environment's `ACTIVE` pointer. The two fields — one on the stored component, one on the activation record — silently diverged the moment the script was run a second time in the same environment.

  Every reader that loads the catalog checks those two fields for equality and fails closed on a mismatch, which is exactly what surfaced here: `loadAuthorities` in `candidate-service.ts` (the red-flag router's authority source — surfaced to the doctor as `RED_FLAG_EVALUATION_FAILED`) and `loadActiveCatalog` in `assessment-service.ts` (surfaced as `STATE_CONFLICT`, "Active catalog is unavailable"). Both checks are the gate correctly refusing to trust an inconsistent policy; the inconsistency was produced by this script, not by either reader.
- Decision:
  - **Derive `catalogVersion` deterministically from `DEMO_CATALOG_ENTRIES` itself**, not from a clock: `demo-catalog-${digestOf(DEMO_CATALOG_ENTRIES).slice('sha256:'.length, 'sha256:'.length + 16)}`. A re-run with unchanged entries now produces the same version **and** the same digest — genuinely idempotent, rather than idempotent in storage but not in the version string layered on top of it. A deliberate change to `DEMO_CATALOG_ENTRIES` still produces a new digest and a new version, exactly as before the fix.
  - **Document the failure mode in the script itself**, at the exact line that used to be wrong, since the defect was a coupling between two fields written in two different places (a stored component's content and an activation record) with nothing enforcing they agree — the kind of thing that is invisible in a diff of either write alone.
  - **Regression test placed beside the module the script depends on**, not beside the script: `backend/src/lib/cds/catalog-policy.seed-idempotency.test.ts`. `vitest.config.ts` excludes `scripts/` from both test discovery and coverage, so a test file beside the script itself would never run. The test mirrors the fixed derivation and exercises the real `createImmutableDiagnosisCatalog` contract the script depends on, including a case that reproduces the exact original defect shape (two different `Date.now()`-style versions agreeing on digest but not version) to document why the old approach was wrong, not just that the new one is right.
  - **No repair of the currently-broken live dev policy as a side effect of this fix.** The fix only prevents the drift on the next run of the script; it does not correct the mismatched revision already active in dev. Re-running the (now-fixed) seed script against dev would activate a new policy revision and is a data mutation requiring its own point-of-action authorization, not something to bundle into a code fix.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Make `storePolicyComponent` overwrite on a repeat digest instead of no-op: rejected. Content-addressed storage guaranteeing "same digest implies same content" is a broader invariant the rest of the CDS policy system relies on; weakening it to paper over this one caller's bug would trade a demo-script defect for a correctness guarantee used everywhere else.
  - Make `activatePolicyBundle` re-read the stored component's own `catalogVersion` instead of trusting the caller's value: considered, but rejected for this fix. That would mask the caller passing an inconsistent value rather than preventing the inconsistency from being computed in the first place, and `activatePolicyBundle` is a trusted administrative primitive used by more than this one script — changing its contract to defend against one caller's bug is a larger, unrelated change.
  - Keep `Date.now()` but cache it in a file so repeated runs reuse the same value: rejected. That fixes idempotency only within a single machine's script invocations and does nothing for the actual invariant that matters — version and digest must agree — while adding a stateful side file to a script that is otherwise purely additive against DynamoDB.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: re-running the demo seed script in the same environment with unchanged entries no longer drifts `ACTIVE.catalogVersion` away from the stored component's own version.
  - Positive: the four new tests pin both the fixed derivation and the original defect shape, so a future edit that reintroduces a clock-based version would fail the "same content across two builds" test immediately rather than surfacing three layers away as a doctor-facing `RED_FLAG_EVALUATION_FAILED`.
  - Trade-off: the live dev environment's policy remains in its currently-drifted state until someone explicitly re-runs the fixed seed script against dev — that is a deliberate scope boundary of this ADR, not an oversight, per the standing rule that data mutations need their own authorization separate from a code fix.
  - **Not qualified.** Backend typecheck, lint, and `npm test` (1031 tests across 83 files, 4 new) all pass with no regressions. No frontend, infrastructure, or Terraform change. This does not repair the live dev policy state, does not constitute staging qualification, and does not qualify either CDS model preset.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) decide whether to re-run `seed-cds-demo-policy.ts` against dev now that it is fixed — this activates a new policy revision and needs its own explicit authorization before executing; (b) once re-seeded, re-verify live that "Start fresh evaluation" and manual diagnosis entry both succeed; (c) consider whether `activatePolicyBundle` should assert the caller-supplied `catalogVersion` matches the stored component's own `content.catalogVersion` before writing the `ACTIVE` pointer, which would turn this entire class of drift into a loud failure at write time instead of a silent one caught only by readers much later.
- Related files: `backend/scripts/seed-cds-demo-policy.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/catalog-policy.seed-idempotency.test.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/catalog-policy.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/policy.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/candidate-service.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/assessment-service.ts`
- **Follow-up (2026-08-10, same day):** re-running the fixed script against dev to repair the live drift exposed that the fix above prevents FUTURE drift but does nothing for a component ALREADY stored under an old version at the same digest. `catalogDigest` depends only on `entries`/`aliasIndex`, never on `catalogVersion`, so the dev environment's `diagnosis_catalog` component was already stored under the original `demo-catalog-v1785533067375` from before this fix existed. Re-running with the deterministic derivation computed a *different* new version (`demo-catalog-3627b3061b1ded19`) at the *same* digest; `storePolicyComponent`'s immutability guard correctly no-op'd that write, so the stored component kept its old version while `activatePolicyBundle` recorded the new one on `ACTIVE` — the exact same class of drift, reproduced with a new value pair, confirmed live via direct DynamoDB reads immediately after the first re-run.

  Fix: added `getPolicyComponent` (`policy.ts`) — a strong read of what is already stored at a `(componentType, contentDigest)` pair — and `reconcileStoredCatalogVersion` (`catalog-policy.ts`), a pure function that adopts an already-stored version over a freshly computed one whenever both exist at the same digest, so a writer can never activate a version its own write would not durably persist. `seed-cds-demo-policy.ts` now reads back the existing component before activating and reconciles against it. 4 new tests in `catalog-policy.seed-idempotency.test.ts` pin this reconciliation, including the exact live-dev shape (a freshly-derived version colliding with an old Date.now()-based version at the same digest) and were confirmed load-bearing by temporarily short-circuiting `reconcileStoredCatalogVersion` to return its input unchanged, which failed 2 of the 4 new tests.

  Re-ran the corrected script against dev a second time: it logged adopting the already-stored `demo-catalog-v1785533067375`, activated `policyActivationRevision=4`, and a direct DynamoDB read confirmed `ACTIVE.catalogVersion`, `ACTIVE.catalogDigest`, and the stored component's own `content.catalogVersion`/`contentDigest` all agree. This is the mutation flagged as needing its own authorization in the original write-up of this ADR; the user explicitly authorized it for this specific operation before it was executed.
  - Not qualified. Backend typecheck, lint, and `npm test` (1035 tests across 83 files, 8 in the affected file) pass with no regressions. Live dev DynamoDB reads before and after the corrected re-run are the verification evidence for this follow-up; this does not constitute staging qualification.
  - Additional related files: `backend/src/lib/cds/policy.ts` (adds `getPolicyComponent`), `backend/src/lib/cds/catalog-policy.ts` (adds `reconcileStoredCatalogVersion`).

### ADR-20260810-02: A patient who stayed on the consultation room after "End consultation" was shown "hasn't started yet"

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: Accepted — frontend-only; not applied until the next Amplify build. Not qualified.
- Owner: Dev A (sole engineer)
- Context: Live testing found that after the doctor pressed "End consultation," the patient's screen did not change — it kept showing `NotStartedPanel`, "Consultation hasn't started yet," for a consultation that had, in fact, just ended.

  `GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/state` answers `409` in two genuinely different situations: before the consultation has started, and after it has ended — `CHAT_ENABLED_STATUSES` on the backend (`chat-access.ts`) is `['confirmed', 'in_progress']` and does not include `completed`. `fetchBookingConsultationState` maps both cases to `null` (ADR-20260806-01's design: a `409`/`404` here means "no session to join," not an error to surface). `ConsultationRoom` then had exactly one branch for a `null` read — `if (!stateQuery.data) return <NotStartedPanel />` — so it could not tell "not yet" from "already over," and always rendered the pre-consult waiting state.

  The booking-detail query the room already holds (`bookingQuery`, added to read `videoJoinUrl`) has no such gate: `GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}` answers regardless of consultation phase and its `status` field distinguishes `completed` from everything else. Nothing was reading it for that purpose.
- Decision:
  - **Check `bookingQuery.data?.status === "completed"` and redirect before the ambiguous `!stateQuery.data` branch is ever reached.** This uses a query the component already fetches; it does not add a new network call.
  - **`RedirectToBookingPanel`, a new component, performs the navigation via `router.replace` inside a `useEffect`** (not merely rendering a link) — the goal is for the patient not to have to notice and act on their own, since the entire point of the fix is that a completed consultation has nothing left to offer on this route. `router.replace` is used, not `router.push`, so the ended room is not left as a back-navigation target.
  - **A visible fallback link stays on screen underneath the effect**, in case the redirect is blocked or the effect does not fire (e.g. certain back-forward-cache restores) — the room should never show a dead end with nothing clickable.
  - **`CompletedStep` on the patient's own booking page is left untouched** and is where the patient lands; it already renders the correct next-step messaging (education/prescription cards, "your doctor is writing up their findings"). This route gets no equivalent messaging of its own because it has nothing left to say once redirected away.
  - **New test file `ConsultationRoom.test.tsx`** (none existed before): pins the redirect for a `completed` booking, the fallback link's presence, and that a non-`completed`, not-yet-started booking still renders the ordinary waiting panel rather than a redirect. Verified load-bearing by temporarily disabling the new branch, which failed the first two of the three tests.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Make the backend's `/state` route return a distinguishable status (e.g. a `410 Gone`, or a body flag) for the post-completion case instead of the same `409` it returns pre-consult: rejected for this fix. It is a reasonable API improvement but a larger, backend-touching change for a bug that the frontend can resolve with data it already has; revisit only if another caller needs the same distinction `/state` currently cannot make.
  - Read `session?.endedAt` instead of `booking.status`: rejected. `stateQuery.data` is `null` in exactly the case that needs distinguishing — there is no `session` object to read a field from once `/state` has started answering `409` again.
  - Add the redirect inside `NotStartedPanel` itself rather than gating before it: rejected. `NotStartedPanel` has no booking-status context passed to it and giving it one only to immediately redirect out of it is more indirection than checking the status at the top of the component, where `bookingQuery` already lives.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: a patient staying on the room after the doctor ends the consultation is now moved to their booking page automatically, where the actual post-consult messaging already exists.
  - Positive: the fix reuses an existing query rather than adding one, so there is no additional load on `GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}`.
  - Trade-off: the distinction still lives entirely in the frontend, reading two independently-polled queries (`stateQuery`, `bookingQuery`) to reconstruct a fact the backend's `/state` route cannot express on its own; a future consumer of `/state` alone (without also holding the booking) cannot make this same distinction without either route changes or its own instance of the same fallback.
  - **Not qualified.** Frontend typecheck and the new test file's 3 cases pass; full `npx vitest run` (699 tests across 103 files, 3 new) passes with no regressions. No backend or infrastructure change.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) re-verify live that a patient on the room screen is redirected within a few seconds of the doctor pressing End consultation; (b) consider whether `/state` should itself distinguish pre-consult from post-completion (e.g. a `410` for the latter) so this reconstruction is not needed by every future caller; (c) apply the same `bookingQuery`-first check pattern if any other route is found relying solely on `fetchBookingConsultationState`'s `null` return without also holding booking status.
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/session/ConsultationRoom.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/session/ConsultationRoom.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/api/consultSession.ts`, `backend/src/lib/chat-access.ts`

### ADR-20260810-03: The completed-booking redirect from ADR-20260810-02 hijacked the doctor's own completion navigation, and the patient's booking never polled

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: Accepted — frontend-only; not applied until the next Amplify build. Not qualified.
- Owner: Dev A (sole engineer)
- Context: Live testing of ADR-20260810-02's fix reported two new failures. Pressing "End consultation" as the doctor now redirected to the *patient's own booking page* instead of the post-consult workspace — a regression that had worked correctly before that fix. Separately, the patient side of the original bug this fix was meant to solve was still broken: the patient remained stuck on the consultation room after the doctor ended it.

  Both traced back to the same redirect, for two different reasons:

  1. **The doctor race.** `complete.mutate()`'s `onSuccess` both invalidates `["booking", bookingId]` and calls `router.push` to the post-consult workspace. The invalidated `bookingQuery` refetch is a light `GET` that can resolve, and re-render this component, before the heavier `router.push` navigation completes. That re-render hit ADR-20260810-02's unconditional `if (bookingQuery.data?.status === "completed") return <RedirectToBookingPanel>` check — which fires for the doctor too, since it reads nothing about who is viewing the room. `RedirectToBookingPanel`'s own `router.replace` to `/booking/getBooking/{bookingId}` then won the race against the doctor's intended `router.push`.
  2. **The patient never polling.** `bookingQuery` was written with `staleTime: 1000 * 60 * 5` and no `refetchInterval` — a one-shot read, refreshed only by cache invalidation. The doctor's own `invalidateQueries` call only touches the doctor's own browser tab; it has zero effect on the patient's independent session and query cache. So the patient's `bookingQuery.data.status` never updated to `completed` on its own, and ADR-20260810-02's redirect — correct in principle — never had a trigger to fire on the patient's side at all. This was not a new regression; it was the original bug's fix never actually reaching the patient in the first place, missed because the previous verification only exercised each branch with `getMock` answering once, never a change mid-session.
- Decision:
  - **Gate the redirect with a `dismissedCompletionRedirect` flag**, set to `true` inside the SAME `onSuccess` handler that calls `router.push`. The doctor's own completion flow is the only place this flag is set, so it does not affect the patient's independent read of the same booking.
  - **Poll `bookingQuery` at the same cadence as `stateQuery`** (`POLL_INTERVAL_MS`, 4s) until the booking reaches a terminal status (`completed` or `cancelled`), mirroring the existing pattern already used for `stateQuery`. This is what lets the patient's own client observe the doctor's completion without a manual refresh — the fix ADR-20260810-02 needed but did not include.
  - **Read `consultationId` from `bookingQuery.data`, not `stateQuery.data?.session`**, for the post-consult navigation target. `booking.consultationId` is written by the same `/start` call that creates the session, so it is available on the same timeline and does not depend on which of the two queries happens to resolve first — removing the ambiguity that made the redirect race possible to trigger by ordering. `stateQuery.data?.session?.consultationId` is kept as a fallback only.
  - **Two new tests**, verified load-bearing by independently reverting each fix: (a) a doctor-session test where `postMock` flips the mocked booking status to `completed` the moment `/complete` resolves, asserting `router.push` is called with the post-consult URL and `router.replace` is never called — reverting the `dismissedCompletionRedirect` guard alone made this fail; (b) a patient-session test where the mocked booking status changes mid-poll without any user action, asserting the redirect eventually fires — reverting the `bookingQuery` polling alone made this fail.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Have `complete.mutate()`'s `onSuccess` navigate BEFORE invalidating the booking query, hoping `router.push` always wins the race by ordering: rejected. Ordering two independent async operations without an explicit signal between them is exactly the kind of race that produced this bug in the first place; a boolean gate makes the doctor's intent explicit instead of relying on relative timing that could flip with any unrelated change to query or router internals.
  - Read `isAssignedDoctor` inside the redirect check and skip the redirect for the assigned doctor unconditionally: rejected. A doctor who reloads this room well after ending the consultation (not mid-completion) should see the same "this has ended" redirect a patient would — there is nothing left to do here either way. Gating on the mutation's own success, not on role, preserves that.
  - Poll `bookingQuery` unconditionally, forever, rather than stopping at a terminal status: rejected. A booking that will never again change status (`completed`, `cancelled`) has nothing left to poll for, and polling it forever wastes a request every 4 seconds on an idle client left on the tab.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the doctor's own completion flow reaches the post-consult workspace again, matching the behavior before ADR-20260810-02.
  - Positive: the patient side of ADR-20260810-02's original fix now actually works — a patient who stays on the room is redirected within one poll interval of the doctor ending the consultation, without a manual refresh.
  - Positive: the post-consult navigation target no longer depends on which of two independent queries resolves first.
  - Trade-off: `bookingQuery` now polls every 4 seconds for the lifetime of an active (non-terminal) consultation room, on top of `stateQuery`'s existing poll at the same cadence — two polls per interval per open room tab instead of one. Not qualified against load; acceptable for a demo-scale environment, revisit if either query is merged or the interval widened once usage grows.
  - **Not qualified.** Frontend typecheck and the full `npx vitest run` (701 tests across 103 files, 2 new) pass with no regressions. No backend or infrastructure change.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) re-verify live that the doctor's End consultation button reaches the post-consult workspace and the patient's room redirects within a few seconds without a manual refresh; (b) consider consolidating `stateQuery` and `bookingQuery` into a single poll, or having `/state` itself return booking status directly, so a room does not need two independently-polled queries to reconstruct one lifecycle; (c) audit other mutations in this codebase that both invalidate a query and navigate in the same `onSuccess` for the same race shape now fixed here twice (ADR-20260809-08's `usePatientBoard` key mismatch, ADR-20260809-09's stale truthiness check, and this ADR are three different mechanisms producing the same "two independent operations raced and the wrong one won" shape in three days).
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/session/ConsultationRoom.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/session/ConsultationRoom.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/api/consultSession.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/api/bookingDetail.ts`

### ADR-20260810-04: The post-consult workspace enforced PRD v3.2 correctly but had one catalogued diagnosis and spoke in contract enums

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: Accepted — backend catalog content + frontend copy; not applied until `Deploy Dev` and the next Amplify build. Not qualified. Catalog content is **not clinically approved**.
- Owner: Dev A (sole engineer)
- Context: Live physician testing of the post-consult workspace reported it as "buggy and unclear", with the specific question of whether the CDS pipeline follows `architecture/QA feedback v1.pdf`. That file is not a QA feedback log — it is **Master PRD v3.2**, "Post-Consult, Assessment-First, Harness-Only Implementation Baseline", 11 pages; the QA harness is its §10.

  Assessed against it, the **enforcement architecture complies** and was left alone: the global invariant (no confirmed Assessment → no Plan/Rx/final ICD/medcert/lab/imaging/patient education) is enforced server-side on all seven output types; §5.2's protected endpoint contract matches exactly (`consultation_id` + `assessment_version` + `physician_actor_id` + signed `gate_token`, with revocation and artifact staleness on Assessment edit); §4.2's no-visible-scores rule holds, with a test asserting no `data-rank` and no `/confidence|score|verdict|dosage/i` in the DOM; §4.3's CPG Preview is capped at three facts and three questions with a source citation and no treatment content; §9.1's red-flag router runs before candidate display and again before generation, suppressing routine candidates on `REFER_F2F`/`EMERGENCY`; §6.2 keeps LLMs out of the safety, eligibility, and citation paths entirely.

  What did not comply was the **content and the copy**, and together they made a compliant engine unusable:

  1. **One catalogued diagnosis of a specified 100.** §8 calls for 20 full solvers plus 80 reference-only cards. `seed-cds-demo-policy.ts` carried exactly one inline entry, `Upper respiratory tract infection`. Anything else a physician typed resolved through `resolveManualAlias` to `UNMAPPED_MANUAL_ELIGIBILITY` — `unmapped_manual`, empty `eligibleOutputTypes` — which disables all seven generation controls permanently regardless of tokens, acknowledgements, or refreshes. The physician typed **Migraine**, which §8.1 names explicitly as a first-20 full solver. That was the dead end, and it was the gate behaving exactly as §8 specifies.
  2. **The one entry's fact selectors did not exist.** It used `symptomDurationDays` and `severity`. `getCandidatePreview` resolves selectors as dot-paths into `snapshot.canonicalContent[].content`, whose only shape for a patient intake is `IntakeClinicalContent` — which has neither field. So the CPG Preview rendered zero support facts even for the single diagnosis that worked. Silent, because a missing selector `flatMap`s to nothing.
  3. **Raw contract enums shown to clinicians.** `assessment.lockReasons.join(", ")` put the literal `clinical_input_changed` on screen. No lock-reason lookup table existed anywhere in the frontend. Stale reasons and routing outcomes were `replaceAll("_", " ")`, giving "postflight failed" and "REFER F2F".
  4. **Unmapped error codes printed verbatim.** `physicianErrorMessage` handled eight codes; `RED_FLAG_EVALUATION_FAILED` and `STATE_CONFLICT` had no case and fell to a `default` that interpolated the code, producing the reported "The server rejected this action (RED_FLAG_EVALUATION_FAILED)".
  5. **No §4.1 state guidance.** §4.1 is normative about five doctor-facing states each with a defined "Doctor sees" and "System permits". The workspace rendered every control at all times, and a disabled row was the only signal anything was wrong.
  6. **The Assessment field violated §4.2.** That section says the control MUST initialize empty with placeholder `Type or select diagnosis...`, and §12.1 lists "Assessment starts blank" as traceable acceptance evidence. The field had no `placeholder` at all and pre-filled from `editableDiagnosis`. §4.2 also specifies that clicking the field opens intake-based suggestions; the physician had to find a separate "Start fresh evaluation" control.
- Decision:
  - **Seed every full solver legible in §8.1 (16 entries)**, in a new `backend/src/lib/cds/full-solver-catalog.ts` rather than inline in the seed script, because `vitest.config.ts` excludes `scripts/` from test discovery — as a library module the content is covered by `full-solver-catalog.test.ts`, which builds it through the real `createImmutableDiagnosisCatalog` validator.
  - **16, not 20, and the reason is recorded rather than papered over.** §8.1's table is visually **clipped in the source PDF**: the respiratory row ends mid-word at "acute bronc" and the GI/neurologic/GU/eye row ends at "; d". Text extraction confirms the truncation is in the document. Two editorial calls are recorded in code: "Viral URTI" and "acute nasopharyngitis" are one entry (same condition, and the prior seed already aliased them), and "Dry eye disease" is an **inference** for the trailing "; d", flagged in an exported `INFERRED_FROM_CLIPPED_SOURCE` constant. The remaining slots need Clinical Ops to supply from an unclipped §8.1 and were deliberately **not invented**.
  - **Every fact selector is asserted against the real intake projection.** `INTAKE_SELECTOR_VOCABULARY` enumerates `IntakeClinicalContent`'s top-level fields and a test fails on any selector outside it, so a field rename breaks a test instead of silently emptying previews. A dedicated case asserts `symptomDurationDays` and `severity` are absent from that vocabulary.
  - **Content is modelled as PRD §7.2 Tier 4 internal, labelled provisional in its own title**, because the physician reads that title in the CPG Preview. It deliberately does **not** cite DOH, FDA Philippines, PhilHealth, or specialty-society documents: fabricating an authoritative-looking citation no reviewer has bound is worse than an honestly provisional one. §8.2's promotion gate has **not** been run on any of it.
  - **Eligible outputs are scoped per diagnosis, not uniform.** `imaging_request` is granted to no entry (none of these presentations warrants routine imaging absent red flags, and red flags divert off this pathway); `lab_request` only to COVID-like illness and uncomplicated UTI; `prescription` is withheld from COVID-like illness because antiviral eligibility is not assessable here. Tests pin all three decisions so a later widening is deliberate.
  - **A new `cdsCopy.ts` maps every lock reason, stale reason, and routing outcome to clinical language with the next action named.** Clear preconditions come from the gating design's lock-ownership table: only `clinical_input_changed` and `evaluation_failed` are cleared by a fresh ROUTINE evaluation at token issuance, so only those two point at the re-check control. `operator_hold` and `recovery_required` are marked not self-clearable and say so, rather than implying a button will help.
  - **`physicianErrorMessage` names every code the CDS surface can return, and its `default` no longer interpolates `error.code`.** It also does not log: `assessmentFirst.security.test.ts` forbids logging calls anywhere in that module because everything there is adjacent to gate tokens and clinical content. An unmapped code is a gap in the switch, closed there.
  - **`stepGuidance` derives §4.1's state and renders one next-step line**, ordered so the most restrictive condition wins — an operator hold outranks a safety flag, which outranks the Assessment lifecycle. That ordering is what stops the workspace telling a physician to "choose an output" while a safety flag holds the gate.
  - **The Assessment field initializes empty with the §4.2 placeholder and loads suggestions on focus.** It is seeded only from a server-*confirmed* diagnosis, which §4.1 does show. Because it no longer pre-fills `editableDiagnosis`, a saved-but-unconfirmed diagnosis is surfaced as its own "Saved, not yet confirmed" line — otherwise "Confirm Assessment" would act on a value the physician cannot see.
  - **The zero-eligible-output state now explains itself.** "No protected output types" named the mechanism but neither the cause nor the way out; it now says the diagnosis is not in the approved catalogue, that the Assessment is still recorded, and what to do instead.
  - **"Request current-state token" is relabelled "Re-check safety and unlock"**, because issuing a token runs the fresh deterministic evaluation that also clears the two recoverable locks — so the label names the effect a physician staring at those locks needs to find.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Grant `unmapped_manual` a default output set so typing anything enables drafting: **rejected outright.** That inverts §8's entire premise, that only reviewed published full-solver cards unlock disease-specific drafting, and would let the model draft a prescription for a diagnosis no reviewer has ever seen. The dead end was the gate working; the fix is catalogue content, not a weaker gate.
  - Invent the three illegible §8.1 entries from clinical plausibility: rejected. Inventing catalogue entries that silently gain prescription eligibility is precisely the failure §8.2's promotion gate exists to prevent.
  - Cite real DOH/specialty-society CPGs for the provisional cards to look authoritative: rejected, and worth stating plainly — an unverified citation bound to generated clinical guidance is a patient-safety defect, not a cosmetic one. The prior single entry did exactly this (`DOH Clinical Practice Guideline: Acute Upper Respiratory Tract Infection`, `demo-approval-doh-urti-2022`) and that citation is now gone.
  - Loosen `assessmentFirst.security.test.ts` to allow a `console.warn` for unmapped codes: rejected. The guard is correct; the module sits next to gate tokens and clinical content. (Two iterations were needed because its regex matches the literal `console.` even inside a comment, including in prose like "a systems console." — the comment was reworded rather than the test weakened.)
  - Keep `physicianErrorMessage`'s existing terse strings: rejected for the mapped ones but with one carve-out — "No prior preview is shown" was preserved verbatim in the `CPG_PREVIEW_UNAVAILABLE` message, because it is a clinical safety statement (no stale summary is standing in for the one that failed) rather than filler, and an existing test correctly pins it.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: 16 diagnoses can now reach protected generation instead of 1, including the migraine case that was reported. Aliases cover common Filipino and colloquial terms (`sipon`, `trangkaso`, `LBM`, `pantal`, `sakit sa likod`, `pilay`, `hives`, `URTI`, `UTI`).
  - Positive: the CPG Preview will actually populate support facts, for the first time — the previous selectors resolved to nothing on every consultation.
  - Positive: no lock reason, stale reason, routing outcome, or error code renders as a raw enum to a clinician; each states what happened and what to do next.
  - Positive: §4.2's blank-initialisation and placeholder requirements now hold, closing a §12.1 traceability item that was failing.
  - **Trade-off, and the important one: the catalogue content is not clinically approved.** §8.2's promotion gate — complete full-solver fields, bound published sources, clinical reviewer approval of red flags and differentials, compliance/privacy approval, passing golden cases, release-owner publication — has been run on **none** of these 16 entries. They are structurally valid and honestly labelled provisional, and they now gate real prescription and medical-certificate drafting. A physician must review them before this is shown to anyone outside a controlled demo.
  - Trade-off: `ApprovedPreviewSourceMetadata` forces `reviewStatus: 'reviewed'`, so the UI prints "Reviewed" for provisional content. The honesty lives in the source title until the schema grows a provisional tier. This is a real mismatch, recorded rather than hidden.
  - Trade-off: §8.1's three illegible entries remain unimplemented, so the catalogue is 16 of a specified 20 full solvers and 0 of 80 reference-only cards.
  - Unchanged and still failing §10: the QA harness has 40 synthetic intake cases against a threshold of ≥500 before internal physician pilot and 1,000 before broader clinical pilot. Nothing in this change moves that.
  - **Not qualified.** Backend typecheck, lint, and `npm test` (1059 tests / 84 files, 24 new) pass. Frontend typecheck, lint (0 errors, 52 pre-existing warnings unchanged), and `npx vitest run` (729 tests / 104 files, 28 new) pass. The blank-field/placeholder fix and the lock-copy fix were each verified load-bearing by independent revert. No infrastructure or Terraform change. This is not staging qualification, not clinical approval, and not provider qualification.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) **clinical review of all 16 entries** against §8.2 before any non-demo use, starting with the eligible-output sets that gate prescriptions; (b) obtain an unclipped §8.1 and add the three missing full solvers, confirming or removing the inferred "Dry eye disease"; (c) bind each Tier 4 card to a real upstream published source and replace `UPSTREAM_BINDING_PENDING`; (d) extend the QA pack from 40 toward §10's 500; (e) consider a provisional review tier in `ApprovedPreviewSourceMetadata` so the UI stops printing "Reviewed" for unreviewed cards; (f) begin the 80 reference-only knowledge cards, which appear in the picker and preview without unlocking drafting.
- Related files: `backend/src/lib/cds/full-solver-catalog.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/full-solver-catalog.test.ts`, `backend/scripts/seed-cds-demo-policy.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/catalog-policy.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/intake-clinical-input.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/candidate-service.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/cdsCopy.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/cdsCopy.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/api/assessmentFirst.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/AssessmentFirstWorkspace.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/AssessmentFirstWorkspace.test.tsx`, `architecture/QA feedback v1.pdf` (Master PRD v3.2)

### ADR-20260810-05: Released documents never appeared for a waiting patient, and releasing a Plan reached nobody

- Date: 2026-08-10
- Status: Accepted — frontend-only; not applied until the next Amplify build. Not qualified.
- Owner: Dev A (sole engineer)
- Context: With ADR-20260810-04 deployed, the physician could generate, review, sign, and release documents end to end. Live testing then asked the obvious next question — where does the *patient* see any of this — and the honest answer was "on their booking page, eventually, if they reload".

  The patient surface itself was already correct and already wired: `PatientBookingDetail` mounts `PatientEducationCard` and `PrescriptionCard` unconditionally, each reads its own patient-readable route, and each renders nothing until something is released. ADR-20260810-02/03 already redirect the patient there when the consultation ends. Three things undermined it:

  1. **Neither released-artifact query polled.** Both used `staleTime: 1000 * 60 * 5` with no `refetchInterval`, and the global query client sets `refetchOnWindowFocus: false`. The booking query polls every 30s for status, but that refetch does not invalidate the artifact queries. So a patient sitting on the page when the physician pressed Release saw nothing for up to five minutes, and in practice until they manually reloaded.
  2. **Release is entirely silent server-side.** `ProtectedArtifactLifecycle.commitRelease` is a four-item transaction — artifact update, `CDS#OUTPUT_CURRENT` pointer, reservation terminal, audit `Put` — and none of them notify anyone. There is no `prescription_released` template in `NOTIFICATION_TEMPLATES`, and the one `patient_education` template that exists is retired: `dispatchNotification` throws `LegacyProtectedWriterRetiredError` for it. So nothing pushed, and nothing pulled.

     Together those two meant `CompletedStep`'s promise — "Anything your doctor shares appears here" — was not backed by any mechanism at all.
  3. **Releasing a Plan reached nobody, and the workspace gave no hint.** `PATIENT_READABLE_OUTPUT_TYPES` is exactly `['patient_education', 'prescription']`, and `infra/modules/cds_operations/routes.tf` exposes exactly two patient routes to match. There is no `/plan/released`, nor one for `final_icd`, `medical_certificate`, `lab_request`, or `imaging_request` — excluded deliberately, because each "needs its own decision about presentation and accompanying explanation before it is handed to a patient unmediated". But the workspace offered seven identical Generate controls and an identical Release control on all of them. In testing the physician released Plan, Prescription, and Patient education and reasonably expected all three to land. Two did.
- Decision:
  - **Poll the two released-artifact queries, scoped to when a release can happen.** `RELEASED_ARTIFACT_POLL_MS = 15_000` and `shouldPollReleasedArtifacts(status)` live in a new `lib/releasedArtifacts.ts` so both cards share one value without importing from each other. `PatientBookingDetail` passes `poll` only for `in_progress` and `completed`, so an old booking left open in a tab does not poll indefinitely. `staleTime` drops to 0 while polling, since a five-minute stale window would defeat the interval it sits behind.
  - **15s, and explicitly a stopgap.** It is the same order of magnitude as the existing 30s booking poll and costs two lightweight GETs per interval. The comment in `releasedArtifacts.ts` records that this exists *because* release enqueues no notification, so whoever adds that notification knows this can go.
  - **No new patient route, and specifically not `/plan/released`.** Considered and rejected for this change — see alternatives. The patient already sees the two artifacts that are meant for them.
  - **Mark patient visibility throughout the doctor's workspace.** A `PATIENT_READABLE_OUTPUT_TYPES` list in `cdsCopy.ts`, documented as needing to stay in lockstep with the backend constant and the Terraform route keys and pinned by a test, drives: a per-artifact badge ("Patient can see this" / "Your records only"), the release button label ("Release to patient" / "Release for records"), the confirmation prompt, the post-release line ("Shared with the patient … on their booking page now" / "Released to your records … the patient has no screen for this document type"), and a section subtitle naming the two types that reach the patient.
  - **The record-only wording avoids implying secrecy.** These artifacts are not withheld from the patient; they are not yet presentable to them, which is a different thing and the actual reason the route does not exist. The Plan's copy also points at Patient education as the patient-facing version of it, so a physician who wanted the patient to have guidance is told how.
  - **Renamed "Current protected outputs" → "Drafted documents"**, continuing ADR-20260810-04's removal of protocol vocabulary from a clinical surface.
- Alternatives considered:
  - **Add `plan` to `PATIENT_READABLE_OUTPUT_TYPES` and a third patient route.** Mechanically cheap — `handleReleasedPatientArtifact` is already parameterised by output type, so it is one handler case, one contract path, one Terraform route key, one API client, one card. Rejected for this change on product grounds, not effort: PRD v3.2 §9.3 makes patient education the patient-facing artifact derived from the approved Plan, which is precisely why the two exist separately. Shipping the raw Plan JSON to a patient would duplicate that channel with something written for a clinician, and the excluded-type comment's "own decision about presentation" is a real decision that has not been made. The demo does not need it: the patient already sees education and a prescription.
  - **Invalidate the artifact queries from the booking query's `onSuccess` instead of polling.** Rejected: booking status does not change when an artifact is released — it is already `completed` — so there is no status transition to hang the invalidation on. Only a poll or a real notification can observe it.
  - **Poll unconditionally rather than gating on status.** Rejected. Nothing can be released before an Assessment is confirmed, so polling a `pending_payment` booking is pure waste on a page patients sit on while paying.
  - **Add the release notification now instead of polling.** The correct long-term fix, and deliberately not bundled: it needs a new template in a retired-template neighbourhood, SES/SNS delivery, and its own dedup/idempotency decisions. Polling is a self-contained frontend change that makes the demo honest; the notification remains open follow-up work.
  - **Hide the Release control entirely for record-only output types.** Rejected. Finalising and releasing are the audit-bearing lifecycle for every artifact regardless of who reads it, and suppressing the control would leave those five stuck at `finalized` with no way forward.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: a patient sitting on their booking page now sees released education and a prescription appear within ~15s, with no reload and no action. `CompletedStep`'s promise is finally true.
  - Positive: a physician can tell, before releasing, whether the patient will see the document, and afterwards whether they did. The Plan case says so plainly and names the artifact that does reach the patient.
  - Positive: the visibility list is pinned by a test against the backend constant and Terraform route keys, so adding a patient route without updating the frontend list (or vice versa) fails.
  - Trade-off: two extra GETs per 15s per open booking page while `in_progress` or `completed`. Bounded and small, but it is polling standing in for a notification, and it should be removed when that notification exists.
  - Trade-off, unresolved and deliberately out of scope: the **chat contradiction from the previous investigation still stands.** `JoinConsultationCard` is gated to `confirmed`/`in_progress`, so the chat entry disappears on `completed`, while `CompletedStep` says "Your chat history stays available either way" and the prescription card twice tells the patient to message their doctor in the chat. `CHAT_ENABLED_STATUSES` on the backend also excludes `completed`, so the server refuses it too. Fixing it either extends a backend gate or retracts three user-facing promises, and that choice was explicitly deferred pending direction.
  - Trade-off: the five record-only output types still have no patient presentation. That is the status quo, now merely visible instead of silent.
  - **Not qualified.** Frontend typecheck, lint (0 errors, 52 pre-existing warnings unchanged), and `npx vitest run` (739 tests / 104 files, 10 new) pass. The polling fix was confirmed load-bearing by reverting `refetchInterval` alone, which failed the release-appears test. No backend, contract, or infrastructure change — the two patient routes and the release transaction are untouched.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) add a release notification (new template, SES/SNS delivery, dedup) and then reconsider whether this poll is still needed; (b) resolve the chat contradiction one way or the other; (c) decide the presentation question for `plan`, `medical_certificate`, and the request types before any of them is shown to a patient — the medical certificate is the most likely next one a patient will ask for; (d) consider a patient-visible "your doctor is preparing documents" affordance if the wait proves long in practice, weighed against the existing deliberate choice to render nothing rather than an empty state.
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/releasedArtifacts.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/patientEducation/PatientEducationCard.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/patientEducation/PatientEducationCard.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/prescription/PrescriptionCard.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/PatientBookingDetail.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/cdsCopy.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/cdsCopy.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/AssessmentFirstWorkspace.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/AssessmentFirstWorkspace.test.tsx`, `backend/src/lib/cds/released-patient-education.ts` (read-only), `backend/src/lib/cds/protected-artifact-lifecycle.ts` (read-only), `infra/modules/cds_operations/routes.tf` (read-only)

### ADR-20260817-01: Every push to main paid for two Amplify builds, ~60% of them byte-identical, and red commits deployed anyway

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Status: Accepted — repository-side implementation complete. **Applied to `dev` on 2026-08-18**; `staging` and `prod` remain definitions. Not qualified.
  - Superseded in part by **ADR-20260818-01**, which found the diff gate inert as shipped and corrected it. Read the two together: the decisions below stand, but the gate's job-selection filter and the `--commit-id` claim were wrong and are fixed there.
  - Applied-state correction (2026-08-18, read-only verification): the original status line read "`enable_auto_build = false` has not been applied to any environment." That is no longer true. `aws amplify get-app --app-id d27420akzuajw` and `get-branch --branch-name main` both report `enableBranchAutoBuild`/`enableAutoBuild` = `false`, so dev **is** applied. `aws amplify list-apps` returns exactly one app, `bayanhealth-dev-frontend`, so staging and prod have no Amplify app to apply to — those two remain definitions because the environments were never stood up, not because the change was withheld.
- Owner: Dev A (sole engineer)
- Context: A cost review of the delivery pipeline against ADR-20260629-01 (pre-production cost optimization) found the CI/CD path itself was never included in that pass. ADR-20260629-01 right-sized runtime spend — CDS fallback de-dup, the opt-in inference cache, log/S3/Lambda sizing — but the build pipeline that runs on all 123 monthly pushes to `main` was untouched. Four distinct problems, all of them structural rather than tuning:

  1. **Two builds per push.** `infra/modules/amplify_hosting` set `enable_branch_auto_build` and the branch's `enable_auto_build` from `var.enable_auto_build`, which defaults to `true` and was not overridden by any of the three environment roots. So Amplify's own webhook started a build on every push, *and* `deploy-dev.yml` called `aws amplify start-job`. The workflow already contained an elaborate `LimitExceededException` branch — roughly 45 lines that listed active jobs, adopted a webhook-started job when its `commitId` matched, and otherwise polled the older job to completion before starting its own — which is best read as evidence of the collision rather than a fix for it. `infra/README.md` recorded two consecutive dev run failures (`29801207554`, `29814984966`) caused by syntax and `sort_by` errors *inside that branch*, so the duplicate-build workaround had itself been a source of pipeline outages.
  2. **Rebuilding commits with no frontend input.** `start-job` was unconditional. Of 123 monthly commits, 72 (~60%) touch nothing under `frontend/`, `amplify.yml`, or `contracts/openapi.yaml`, so their build output is byte-identical to the one already deployed. Backend-only and docs-only commits each paid a full `npm ci` + `next build` to redeploy the same artifact.
  3. **Deploying commits CI had not validated.** `deploy-dev.yml` triggered on `push: main`, the same event as `ci.yml`, so the two ran *concurrently*. A commit that failed typecheck, lint, or tests still ran `terraform apply` against dev and still burned an Amplify build; CI turning red afterwards changed nothing. There was no ordering relationship between validation and deployment at all.
  4. **A cache that could not possibly hit.** `amplify.yml` listed `node_modules/**/*` in `cache.paths` while `preBuild` ran `npm ci`, whose documented behaviour is to delete `node_modules` and reinstall from the lockfile. The cache was therefore restored and immediately discarded on every build, paying restore cost for zero benefit. `.next/cache` was cached correctly, but it also sits inside `artifacts.baseDirectory: .next` with `files: ['**/*']`, so the webpack cache was additionally shipped in the deployment artifact and billed as recurring hosting storage.

  Two smaller items in the same pass: `ci.yml` had no `concurrency` block, so stacked pushes to a pull request ran every superseded commit to completion; and its "Cache Lambda artifacts" step had no `cache-hit` guard, so `package:lambdas` re-ran even on a hit — a guard both deploy workflows already had.

- Decision:
  - **Disable Amplify auto-build; the workflow is the sole trigger.** `enable_auto_build = false` is set explicitly on the `amplify_hosting` module call in all three environment roots. The `var.enable_auto_build` default stays `true` so a future environment can opt back in without a module change. The alternative — dropping `start-job` and letting the webhook build — was considered and rejected (see alternatives).
  - **Delete the `LimitExceededException` adoption branch.** With auto-build off, no foreign job exists to adopt. What remains is ordinary API throttling and the genuine limit hit when a manual dispatch overlaps a scheduled run, so it is now a bounded 3-attempt retry with 20s/40s backoff, matching only `LimitExceededException|ThrottlingException|TooManyRequestsException` and failing immediately on anything else.
  - **Gate the Amplify release on a diff against the last *deployed* commit.** A new step resolves the most recent `SUCCEED` + `RELEASE` job's `commitId` from `aws amplify list-jobs` and builds only when `git diff --name-only "$LAST_SHA...HEAD"` touches `frontend/`, `amplify.yml`, or `contracts/openapi.yaml`. `contracts/openapi.yaml` is watched because it generates frontend types.
  - **Diff against the last deployed commit, not `HEAD~1`.** A run of backend-only commits would otherwise let a genuine frontend change fall outside a one-commit window and never deploy. Anchoring on what Amplify actually released makes the comparison self-correcting: a skipped build leaves `LAST_SHA` where it was, so the next frontend change still sees the full delta.
  - **The gate fails OPEN in every ambiguous case,** each with a distinct logged reason: no prior succeeded RELEASE job, `list-jobs` erroring, the reported commit absent from the clone (`git cat-file -e`), or present but not an ancestor of `HEAD` after a force-push or rebase (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`). The asymmetry is deliberate: a needless build costs a few minutes, while a wrongly skipped build silently leaves stale frontend code deployed behind a green run. `list-jobs` is paginated, bounded to 5 pages, so a long job history cannot hang the run.
  - **`fetch-depth: 0` on checkout,** because both the diff and the ancestry test are impossible on Actions' default shallow clone.
  - **Gate deployment on CI success via `workflow_run`.** `deploy-dev.yml` now triggers on `workflow_run` for `CI` completing on `main`, with a job-level `if` requiring `conclusion == 'success'` and `head_branch == 'main'`. `workflow_dispatch` is retained and deliberately bypasses the gate. `workflow_run` fires on *completion regardless of outcome*, so the `conclusion` check is what does the work, not the trigger.
  - **Deploy `github.event.workflow_run.head_sha`, not `github.sha`.** Exposed once as a `DEPLOY_SHA` env var and used for checkout `ref`, the Amplify `--commit-id`, and log lines. Under `workflow_run`, `github.sha` resolves to the default-branch tip, which may already have moved past the commit CI validated — deploying it would deploy something no CI run ever checked.
  - **Keep the early `start-job`.** The build still starts before seeded-account verification and the smoke test so it overlaps them rather than adding idle polling minutes, which was the original reason for that ordering.
  - **`concurrency` per branch on the deploy, `cancel-in-progress: false`.** A half-applied Terraform run is worse than a queued one. Keyed on `head_branch` so a manual dispatch elsewhere does not queue behind `main`.
  - **`concurrency` on CI with `cancel-in-progress` scoped to pull requests only** (`${{ github.event_name == 'pull_request' }}`). Main-branch runs must never be superseded, because `deploy-dev.yml` now gates on them — cancelling one would silently skip a deploy.
  - **Make the Amplify cache real.** `node_modules/**/*` removed; `npm_config_cache=.npm-cache` set inline on the install and `.npm-cache/**/*` cached instead; install becomes `npm ci --prefer-offline --no-audit --fund=false`, where `--prefer-offline` is what converts the restored cache into an actual speedup. `.next/cache` is retained, and `'!cache/**/*'` is added to `artifacts.files` so the webpack cache stops being shipped as a deployment artifact.
  - **`.npm-cache` inside `appRoot`, not `$HOME/.npm`.** Amplify resolves monorepo `cache.paths` relative to `appRoot`, so a home-relative path is not reliably captured. A path under `appRoot` is unambiguous and is exactly what `cache.paths` restores. This was the documented decision point on Amplify cache-path semantics; it was resolved by choosing the form that does not depend on the ambiguous behaviour rather than by testing which one Amplify tolerates.
  - **Remove the `.env.production` heredoc.** Amplify injects the five `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` variables into the build shell, Next.js inlines them from `process.env` at build time, and `validate-env.mjs` reads `process.env` directly. Verified first: all five are present in `local.frontend_environment_variables` and applied to both `aws_amplify_app` and `aws_amplify_branch` in all three environments, matching `REQUIRED_ENV_VARS` exactly. No Terraform variable had to be added. The guard's behaviour is unchanged, since an unset variable yields an empty heredoc value and an empty `process.env` value alike.
  - **Collapse the redundant test steps.** `test:property`, `test:integration`, `test:handler`, `test:legacy-bypass`, and `test:cds-security` were verified to reference 20 distinct files, every one under `src/` and ending `.test.ts`, therefore all inside vitest's `src/**/*.test.ts` include glob that `test:unit` already runs across 84 files. Two are narrower than subsets, filtering by `--testNamePattern`. All five steps are removed from `ci.yml`; **the npm scripts are retained** for targeted local runs and for generating Task 10.3 security evidence. The three `tsx`-based tool steps (`test:cds-safety-regression`, `test:cds-resilience-campaign`, `cds:retirement-manifest`) are *not* vitest files and are kept.
  - **`ci.yml` becomes the sole writer of the `lambda-dist-*` cache key;** both deploy workflows switch to `actions/cache/restore` (read-only), removing the save race in which two workflows wrote the same key on every main push. `ci.yml` also gains the `cache-hit` guard on `package:lambdas` that the deploy workflows already had.
  - **No Terraform provider cache, and the matrix is left alone.** `TF_PLUGIN_CACHE_DIR` was implemented and then reverted after testing proved it would break CI. The committed `infra/environments/*/.terraform.lock.hcl` files contain **no `linux_amd64` `h1:` hash**: running `terraform providers lock -platform=linux_amd64` against a scratch copy of each environment added one hash per provider, confirming the gap. CI's `terraform init` succeeds today because a registry download is verified against the platform-independent `zh:` zip hashes; a plugin-cache install is verified against the per-platform `h1:` *directory* hash instead, so the cache would have populated successfully on the first run and then failed checksum verification on every subsequent one. Collapsing the matrix was also reconsidered and rejected as a substitute, because it does not actually solve the stated problem — each environment directory runs its own `terraform init` and downloads providers independently regardless of whether the three run in one job or three, so collapsing saves runner and checkout overhead but not the provider downloads. The genuine fix is committing cross-platform lock hashes, which is a dependency-verification change rather than a CI tweak and is deliberately left as follow-up. `TF_PLUGIN_CACHE_MAY_BREAK_DEPENDENCY_LOCK_FILE` was rejected outright: it works by weakening checksum verification.
  - **Cache npm's store in the contract job instead of adding a `@redocly/cli` devDependency.** There is no root `package.json`, so `cache: npm` on `setup-node` cannot be used — it requires a lockfile — which is noted inline. Adding redocly to `backend/package.json` would couple contract linting to the backend dependency tree and force a full backend `npm ci` in a job that otherwise installs nothing, so `~/.npm` is cached keyed on the pinned `1.34.0` and `npm_config_prefer_offline` is set.
  - **Remove the `custom_rule`.** `/<*>` → `/index.html` with `404-200` is the static-SPA fallback, where a client-side router resolves the path. This app is `platform = "WEB_COMPUTE"` with `framework = "Next.js - SSR"` and no `output: "export"`, so the build emits no `/index.html` and routing belongs to the SSR compute tier — the rule's target did not exist. Its only observable effect was converting genuine 404s into HTTP 200s, hiding missing routes from crawlers and monitoring. There is no `not-found.tsx`, `error.tsx`, or `global-error.tsx` in `src/app`, so Next's built-in 404 is what should serve.
  - **Delete two unreferenced binaries,** `public/authPlaceholderImage2.jpeg` (2.2 MB) and `public/TeleconsultSample.mp3` (2.7 MB), confirmed to have zero references via workspace grep, `git grep`, and a widened search for dynamic construction, `url()`, and `next/image` props. `AudioTrail`'s sole call site renders `<AudioTrail />` with no `audioSrc`, and the component's own comment states it renders an empty state "rather than playing a demo file" — the mp3 was already deliberately orphaned.
- Alternatives considered:
  - **Drop `start-job` and let the Amplify webhook build, polling the webhook's job instead.** Rejected. The workflow needs a deterministic job ID to poll for `SUCCEED` and to fail the run on `FAILED`, and a webhook-started job has to be *discovered* by listing jobs and matching on `commitId` — which is precisely the fragile adoption logic this ADR deletes, and which `infra/README.md` records as the cause of two dev run failures. It would also reintroduce a race between the webhook build and `terraform apply`, with no way to gate the build on CI. The workflow already resolves `amplify_app_id` and `amplify_branch_name` from Terraform outputs, so `start-job` costs nothing extra.
  - **Gate the whole deploy job on the frontend diff, not just the Amplify steps.** Rejected: `terraform apply` must still run for infra-only commits, which are exactly the commits the frontend gate skips. The backend smoke test is kept unconditional for the same reason — it exercises the backend Terraform just applied, so it matters *most* on the runs where no frontend build happens.
  - **Make `test:unit` exclude the subset files so the named steps could stay as evidence gates.** Rejected, and it was the explicitly offered alternative. It would make plain `vitest run` — and therefore `npm test` — stop covering 20 files, so a developer running the default command locally would silently skip the CDS security and legacy-bypass suites. Trading a correct default for CI step naming is the wrong direction, particularly as the actual evidence artifacts are `CDS_SECURITY_VALIDATION_TASK_10_3.md` and `backend/reports/*.json`, not a step label. The retained npm scripts remain the evidence-generation path.
  - **Collapse the Terraform matrix into a single job looping the environment directories.** A legitimate option that would share one provider download without any cache configuration. Rejected in favour of the plugin cache because collapsing serialises three validations that currently run in parallel and merges three independent results into one pass/fail, losing the per-environment attribution `fail-fast: false` exists to provide.
  - **Keep `.env.production` as belt-and-braces.** Rejected: it duplicates variables Amplify already injects, and a second source for build configuration is a place for the two to disagree. The env-var parity check was run first precisely so removal would not be a guess.
  - **Delete `public/authPlaceholderImage.jpeg` (153 KB) in the same pass.** It is also unreferenced, but it was outside the authorized deletion scope, so it is left in place and recorded as follow-up.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: one Amplify build per push instead of two, and the ~45-line collision branch that caused two pipeline outages is gone rather than merely bypassed.
  - Positive: the ~72 of 123 monthly commits that touch no frontend input cost zero Amplify build minutes.
  - Positive: a commit that fails CI performs no `terraform apply` and starts no Amplify build, so a red commit costs no AWS spend.
  - Positive: the Amplify cache does real work for the first time — `--prefer-offline` against a genuinely restorable npm store — and `.next/cache` stops being billed as recurring hosting storage.
  - Positive: superseded pull-request CI runs are cancelled; `package:lambdas` is skipped on a cache hit; the redocly CLI is no longer re-downloaded per run; ~1 minute of duplicate test execution per CI run removed.
  - Trade-off: Terraform providers are still re-downloaded by all three matrix legs on every run. This was the one intended optimization in this ADR that could not be delivered, and the reason is a pre-existing gap in the lock files rather than anything about the pipeline.
  - Discovered, unresolved: the lock files carry no `linux_amd64` hash and also lacked `darwin_arm64`, which means provider verification currently depends entirely on `zh:` registry hashes. That is functional but it blocks any plugin-cache use and makes local Terraform runs on an Apple Silicon machine unable to use a shared cache either.
  - Positive: 4.9 MB of unreferenced binaries removed from the repository and from every build.
  - Trade-off: **deploys are now strictly serialised behind CI**, adding CI's wall-clock duration to time-to-deploy. Previously the two overlapped. This is the intended cost of not deploying unvalidated commits, and `workflow_dispatch` remains as the manual bypass.
  - Trade-off: `workflow_run` workflows only trigger from the version of the file on the default branch, so changes to this trigger cannot be tested on a branch — a known and awkward property of the event.
  - Trade-off: the diff gate's fail-open design means a misconfiguration tends toward *extra* builds, not missing ones. Correct for safety, but it also means a silently broken gate looks like the old behaviour and would not announce itself; the `::notice::` lines exist so the reason is always in the log.
  - Trade-off: staging's gate is bypassable via a new `force_build` dispatch input, added because a manual staging deploy may intentionally want a rebuild. Nothing enforces that an operator reaches for it correctly.
  - **Not qualified, and specifically unapplied.** `terraform fmt -check -recursive infra` passes and `terraform init -backend=false && terraform validate` passes for `dev`, `staging`, and `prod` (pre-existing `hash_key`/`range_key` deprecation warnings unchanged). `npm run test:unit` passes locally. **`terraform plan` was not run**: neither Terraform nor the AWS CLI was installed in this environment and no AWS credentials are available, so the plan could not be produced and the stated acceptance criterion — that the plan shows only `enable_auto_build` flipping `true → false` — is **unverified**. The `custom_rule` removal is an additional in-place change to `aws_amplify_app` that will appear in that same plan and must be reviewed with it. Three behaviours cannot be verified from the repository and require the first real dev run: Amplify's acceptance of the `'!cache/**/*'` artifact exclusion, `start-job --commit-id` honouring an explicit SHA, and the build succeeding without `.env.production`.
  - Follow-up actions: (0) commit cross-platform provider lock hashes via `terraform providers lock -platform=linux_amd64 -platform=darwin_arm64` in each of `dev`, `staging`, `prod` (and `bootstrap`), then reinstate `TF_PLUGIN_CACHE_DIR` in the CI Terraform matrix; (a) run `terraform plan` for dev and confirm it contains only the `enable_auto_build` flip and the `custom_rule` removal before applying; (b) on the first dev deploy after apply, confirm the frontend build still succeeds without the heredoc and that Amplify accepted the artifact exclusion — revert the exclusion and document instead if it did not; (c) confirm the 404 behaviour change from removing `custom_rule` is acceptable on dev before it reaches staging or prod; (d) delete `public/authPlaceholderImage.jpeg` if it is genuinely unwanted; (e) `public/authPlaceholderImage3.jpeg` is 2.0 MB and *is* in use behind `next/image` — optimizing it is separate, unstarted work; (f) revisit whether root-scoped `force-dynamic` can be narrowed, which is blocked on the nonce-based CSP in `src/proxy.ts` and is not a build-pipeline change (see below).
- Notes: A `force-dynamic` scoping review was requested as a memo only and produced no change. It appears exactly once, in `src/app/layout.tsx`, and is load-bearing: `proxy.ts` mints a per-request CSP nonce under `strict-dynamic`, a statically prerendered page cannot carry one, and the layout comment records the resulting incident — `/signIn` served 30 scripts with 0 nonced and sign-in broke, while dynamic `/intake/[token]` served 26 of 27 and worked. `src/proxy.test.ts` enforces the coupling. Of 28 pages only 4 are even theoretical prerender candidates, two of which are the pages that broke; 13 are already `"use client"`. Narrowing it requires changing the CSP strategy first and is not worth 4 low-traffic routes.
- Related files: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/deploy-dev.yml`, `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml`, `amplify.yml`, `infra/modules/amplify_hosting/main.tf`, `infra/environments/dev/main.tf`, `infra/environments/staging/main.tf`, `infra/environments/prod/main.tf`, `infra/README.md`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/public/authPlaceholderImage2.jpeg` (deleted), `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/public/TeleconsultSample.mp3` (deleted), `backend/vitest.config.ts` (read-only), `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/scripts/validate-env.mjs` (read-only), `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/layout.tsx` (read-only)

### ADR-20260817-02: A second CI/CD pass — the Lambda artifact cache was measurably net-negative, every Lambda redeployed on every apply, and no job had a timeout

- Date: 2026-08-17
- Status: Accepted — repository-side implementation complete, and now validated with Terraform and the AWS CLI actually installed: `terraform fmt -check -recursive infra` passes and `terraform init -backend=false && terraform validate` passes for `dev`, `staging`, and `prod`. This also closed ADR-20260817-01 follow-up (0) — cross-platform provider lock hashes are committed and `TF_PLUGIN_CACHE_DIR` is reinstated. The Terraform half remains **a definition, not an applied state**: `audit_reconciliation_schedule_expression` has not been applied to any environment, so dev still runs the per-minute sweep, and **no `terraform plan` or `apply` was executed** (see the deferral note under Consequences). Not qualified.
- Owner: Dev A (sole engineer)
- Context: ADR-20260817-01 closed the structural problems in the delivery pipeline (duplicate Amplify builds, deploy-before-CI, the unusable `node_modules` cache). A follow-on sweep measured what that pass had assumed, and three of the findings inverted the assumption:

  1. **The `backend/dist` cache cost more than it saved.** Measured on this repository: a full `npm run package:lambdas` for all 21 handlers completes in **1.7s** warm, while `backend/dist` is **46.0 MB** — 38.1 MB of unpacked `index.js` bundles plus 8.0 MB of zips. All three workflows were moving 46 MB through the Actions cache to avoid 1.7 seconds of esbuild. Only the zips are consumed by anything downstream; the 38.1 MB of unpacked bundles were cached, uploaded, and restored for no consumer at all.
  2. **That cache also had a correctness hole.** `infra/environments/*/main.tf` resolves every zip as `fileexists(built) ? built : local.health_lambda_placeholder_zip`, and packaging was guarded by `if: steps.lambda-cache.outputs.cache-hit != 'true'`. A partial or corrupt restore therefore satisfied the guard, skipped packaging, failed `fileexists`, and deployed `infra/modules/http_api/lambda_placeholder.zip` as production handler code behind a green run. The cache was the only thing that could produce that state.
  3. **Every `terraform apply` redeployed all 21 Lambda functions whether or not any code changed.** `bestzip` embeds the bundle's mtime in the archive, so two consecutive builds from byte-identical source produced **21 of 21 zips with different SHA-256 hashes** (measured). Terraform derives `source_code_hash` from `filebase64sha256(<zip>)`, so all 21 functions were reported changed, re-uploaded, and cold-started on every apply. The compounding cost is on the plan: 21 permanent "will be updated in-place" entries in output that `deploy-dev.yml` truncates to `tail -100`, so genuine infrastructure changes were being pushed out of view by unconditional noise.
  4. **No job in any of the four workflows had `timeout-minutes`.** All seven inherited GitHub's 6-hour default. The only bounded waits were hand-rolled polls inside deploy steps, so a wedged `terraform apply` or Amplify poll could burn a day of runner minutes while holding its `concurrency` group — and for staging that group is shared with `destroy-staging.yml`, so a hang would also block teardown.
  5. **`ci.yml` had no path filters.** Of the last 23 commits, 4 touched only `architecture/`, `.kiro/`, or Markdown, and each ran four jobs including three Terraform legs that download the full AWS provider.
  6. **CI's `next build` was always cold.** `amplify.yml` caches `.next/cache` for the deployed build; the CI job that runs the same compile cached nothing.
