- Related files: `backend/src/lib/scheduled-requests.ts`, `backend/src/lib/scheduled-requests.test.ts`, `backend/src/lib/intake-queue.ts`, `backend/src/lib/intake-queue.scheduled-request-exclusion.test.ts`, `backend/src/lib/notifications.ts`, `backend/src/lib/notifications.stream-decline.test.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/intake-queue.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/scheduled-requests.test.ts`, `contracts/openapi.yaml`

### ADR-20260828-02: Terraform routes and flag wiring for scheduled-request accept/decline (plan only)

- Date: 2026-08-28
- Status: Accepted — Terraform changes written and validated (`fmt`, `validate`, `plan` against all three environments); **no `terraform apply` executed against any environment**
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: ADR-20260828-01 implemented and tested the backend for scheduled-request accept/decline behind `SCHEDULED_REQUEST_RESPONSE_ENABLED`, but the flag and the two new routes did not yet exist in Terraform, and the flag's default value could not be `"false"`-by-omission the way `ON_DEMAND_POOL_ENABLED` is, because `declineScheduledRequest` reaches `refundPaymentForBooking` — a code path the `intake_queue` Lambda's environment block had never needed to configure before, since only the `payments` Lambda called it previously.
- Decision:
  - **New module variable `scheduled_request_response_enabled`** (`infra/modules/http_api/variables.tf`), default `"false"`, same `contains(["true","false"], ...)` validation shape as `on_demand_pool_enabled`. Threaded onto the `intake_queue` Lambda's `SCHEDULED_REQUEST_RESPONSE_ENABLED` env var.
  - **Two new routes**, both routed to the existing `intake_queue` Lambda integration (no new Lambda, no new integration): `GET /v1/doctors/me/scheduled-requests` and `POST /v1/doctors/me/scheduled-requests/{bookingId}/respond`. Present in every environment's route inventory regardless of the flag, matching the on-demand-pool convention: a disabled feature 404s rather than being absent from the API surface, so the inventory itself never needs to change when the flag flips.
  - **`PAYMENTS_GATEWAY_PROVIDER` added to the `intake_queue` Lambda**, derived identically to the `payments` Lambda's own derivation (`var.payrex_enabled == "true" ? "payrex" : "ledger"`). Found while wiring this task, not before: `lib/payments.ts`'s `refundPaymentForBooking` resolves its gateway adapter from this variable, defaulting to `'ledger'` when unset — harmless today since every environment runs `payrex_enabled = "false"`, but latent, because if PayRex were ever enabled without this fix, a scheduled-request decline would silently refund through the ledger simulator while every capture went through real PayRex, leaving the two systems permanently disagreeing about the money. `PAYREX_AUDIT_LOG_GROUP`/`PAYREX_PERFORMANCE_LOG_GROUP`, present on the `payments` Lambda, were deliberately **not** copied — grepping the backend confirmed neither is read by any application code (they are set but never consumed even where they already exist), so copying them would only be decoration.
  - **Dev's own flag stays `"false"`, unlike `on_demand_pool_enabled`'s `"true"`.** `infra/environments/dev/variables.tf` declares `scheduled_request_response_enabled` with its own `"false"` default and dev's `main.tf` passes it through explicitly (so the intent is visible, not merely inherited) — but the value itself does not flip to `"true"` in this task, because the frontend surface that would let a doctor act on these routes (Tasks 11–13) had not been built yet. Turning the flag on before that lands would expose a route with no client able to use it correctly.
  - **Staging and prod receive no explicit override**, exactly mirroring `on_demand_pool_enabled`'s own precedent of relying on the module's `"false"` default rather than restating it in every environment.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Also copying `PAYREX_AUDIT_LOG_GROUP`/`PAYREX_PERFORMANCE_LOG_GROUP` onto `intake_queue` for consistency with the `payments` Lambda's env block: rejected once confirmed neither variable is read anywhere in `backend/src` — adding unread configuration to a Lambda invites the next reader to assume it does something.
  - Flipping `scheduled_request_response_enabled` to `"true"` in dev now, to make the backend immediately exercisable: rejected. The routes would answer real requests with no dashboard control to drive them, and a partially-wired feature demoed by accident is worse than one that visibly does not exist yet.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the route inventory is uniform across environments before any environment turns the feature on, matching this project's established flag convention exactly; the latent gateway-provider gap is closed before it can matter, not after a PayRex enablement surfaces it as a live incident.
  - Trade-offs: none beyond the residual, deliberate one already named in ADR-20260828-01.
  - **Not qualified — plan only.** Verified via `terraform fmt -check -recursive` (clean, one unrelated pre-existing `.tfvars` formatting note not touched by this change), `terraform validate` on `dev`/`staging`/`prod` (all succeed, pre-existing unrelated `data_stores` deprecation warnings only), and `terraform plan` on all three: dev shows exactly the two new routes plus the `intake_queue` env-var addition (3 to add, 22 to change — the 22 are pre-existing local-vs-CI zip-path/hash drift and one already-documented PagerDuty-placeholder drift item from ADR-20260823-01, neither caused by this change); staging and prod, both fully unprovisioned, show the two new routes correctly present inside their from-scratch 391/395-resource plans. `infra/tests/test_cds_infrastructure.py`'s `OnDemandPoolFlagWiring` suite (19 tests / 9 subtests) still passes — a doc comment had to be reworded after it first failed the suite's literal-substring check on `ON_DEMAND_POOL_ENABLED` appearing inside prose, which is recorded here so the reason for that phrasing is not mysterious later. No `terraform apply` was run against any environment; no live resource was created, modified, or destroyed.
- Related files: `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/variables.tf`, `infra/environments/dev/main.tf`, `infra/environments/dev/variables.tf`

### ADR-20260828-03: Doctor-facing accept/decline UI for scheduled requests (Task 11)

- Date: 2026-08-28
- Status: Accepted — implemented and tested locally; not applied to any environment (the backing flag stays off everywhere per ADR-20260828-02)
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: ADR-20260828-01/02 delivered the backend and infrastructure for scheduled-request accept/decline with the flag off everywhere, explicitly deferring the dashboard UI to a separate task because no client could act on the routes correctly yet. This closes that gap with a new dashboard card.
- Decision:
  - **New module `lib/api/scheduledRequests.ts`**, following `requestPool.ts`'s exact shape: a hand-written interface mirroring the contract (this codebase's established convention — no `features/doctor` file imports `components[...]` from the generated OpenAPI types directly), a `{ kind: "unavailable" | "ok" }` result distinguishing "flag off" from "empty list", and `acceptScheduledRequest`/`declineScheduledRequest` functions. `declineScheduledRequest` returns `{ kind: "declined" | "refund_failed" }` rather than throwing on `409 PAYMENT_NOT_REFUNDABLE` — the same "distinct outcome, not an error" treatment `acceptRequest` already gives `REQUEST_ALREADY_CLAIMED` — because the booking is left untouched and retrying is the correct next action, not treating it as a generic failure. A `409 STATE_CONFLICT` (the booking stopped being respondable) is thrown normally instead, since there is nothing to retry.
  - **New card `ScheduledRequestsCard.tsx`**, structurally mirroring `RequestPool.tsx`: same card shell, same 4-row visible cap with an "N more" expander, same polling cadence (`LIVE_QUEUE_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`), same render-`null`-when-unavailable rule. Accept is a one-click mutation; decline opens a `Dialog` with an optional, 500-character-bounded note field.
  - **The decline dialog states its in-app-only scope in its own copy** ("Shown to the patient in-app only — never sent by email or SMS"), rather than leaving the doctor to infer it. A doctor about to write something candid about a patient needs to know before submitting whether it could reach that patient's inbox, not discover the answer afterward.
  - **A refund failure keeps the dialog open** with a distinct alert ("This booking has not been changed…") instead of closing on a false success or reading as a generic error — the UI-level expression of the backend's fail-closed guarantee.
  - **Accept invalidates `DOCTOR_INTAKE_QUEUE_QUERY_KEY`** via the shared exported constant, not a parallel string literal — the exact handoff mechanism ADR-20260809-08 fixed for `RequestPool`'s own accept flow, reused here rather than re-derived, since an accepted scheduled request lands on the intake queue through the identical backend path.
  - **Card position**: placed after `RequestPool` and before `IncomingRequestsCard` in the dashboard grid. Grouped with `RequestPool` rather than with `IncomingRequestsCard`/`UpcomingTodayCard`: both are pre-acceptance decision queues (on-demand broadcast vs. scheduled-slot-specific), while the cards after them cover bookings that have already entered the intake pipeline.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Reusing `RequestPool`'s own component with a mode prop for scheduled vs. on-demand: rejected. The two differ enough at the point of decline — on-demand has no decline action at all, since an unclaimed pool entry has no assignee to decline it — that a shared component would need its own internal branching for a case that does not exist on one side, adding complexity to save writing one more card matching an established pattern.
  - A confirmation via `AlertDialog` (yes/no only) with the note collected in a separate step: rejected. The note is optional and the whole interaction is already a single confirmation; splitting it into two steps would cost an extra click for the common case of declining with no note.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: a doctor can now see, accept, and fail-closed-decline scheduled requests entirely from the dashboard, completing the user-facing half of ADR-20260828-01's backend.
  - Trade-offs: none beyond what ADR-20260828-01/02 already named.
  - **Not qualified.** `SCHEDULED_REQUEST_RESPONSE_ENABLED` remains off in every environment (ADR-20260828-02), so this card renders nothing anywhere today. Frontend `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` are clean on every new/changed file. `npx vitest run src/features/doctor` (31 files / 334 tests, all passing) and the page-composition suite were run; a pre-existing, unrelated uncommitted diff to `AssessmentFirstWorkspace.tsx`/`.test.tsx` and `workspacePhase.test.ts` fails independently of this change (confirmed by reproducing the same failures against the committed `HEAD` version of those files with this session's changes stashed out, then restoring the stash) and is out of scope for this task.
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/lib/api/scheduledRequests.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/lib/api/scheduledRequests.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/homepage/ScheduledRequestsCard.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/homepage/ScheduledRequestsCard.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/doctor/(homepage)/page.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/doctor/(homepage)/page.test.tsx`

### ADR-20260828-04: Patient-side decline visibility (Task 12) — three fields were reaching the API response but nothing read them

- Date: 2026-08-28
- Status: Accepted — implemented and tested locally; inert everywhere today (the backing flag stays off per ADR-20260828-02, so no booking can currently carry `declinedBy` in any live environment)
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: `Booking.declinedBy`/`declinedAt`/`declineReason` were added to the contract and to the backend write path in ADR-20260828-01, but nothing on the patient-facing frontend read them. Investigation found every layer between the API response and the rendered page — `BookingDetail`, `BookingListItem`, the wizard's local `Booking` type, and `toWizardBooking()` — deliberately declares its own narrower interface "kept permissive" as a documented *subset* of the contract, per this codebase's established convention (the same one `lib/api/scheduledRequests.ts` follows on the doctor side: no frontend file imports `components[...]` from the generated OpenAPI types directly). That convention is exactly what caused the gap: the three fields were present in every raw JSON response at runtime, and TypeScript correctly reported them as absent because no local interface declared them, so a doctor's decline note reached the browser and was silently discarded before render.
- Decision:
  - **Added the three fields to `BookingDetail` (booking detail) and `declinedBy` alone to `BookingListItem` (booking list / home view)** — `declinedAt`/`declineReason` are read only on the detail page, where the note actually renders; the list views need only know *whether* a cancellation was a decline, not the note itself, to pick the right badge.
  - **`displayBookingStatus` gained an optional second parameter, `declinedByDoctor`**, rather than a new status value. `cancelled` is still the correct lifecycle status — the booking is, factually, cancelled — so this is a presentation distinction, not a new state. When `status === "cancelled" && declinedByDoctor`, the badge reads "Declined by doctor" (tone `warning`) instead of the generic "Cancelled" (tone `neutral`), on the detail page, the "my bookings" list, and the patient home view alike — the same status-badge call site pattern shared across the three surfaces, so passing the flag through was a one-line change at each existing call site rather than three separate implementations. Omitting the parameter (every pre-existing caller) is behaviourally identical to passing `false`, verified by an explicit backward-compatibility test.
  - **`CompletedStep`'s cancelled branch gained a `declinedBy`-gated sibling branch**, `isDeclined`, checked before the existing generic `isCancelled` branch. States plainly that the doctor was unable to take the appointment and that the patient was fully refunded, renders `declineReason` — when present — as plain text inside its own block (never markup, matching the contract's explicit "plain text, never as markup" requirement for every authenticated screen that shows this field), and offers a "Find another doctor" link to `/booking/search`.
  - **The rebooking link is a plain link to the existing doctor-search route, not a dedicated flow that excludes the declining doctor.** The contract already documents the target behaviour ("redirected client-side into a rebooking flow that excludes this doctor"), and Task 13 is the guided rebooking wizard that will build the actual exclusion; wiring a bare link now, and swapping its target once Task 13 exists, keeps this task from partially reimplementing the next one.
- Alternatives considered:
  - A new `"declined"` value in `BookingStatus`: rejected. It is not a new lifecycle state — a declined booking is cancelled, full stop, for every purpose except the badge a patient reads — and threading a seventh status value through every switch/map already keyed on the six real ones (`STATUS_DISPLAY`, `stepByStatus`, `STATUS_ORDER`) would multiply the surface for a distinction that only affects display copy.
  - Importing `components["schemas"]["Booking"]` from the generated types directly into the patient side, bypassing the hand-written-interface convention entirely: rejected, for the same reason `scheduledRequests.ts` gave on the doctor side — it would be the one file breaking an established, deliberate pattern, not a improvement to it.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: a patient can now tell "you cancelled" apart from "the doctor declined" everywhere a booking's status appears, sees the doctor's optional note exactly where the contract intends it (in-app, this page, nowhere else), and has an immediate next step rather than a dead end.
  - Positive: closes the specific gap found — three contract fields that were live in every response and invisible to every render — before Task 13's wizard would have had to duplicate the same plumbing to work at all.
  - Trade-offs: none beyond what ADR-20260828-01 already named; this task added no new backend calls, no new routes, and no new Terraform.
  - **Not qualified — and currently unobservable in any environment.** `SCHEDULED_REQUEST_RESPONSE_ENABLED` is off everywhere (ADR-20260828-02), so no booking can carry `declinedBy` today; this UI is exercised only by the tests below until that changes. Frontend `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` are clean (zero new errors/warnings; the pre-existing 56 warnings and 0 errors on the full lint run are all in files this task never touched). New/updated tests: `lib/bookings.test.ts` (10, new file), `CompletedStep.test.tsx` (8, new file), 3 new cases in `PatientBookingDetail.test.tsx` (24 total, up from 21), 1 new case each in `PatientBookingList.test.tsx` (15 total, up from 13) and `PatientHomeView.test.tsx`. Full frontend suite: 127 files / 1063 tests, all passing.
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/bookings.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/bookings.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/api/bookingDetail.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/api/bookingList.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/types/booking.types.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/PatientBookingDetail.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/PatientBookingDetail.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/consultation/CompletedStep.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/consultation/CompletedStep.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/PatientBookingList.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/PatientBookingList.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/patient/components/homepage/PatientHomeView.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/patient/components/homepage/PatientHomeView.test.tsx`

### ADR-20260828-05: Guided rebooking wizard reuses the existing doctor-search page rather than a new route (Task 13)

- Date: 2026-08-28
- Status: Accepted — implemented and tested locally; inert everywhere today (the backing flag stays off per ADR-20260828-02, so no patient can reach this path in any live environment until a booking can actually carry `declinedBy`)
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Task 12 (ADR-20260828-04) added a "Find another doctor" placeholder link on a declined booking's terminal step, pointing at plain `/booking/search`, with an explicit note that it was a stand-in for this task's real doctor-excluding flow. The contract documents the intended behaviour precisely: "The patient is redirected client-side into a rebooking flow that excludes this doctor; the backend records only the decline itself" — frontend-only, no new mutation, no new endpoint.
- Decision:
  - **No new page, no new route.** Investigation (via a context-gatherer) traced the full existing pipeline — `/booking/search` → `DoctorSearchPanel` (filter state) → `DoctorSearchView`/`FilteredDoctorList` (fetch + client-side filter) → `DoctorList` cards → `/booking/doctor/{doctorId}` (slot picking) → `/booking/createBooking/{doctorId}` (`DoctorBookingLoader` → `DoctorBookingPage`, real `POST /v1/bookings` with `preferredDoctorId`/`slotId`) → `/booking/getBooking/{bookingId}`. Every step already exists, is already tested, and needs no change to serve "pick a different doctor" — the only genuinely missing piece was a way to hide one specific doctor from step one. Building a parallel wizard page would have duplicated this entire chain for no behavioural gain.
  - **Doctor exclusion is one new client-side filter dimension, `excludeDoctorId`, added to `useDoctorFilter` and threaded through `DoctorSearchFilter`/`FilteredDoctorList`'s existing predicate** — the same mechanism `name`/`specialty` already use, since `GET /v1/doctors` has no filter query parameters and every existing filter already runs over the already-fetched list in the browser. This confirms the contract's "client-side… flow" framing is not just accurate but the *only* way this could work without a backend change.
  - **`excludeDoctorId` is seeded from the URL (`?excludeDoctorId=`) and is not a control the filter drawer exposes** — a doctor exclusion did not come from a choice the patient made on the search screen, so there is deliberately no UI to toggle it there, and `clearFilters()` (the "Clear All Filters" button) does not reset it. Clearing the patient's own name/specialty choices must not silently re-admit the doctor who just declined their booking.
  - **`buildRebookingUrl()`** (`lib/rebookingUrl.ts`) is the entire "wizard": it takes the declining doctor's id and returns `/booking/search?excludeDoctorId=<id>`. `CompletedStep`'s declined branch now calls it with `booking.doctorId` — confirmed still present on a declined booking, since `declineScheduledRequest`'s cancel write only adds fields (`status`, `declinedBy`, `declinedAt`, `declineReason`), never clears `doctorId` — replacing Task 12's placeholder link. Falls back to plain, unfiltered `/booking/search` if `doctorId` is somehow absent (defensive; should not occur, since a decline's own precondition requires `item.doctorId === input.doctorId`).
  - **A visible banner (`data-slot="rebooking-exclusion-notice"`), not a silent filter.** `DoctorSearchPanel` renders an `Alert` — "Finding you another doctor… The doctor who declined your previous booking is not shown in these results" — whenever `excludeDoctorId` is set. A silently shortened list with no explanation would read as a bug (a doctor with real availability just vanishing) rather than an intentional, contract-mandated exclusion.
  - **A distinct empty state** (`data-slot="doctor-search-only-excluded-doctor"`) fires specifically when the exclusion, and no name/specialty filter, is what emptied the results — separate from the pre-existing "your filter excluded everyone" message, so a patient whose only nearby doctor for a specialty happens to be the one who declined is told what actually happened rather than left assuming their own search was too narrow.
- Alternatives considered:
  - A dedicated `/booking/rebook` page with its own guided steps: rejected once the pipeline trace confirmed every step already exists and works; a new page would be a second, parallel doctor-booking flow to keep in sync with the real one indefinitely.
  - Exposing `excludeDoctorId` as a togglable chip in the filter drawer (so a patient could "un-exclude" the doctor mid-search): rejected. The doctor declined the booking; re-admitting them into the same rebooking search defeats the reason the patient landed here, and the contract does not describe an escape hatch.
  - Pre-selecting the same service type on rebooking: investigated and explicitly deferred. The wizard's `Booking.serviceRequested` is hardcoded to `"teleconsult"` regardless of the real `serviceType` (a pre-existing gap, confirmed but out of this task's scope), and separately the frontend's six-value patient-facing service catalog has no working round-trip to the backend's four-value `serviceType` enum (everything currently sends `"general"`) — there is no authoritative value to carry forward without first fixing that unrelated gap, so this task does not promise it.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: closes the loop the contract specifically calls for, with the smallest change that could work — one new filter dimension, one URL builder, one banner, zero new pages, zero backend changes.
  - Positive: every downstream step (slot picking, booking creation, idempotency, error handling) is exercised by tests that already existed and continue to pass unmodified — nothing about the actual booking-creation path was touched.
  - Trade-offs: none beyond the already-scoped-out service-type carry-forward, which was a pre-existing gap this task did not introduce and does not claim to fix.
  - **Not qualified — and currently unreachable in any environment.** `SCHEDULED_REQUEST_RESPONSE_ENABLED` is off everywhere (ADR-20260828-02), so no booking can carry `declinedBy` today and this flow is exercised only by tests. Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; full project `eslint` run unchanged at 56 warnings / 0 errors, all pre-existing (confirmed via `git show HEAD` that `useDoctorFilter.ts`'s one flagged line predates this task, and via the prior task's own recorded baseline that this file was already on that list before being touched here). Full frontend suite: 129 files / 1080 tests, all passing (up from 127/1063 before this task).
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/rebookingUrl.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/lib/rebookingUrl.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/hooks/useDoctorFilter.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/hooks/useDoctorFilter.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/DoctorSearchView.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/DoctorSearchPanel.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/patient/DoctorSearchPanel.test.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/consultation/CompletedStep.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/booking/components/consultation/CompletedStep.test.tsx`

### ADR-20260831-01: Physician amendment of generated CDS artifacts, a stored signature specimen, and the post-consult workspace rebuild they enable

- Date: 2026-08-31
- Status: Accepted — implemented and tested locally; not qualified in any environment
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Context: Live use of the post-consult workspace surfaced a cluster of complaints that all resolve to the same two structural facts, not to styling. **(1) Every clinical document on the page is model-authored and the physician could neither tell nor change it.** A drafted prescription rendered in the same neutral card chrome as the physician's own Assessment, and the only response to a wrong frequency or a clumsy sentence was to regenerate the whole document and hope — because no endpoint in the contract could mutate an artifact payload after generation. **(2) The gate token was the physician's problem.** Confirming an Assessment left every protected tool dark until they found "Re-check safety and unlock" in a different column, and signing required drawing a signature by hand on a 500×200 canvas for every document, on every consultation, with that canvas opening over the document being reviewed. Around those two: the CPG preview — the entire reason to pick a diagnosis here rather than type one — rendered below a scrolling candidate list where it was never read, and a click on a candidate *committed* to it rather than previewing it; generation produced no visible change until the artifact arrived, and pressing a second tool meanwhile did nothing at all, because one page-wide `busy` flag disabled everything; drafted documents stacked as full cards, so reviewing the fifth meant scrolling past four; and the layout jumped from one column straight to three at `xl`, giving every laptop between those widths both rails stacked around the work.
- Decision:
  - **A new protected-artifact amendment endpoint, `POST /v1/cds/consultations/{consultationId}/outputs/{artifactId}/amendments`.** Modelled as a sibling of finalization rather than as a generation: `ProtectedArtifactLifecycleService.amend()` reuses finalization's snapshot load, its `assertCurrent` authority revalidation and its authority `ConditionCheck` set verbatim, so an amendment can never commit against state a finalization would have refused. Three things are specific to it — the artifact stays `generated` (amending is authoring, not signing); the submitted payload is revalidated through the same `validateProtectedOutputPayload` the generated payload passed, so a hand-edited prescription is not exempt from the bounds that make a prescription safe to dispense; and the write stamps `physicianEdited` / `physicianEditedAt` / `physicianEditRevision`, new optional fields on `CdsProtectedArtifact` that `toArtifactView` now carries, with a `protected_artifact_amended` audit-outbox event. The artifact update's `ConditionExpression` includes `attribute_not_exists(signature)` so a finalization racing between the read and the write wins rather than having its signed content silently replaced.
  - **`patient_education` is validated in whichever origin mode the *stored* artifact was published in**, with the stored payload standing in as the corpus article. Provider mode is a closed object without the corpus keys, so validating a corpus-backed article there would reject every amendment of the commonest case outright; validating with no corpus would drop the guarantee that matters. The result is exactly right clinically: `assertCorpusOwnedFieldsUnchanged` lets a physician rewrite the English body they are accountable for and byte-refuses any change to the approved citation, the Filipino text, the warning signs, or the article's structure (ADR-20260804-01). The editor mirrors that rule rather than offering fields the server will reject.
  - **The gate token stops being something the physician manages.** Confirming an Assessment issues one immediately, against the state the server just returned; drafting re-issues one on demand when it has lapsed. The server-side gate is untouched — it still verifies a signed token on every protected generation, and issuance still re-runs safety, assignment, clinical-input and policy checks — so the check runs exactly as often and the physician is simply no longer asked to trigger it by hand. `deriveRailState`'s `hasGateToken` was accordingly renamed `draftingAuthorized`: keyed on a held token, the rail read "locked" on the physician's own page reload of a confirmed consultation the server would have authorised. The manual "Re-run safety check" control survives only where drafting is genuinely held.
  - **A reusable signature specimen on the doctor's profile** — `DoctorSignatureSpecimen` (`signerName`, `strokes`, `updatedAt`, `drawingSha256`) plus `bio`, added to `DoctorProfile` and `DoctorProfileUpsertRequest`. Signing a document becomes: read it, tick the attestation, press a button showing your own signature. It is explicitly a convenience and not an identity credential — the evidentiary signature is still the one committed with each finalization, which the server stamps with its own digest and timestamp at signing time, and the per-document attestation is still required (a stored specimen alone leaves the sign button disabled). Drawing stays available inline for a doctor who has not set one up, so a first-time signer is never sent away mid-consultation.
  - **`PUT /v1/doctors/me/profile` no longer refuses every edit after submission.** It threw `PROFILE_NOT_EDITABLE` for any profile past `draft`/`rejected`, which meant a verified doctor could not fix their own phone number, write a bio, or save a signature — there was no other doctor-owned account surface in the product. From `pending` onward the endpoint now pins `fullName`, `licenseNumber`, the verification status and the KYC queue index to their reviewed values and accepts the rest; the identity fields are the evidence a moderator reviewed, and a plain "recreate the item" upsert would otherwise have reset an approved doctor to `draft`.
  - **Violet (`--ai-*`) is reserved across the app for machine-authored content**, deliberately outside the brand ramp: navy, teal, gold and red already carry meanings a clinician reads for safety, and marking model output with any of them would collide with a status. A generated artifact card carries a violet border, header and "AI draft · you own it" label; once signed it steps down to neutral card chrome, because at that point it is the physician's attested output rather than a draft awaiting their judgement.
  - **The workspace was split into `CandidatePicker`, `DeliverablesDeck`, `ArtifactCard`, `ArtifactPayloadEditor` and `SignaturePreview`.** The picker puts the CPG preview beside the list (sticky on wide screens, *above* it on one column, so the thing that just appeared in response to a tap is the thing in view) and separates previewing from committing — a click previews, a button in the preview commits. The deck renders one document at a time behind a tab strip that doubles as the review checklist, and a generation in flight gets its own tab immediately, which is what makes pressing a second tool do something visible. Generation moved off the page-wide `busy` flag onto a per-output-type set, so drafts are concurrent and the rest of the page stays live. The layout steps 1 → 2 → 3 columns with both rails sticky on the wide ones.
  - **`/doctor/kyc` became `/doctor/profile`** (a permanent redirect, not a duplicate page — two routes rendering one surface is how the two drift apart), with credentialing demoted to one section of it alongside details, bio and signature. The sidebar item keeps its `credentialing` id so nothing keyed on it silently resets.
- Alternatives considered:
  - **Holding physician edits client-side and submitting them with the finalization call**: rejected outright. `CdsProtectedArtifactFinalizationRequest` is `additionalProperties: false`, so the edits would have been discarded server-side while the UI reported success — a doctor believing they had corrected a dose that was never corrected. Cosmetic editing of clinical content is worse than none.
  - **A generic "regenerate with instructions" instead of a structured editor**: rejected. It re-rolls the whole document, leaves the physician re-reviewing everything, and still cannot guarantee the one field they wanted changed actually changed.
  - **A free-text JSON editor over the payload**: rejected. Every output type has a closed schema in the contract; typed fields that enforce the schema's `minItems`/`maxItems` and per-field bounds stop the physician with a disabled button rather than a 422 after they have typed a paragraph.
  - **Allowing amendment of a finalized or released artifact**: rejected. That edits clinical content out from under an attestation that has already been made. The route after signing is a redraft.
  - **Storing the signature specimen in `localStorage`**: rejected. It would not survive a device change, would be invisible to any audit, and the doctor profile is the correct home for something a doctor sets up once.
  - **Dropping the `PROFILE_NOT_EDITABLE` guard wholesale**: rejected. The upsert recreates the item, so an unguarded save would have reset an approved doctor to `draft` and dropped them out of the moderator queue index.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: a physician can now see that a document was written by a model, correct it in the fields the schema defines, and sign it in two clicks with the signature already on file — the three things the surface was most criticised for.
  - Positive: an amended artifact is distinguishable from untouched model output everywhere it is projected — the card, the history list, the audit outbox — because `toArtifactView` is a whitelist and the provenance fields were added to it deliberately.
  - Positive: the amendment path adds no new authority. It reuses finalization's revalidation and condition set, so any future tightening of the gate applies to it automatically rather than needing to be remembered twice.
  - Trade-offs: `CdsProtectedArtifact` grows three optional fields, and one more Lambda route joins the assessment-first set in `infra/modules/cds_operations/routes.tf`. A corpus-backed patient-education article is only partially editable, which is correct but must be explained on the surface rather than discovered through a 422 — the editor states it inline.
  - Trade-off: the physician no longer sees the gate token's lifecycle at all. That is the intent, but it means a token failure now surfaces as a generation error rather than as a visibly-locked rail; `confirm` reports explicitly when automatic unlocking did not succeed.
  - **Not qualified.** No staging or canary run, no live inference exercise, and the new endpoint has never been called against a deployed environment — the Terraform route is written but unapplied. Local evidence only: backend `tsc --noEmit` and frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean (the sole remaining errors on both — `zod` missing for `waitlist-validation.ts`, and `embla-carousel-auto-scroll` missing for `DoctorsSection.tsx` — are pre-existing on `main` and were confirmed by stashing this work). `eslint` reports 0 errors on every file touched. Backend suite: 1214 tests passing across 94 files, with the 95th (`waitlist.test.ts`) failing only on that same pre-existing missing dependency; 5 new amendment cases in `protected-artifact-lifecycle.test.ts` (20 total, up from 15). Full frontend suite: 1258 tests passing across 140 files, with 3 files (`DoctorsSection.test.tsx`, `dark-mode.test.tsx`, and one sibling) failing only to resolve the same pre-existing missing `embla-carousel-auto-scroll` import in landing-page code this task never touched. Post-consult frontend suite specifically: 98 tests passing across 7 files, up from 92, including 6 new cases covering AI provenance, the amendment round-trip and its revision fences, signing from a stored specimen with the attestation still required, per-document drafting progress with a concurrent second draft, and the deck's one-document-at-a-time behaviour. `assessmentFirst.security.test.ts` was widened to scan all five workspace source files rather than the one it used to be, so splitting the component did not quietly narrow its coverage, and gained two cases pinning the amendment's revision fences and the per-document attestation.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/lib/cds/protected-artifact-lifecycle.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/protected-artifact-lifecycle.test.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/output-views.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/boundary.ts`, `backend/src/lib/cds/task4-dispatch.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/cds.ts`, `backend/src/lib/doctor-kyc.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/doctor-kyc.ts`, `infra/modules/cds_operations/routes.tf`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/styles/bayanhealth-tokens.css`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/AssessmentFirstWorkspace.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/ArtifactCard.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/ArtifactPayloadEditor.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/CandidatePicker.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/DeliverablesDeck.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/SignaturePreview.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/ProtectedToolsRail.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/workspacePhase.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/cdsCopy.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/lib/api/assessmentFirst.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/profile/DoctorProfileView.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/kyc/DoctorKycView.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/lib/api/kyc.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/sidebar/DoctorSidebar.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/doctor/(homepage)/profile/page.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/app/doctor/(homepage)/kyc/page.tsx`

### ADR-20260904-01: Per-environment security baseline — account-scoped controls, closed non-production, WAF only where traffic is anonymous
- Date: 2026-09-04
- Status: Accepted
- Owner: Lead Engineer (Dev A)
- Supersedes: the **Security baseline (Low tier)** clause of **ADR-20260710-01** only. That ADR's budget envelope, cost-optimization stack, telemetry scope and AI posture are unchanged.
- Context: The three environments now have distinct, stated roles — **`dev` is the sandbox**, **`staging` is the beta-testing environment for features before they go live**, and **`prod` is the live environment**. ADR-20260710-01 approved a **WAF + GuardDuty + AWS Config** baseline and described it as **prod only**. Two things are wrong with that as written, and a third has changed underneath it.
  - **It was never built.** A search across `infra/` finds no `aws_guardduty_detector`, no `aws_config_configuration_recorder` and no `aws_wafv2_*` resource in any module or environment root — the only match anywhere is a `wafv2:*` grant in `infra/bootstrap/github_actions.tf`. The live account agrees: zero detectors, zero recorders, zero web ACLs (`architecture/INFRASTRUCTURE_COSTS.md` §9A). ADR-20260710-01 follow-up (a) was never actioned, so this ADR is a design decision about what to build, not a change to running infrastructure.
  - **"Prod only" is not implementable for two of the three controls.** All three environments share **one AWS account (`472206752892`) in one region (`ap-southeast-1`)**, separated only by Terraform state keys (`infra/environments/*/versions.tf`). A **GuardDuty detector** and an **AWS Config configuration recorder** are both **account-and-region scoped**: one of each covers every resource in the account regardless of `Environment` tag. They cannot be switched on for `prod` and off for `dev`/`staging` without separate AWS accounts. Only **WAF** is genuinely per-environment, because a web ACL is associated with a named Amplify app or API Gateway stage. The prod-only framing therefore both understated GuardDuty's coverage (it free-rides to all three) and understated AWS Config's cost (it would record `dev`'s 21 Lambdas and `staging`'s resources too, not just `prod`'s).
  - **`staging`'s data classification has changed.** Beta testing means real humans exercising real clinical flows. A doctor beta-testing a consultation will type real clinical language and a patient beta-tester will enter their real symptoms, whatever the instructions say. `staging` must be treated as PHI-bearing; it can no longer be reasoned about as a second sandbox.
- Decision:
  - **Data classification is the input to every control below.** `dev` = **synthetic only**, enforced by seeded accounts and no production data path. `staging` = **PHI-bearing**. `prod` = **PHI-bearing**. Where a control protects PHI, `staging` gets prod's treatment, not dev's.
  - **Account-level controls — one instance, covering all three environments. Not scopeable per environment in a single account.**
    - **Amazon GuardDuty: ENABLED**, one detector, `ap-southeast-1`. It is the only automated detector of stolen credentials or anomalous API behaviour we have, it costs on the order of ₱90/month for the whole account, and in a single-account layout it protects `dev` and `staging` at no extra charge. There is no cost argument for leaving it off and no way to scope it to `prod` anyway. Findings route to the existing SNS alert topic.
    - **CloudTrail: the existing management-events trail is brought into Terraform.** The first trail is free and management events are already being recorded by an unmanaged trail plus an unmanaged bucket (`INFRASTRUCTURE_COSTS.md` §9B). It is adopted rather than recreated. **No S3/Lambda data-event logging** is enabled — that is where CloudTrail becomes expensive and it buys little at pilot volume.
    - **AWS Budgets + cost-anomaly detection: ENABLED**, set to the ADR-20260710-01 envelope. Free, and it is that ADR's never-actioned follow-up (c).
    - **AWS Config: DEFERRED**, explicitly and with named compensating controls (below) and named re-entry triggers. It is an audit-evidence tool, not a runtime or patient-safety control; nothing about clinical safety degrades in its absence, and in this account it would bill for all three environments.
  - **Per-environment edge controls — the axis that is genuinely scopeable.**
    - **All three environments sit behind Cloudflare** (free plan): TLS, L3/L4 DDoS absorption, bot filtering, rate limiting, managed rule coverage.
    - **`dev` and `staging` are CLOSED.** **Cloudflare Access** (free tier, up to 50 users) sits in front of both: the team for `dev`, the named beta cohort for `staging`. Neither is reachable anonymously from the internet. **This is strictly stronger than a WAF for a closed audience** — a WAF filters hostile anonymous traffic, whereas Access means there is no anonymous traffic to filter — and it costs nothing. Neither environment carries an AWS WAF web ACL.
    - **`prod` is the only environment that must accept anonymous traffic, and is the only one carrying an AWS WAF web ACL.** It is attached to the **API Gateway HTTP API** — the front door that reaches PHI — not to the Amplify site. Cloudflare fronts the site.
    - **The $15/month Amplify–WAF integration is not purchased.** It was ₱925 of the ₱1,442 WAF line and buys an attachment point, not protection; Cloudflare covers the site edge.
  - **Encryption, audit and recovery parity follows the classification, not the environment name.** `staging` keeps the same **three KMS CMKs**, **DynamoDB PITR**, **audit outbox** and **retention discipline** as `prod`. `dev`'s backup key drops to the AWS-managed `aws/backup` key (its backups are disposable and its data synthetic); `dev` **retains** its own signing and replay CMKs, because the ES256 gate-signing key is asymmetric and has no AWS-managed equivalent, and because the replay key's scoped policy is exactly the thing `dev` exists to exercise before `prod` runs it.
  - **Beta data handling in `staging`.** Beta testers receive a notice that the environment is a test system; `staging` clinical data is deleted at the end of each beta cycle rather than accumulating; `staging` is torn down between beta cycles using the existing teardown workflow, which also takes its cost to zero.
  - **Compensating controls for the deferred AWS Config**, all already in place and free: **CloudTrail management events** (who changed what, and when), **versioned Terraform state in S3** (what every resource looked like at each apply — a genuine configuration timeline), and the **nightly drift-detection workflow** (whether anything changed out of band). Together these answer the questions Config answers, less conveniently and without managed compliance rules.
  - **Re-entry triggers for AWS Config — a "when", not an "if".** Enable it, scoped by resource type (IAM, S3, DynamoDB, KMS, security groups, Lambda *configuration* but not versions), with **periodic rather than continuous recording** and a **cost anomaly alarm attached from the first day**, on the earliest of: (i) the first external security or privacy audit; (ii) NPC registration or a DPA obligation that names configuration history; (iii) real PHI in `prod` at volume beyond the pilot; (iv) the account moving to multi-account, which makes per-environment scoping real.
  - **Security Hub, Amazon Macie, and external pentest/audit retainers remain OUT of scope**, unchanged from ADR-20260710-01. So do AWS Network Firewall (there is no VPC ingress path — Lambdas run outside a VPC) and AWS Inspector (the CI dependency audit covers the same ground at pilot scale).
- Alternatives considered:
  - **Keep ADR-20260710-01's prod-only baseline as written**: rejected — it is not implementable. GuardDuty and Config are account-scoped, so "prod only" would either silently cover all three environments (GuardDuty, harmlessly) or silently bill for all three (Config, expensively) while the documentation claimed otherwise.
  - **Separate AWS accounts per environment under AWS Organizations**: this is the correct long-term answer and the only thing that makes per-environment GuardDuty and Config real, plus it fixes the single-account blast radius. **Deferred** — it is a multi-week migration of Terraform state, IAM, the CI deploy roles, Cognito pools and the Amplify apps, and it multiplies every per-account fixed charge. Recorded as a follow-up with its own trigger.
  - **An AWS WAF web ACL on `staging` as well** (~₱550/month): rejected. Cloudflare Access removes anonymous reachability entirely, which addresses the same threat more completely for ₱0. Buying a WAF to filter traffic that cannot arrive is spending without a corresponding risk reduction.
  - **Leaving `dev` and `staging` open with no edge control**: rejected. Non-production environments are the classic soft target precisely because they are watched less, and `staging` will hold PHI under this ADR.
  - **Treating `staging` as a sandbox like `dev`**: rejected on the stated role. Beta testers are real people entering real clinical text; classifying that as synthetic would be a fiction with a privacy consequence.
  - **Enabling AWS Config now, unscoped**: rejected. It is the least predictable line in the cost audit (`[VERIFY]`, plausibly half or triple the forecast), it would record three environments, and it answers audit questions we do not yet face.
  - **Enabling GuardDuty for `prod` only**: not possible in one account, as above.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: **broader coverage at lower cost.** GuardDuty now protects all three environments instead of one; `staging` gains a defined security posture it never had; and planned monthly security spend falls from the approved **₱2,650** to about **₱606** — roughly **₱24,500/year** — while adding controls rather than removing them.
  - Positive: **closing `dev` and `staging` removes more risk than a WAF on them would have.** The largest realistic non-production threat is an anonymous actor finding a forgotten environment; Access makes that impossible rather than merely filtered.
  - Positive: the ADR-20260710-01 envelope now holds to nearly 4,000 consults/month instead of breaking at about 3,500 (`INFRASTRUCTURE_COSTS.md` §3).
  - Positive: three of ADR-20260710-01's never-actioned follow-ups — the Terraform baseline, the budget alarms, and the security scope — are given concrete, buildable definitions here.
  - Trade-offs: **no queryable configuration timeline and no managed compliance rules** until Config is enabled. Mitigated by the three named compensating controls, but they are less convenient at audit time and this must be re-read when any re-entry trigger fires.
  - Trade-offs: **Cloudflare is a new external dependency and a second control plane outside Terraform.** Its configuration is not captured in `infra/` and will not appear in drift detection. It needs its own runbook entry and an owner, or it becomes undocumented infrastructure of exactly the kind §9B already flags.
  - Trade-offs: **the single-account blast radius is unchanged.** A credential compromise still reaches all three environments. GuardDuty detects it; it does not contain it. Only the deferred multi-account migration addresses this, and that gap is stated plainly here rather than treated as covered.
  - Trade-offs: `staging` at prod-grade encryption and retention costs more than a sandbox-grade staging would, roughly ₱600/month while it is running. That is the price of classifying it honestly.
  - Follow-up actions: (a) write the Terraform for the GuardDuty detector, the adopted CloudTrail trail, the AWS Budgets/anomaly alarms and the `prod` API Gateway web ACL, gated so non-prod creates no ACL; (b) configure Cloudflare and Cloudflare Access for all three environments and record the configuration in a runbook with a named owner; (c) write the `staging` beta-tester notice and the end-of-cycle data-deletion procedure; (d) fix `payrex_pagerduty_placeholder` so GuardDuty findings have a real alerting endpoint — the nightly drift loop recorded under ADR-20260823-01 must be resolved before an alert path can be trusted; (e) re-read this ADR when any AWS Config re-entry trigger fires; (f) raise the multi-account migration as its own ADR when a second doctor cohort, an external audit, or a funding round makes the blast radius unacceptable.
- Related files: `architecture/INFRASTRUCTURE_COSTS.md`, `architecture/DECISIONS.md`, `infra/environments/dev/`, `infra/environments/staging/`, `infra/environments/prod/`, `infra/modules/http_api/`, `infra/modules/amplify_hosting/`, `infra/modules/cds_operations/backup.tf`, `infra/bootstrap/github_actions.tf`

### ADR-20260907-01: Remove the `moderator` role — `/admin` is admin-only
- Status: Accepted — code, contract, and Terraform changed; **not yet applied to any environment** (the Cognito group still exists in dev until `terraform apply` runs; see Consequences)
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Supersedes: the `moderator` portion of ADR-20260416-13 (Cognito RBAC representation)
- Context: Wiring the admin panel to its design surfaced a role that never worked end-to-end. `route-guard.ts` admitted both `admin` and `moderator` into `/admin`, but **every** route on the admin Lambda is `requireAuth(event, 'admin')` — all 19 of them. A moderator could therefore open every admin page and receive `403` on every request the page made. Separately, `doctor-kyc.ts` carried a real, functioning moderator surface (`GET/POST /v1/moderator/kyc-applications*`, gated on `requireAuth(event, 'moderator')`, with its own `MODERATOR#EVENTS` audit partition), but no frontend code ever called it — only the generated types referenced those paths. So the role was simultaneously over-privileged in the route guard and unreachable in the product.
- Decision: remove the role outright rather than repair it, at every layer.
  - **Contract** (`contracts/openapi.yaml`): `CognitoRbacGroup` reduced to `[patient, doctor, admin]`; the three `/v1/moderator/kyc-applications*` paths and their now-orphaned `ModeratorKycApplicationDetail` / `ModeratorKycDocumentDetail` schemas deleted. Generated types regenerated in both packages (`contracts:check` clean).
  - **Backend**: the three moderator handlers, their router cases, and `recordModeratorAudit` removed from `doctor-kyc.ts`, along with the imports and constants that became orphaned (`getSignedUrl`, `GetObjectCommand`, `PutCommand`, `docClient`, `APP_AUDIT_AI_TABLE`, `MODERATOR_AUDIT_PK`). `COGNITO_RBAC_GROUPS` reduced. `reviewDoctorKyc`'s `moderatorId` parameter renamed `reviewerId` (internal only — the persisted field stays `reviewedBy`, so no stored record changes shape).
  - **Frontend**: `AppRole` reduced to three; `moderator` dropped from `ROLE_PRIORITY`, `AREAS` (so `/admin` lists `roles: ["admin"]`), `ROLE_HOME`, `navigation.ts`, `proxy.ts`'s `KNOWN_ROLES`, the sign-in redirect map, and the admin UI's role labels and `ASSIGNABLE_GROUPS`. Doctor-facing KYC copy now says an *admin* reviews the licence, which is what actually happens.
  - **Terraform**: `moderator` removed from the `aws_cognito_user_group.rbac` `for_each`; the three `aws_apigatewayv2_route.moderator_kyc_*` resources deleted; the dev environment's now-dangling `import` block for `ap-southeast-1_bnXemsKn2/moderator` removed.
  - **A regression test pins the invariant**: `route-guard.admin-access.test.ts` now asserts `AREAS.find(a => a.prefix === "/admin").roles` equals exactly `["admin"]`, so the area cannot quietly regain a second role whose requests would 403.
- Alternatives considered:
  - **Keep the role and give it real backend scopes** (read-only admin endpoints for a moderator tier): rejected for now. It is a genuine product decision about staffing, not a bug fix, and nothing in the product currently needs a second tier. It stays available as a future ADR if operations grows.
  - **Gate the route only** (drop `moderator` from `AREAS`, leave everything else): rejected as the explicitly narrower option. It would leave a Cognito group, a contract enum value, and three deployed API routes that nothing can reach — the same latent confusion, one layer down.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the route guard and the API now agree. `/admin` admits exactly the role the API accepts, so an admin page can no longer render a shell whose every request fails.
  - Positive: three deployed-but-unreachable API routes and their audit partition stop being surface area to maintain and secure.
  - **Destructive on apply — verify before running it.** The next `terraform apply` against dev **destroys** the `moderator` Cognito group. Any user still in that group silently loses it; on their next token refresh they resolve to no recognised role and land on `/no-access` (Requirement 4.6), not an error page. Confirm the group is empty (`aws cognito-idp list-users-in-group --group-name moderator`) before applying. Staging and prod carry no moderator import block, so they are unaffected beyond the group never being created.
  - Trade-off: `ConsentActorRole` narrowed to three values. This is validated at **construction only** (`CONSENT_ACTOR_ROLES` is checked in the record-input path, never on read), so previously stored consent records with `actorRole: "moderator"` remain readable and are not rewritten — only new moderator-authored consents become impossible, which is correct given no moderator can exist.
  - Trade-off: doctor KYC review is now a single-tier responsibility. If credentialing volume ever needs a dedicated reviewer who is not a full platform admin, that tier has to be rebuilt deliberately rather than inherited.
  - Verification: backend `tsc` clean, 95 files / 1220 tests passing (one case fewer — `boundary.test.ts` dropped `moderator` from its non-doctor role list). Frontend `tsc` clean, `eslint` 0 errors on every touched file, `next build` succeeds, 143 files / 1277 tests passing. The one failing suite (`theme-hardcoded-color-scan`) is unrelated and pre-existing: it flags two `bg-white` utilities in the untracked `src/features/patient/components/redesign/primitives.tsx`. `terraform validate` succeeds (warnings are pre-existing DynamoDB `hash_key` deprecations).
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/handlers/doctor-kyc.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/admin.ts`, `backend/src/lib/admin-users.ts`, `backend/src/lib/doctor-kyc.ts`, `backend/src/lib/consent-record.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/stores/useAuthStore.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/roles.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/route-guard.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/navigation.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/proxy.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/route-guard.admin-access.test.ts`, `infra/modules/cognito/main.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `infra/environments/dev/main.tf`

### ADR-20260911-01: Remediate bounded low-severity findings from scan-b3a0e4d4
- Date: 2026-09-11
- Status: Accepted — repository remediation only; no environment mutation, provider enablement, retention-policy apply, or deployment is implied.
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Context: Security Scan Report `scan-b3a0e4d4` recorded twelve Low findings. Ten have bounded code or Terraform-definition remediations that preserve accepted product behavior. Two concern controls whose intended behavior or deletion period is not specified and therefore cannot be safely inferred from a scan result.
- Decision:
  - Bind public intake-link writes to the linked booking's editable lifecycle; inactive links disclose only their lifecycle status; and bound the persisted OLDCART map by key grammar, cardinality, and sanitized aggregate size.
  - Re-authorize every WebSocket fan-out against the authoritative canonical session participants, removing stale connections best-effort after reassignment.
  - Bind explicit break-glass grant use to its owning admin while retaining cross-admin lookup and revocation; conceal unauthorized consultation-document reads as `404`; and require an active consultation for media writes.
  - Bind booking pagination cursors to the exact authenticated patient or doctor partition, not only a key prefix.
  - Align cancellation refunds to a held-only state across the application preflight, PayRex adapter, circuit-breaker fallback, and local ledger transition.
  - Scope notifications SES send authority to a declared verified identity and `ses:FromAddress`; outbox mode receives no SES send permission. Applying this IAM definition remains a separately authorized environment operation.
  - Defer PlatformSettings control semantics (`maintenanceMode`, notification switch, defaults, and reminder timing) until a product/operational specification defines enforceable behavior. Do not silently activate a live kill switch or alter patient notification behavior.
  - Defer lifecycle expiration for `lab-results/`, `kyc-documents/`, and `consultation-exports/` until an approved retention and legal-hold schedule exists for each artifact class. Do not infer a deletion period from DynamoDB TTLs or apply lifecycle rules based solely on the scan.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: stale links, connections, grants, cursors, and payment states cannot be used outside their current server-derived authority boundary.
  - Positive: an unconfigured/outbox notifications Lambda no longer has standing SES sender authority, and a configured sender is limited to its verified identity.
  - Trade-off: the active/inactive public intake inspection response is now a documented union; clients must branch on `linkStatus` before reading active-only fields.
  - Trade-off: captured refunds remain a separate reconciliation/payment-state design rather than being silently attempted through a cancellation path.
  - Follow-up actions: obtain a product decision for PlatformSettings semantics and a legal/retention decision for each uncovered PHI prefix; then make a separately reviewed, authorized change and apply it only to the named environment.
- Related files: `.security-agent/findings-scan-b3a0e4d4.md`, `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/handlers/intake.ts`, `backend/src/lib/intake.ts`, `backend/src/lib/chat-delivery.ts`, `backend/src/lib/sessions.ts`, `backend/src/lib/break-glass.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/consultation-documents.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/media.ts`, `backend/src/lib/dynamo.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/bookings.ts`, `backend/src/lib/conversations.ts`, `backend/src/lib/payments.ts`, `backend/src/lib/payrex-adapter.ts`, `infra/modules/notifications/`.

### ADR-20260913-01: Remove dormant provider secrets from deployed landing-only prod and disabled dev PayRex

- Date: 2026-09-13
- Status: Accepted; deletion authorized for the four exact secrets below with a seven-day recovery window
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Context: The deployed production surface is landing-and-waitlist only and has no CDS, PayRex, or video runtime consuming provider credentials. Dev uses the ledger payment adapter with PayRex disabled and unqualified. Keeping dormant Secrets Manager objects incurs recurring per-secret charges and causes the Terraform graph to imply integrations that are not deployed or enabled.
- Decision:
  - Schedule these exact secrets for permanent deletion with a seven-day recovery period: `bayanhealth-prod-together-api-key`, `bayanhealth-prod-payrex-credentials`, `bayanhealth-prod-video-provider-credentials`, and `bayanhealth-dev-payrex-credentials`.
  - Make each provider secret independently optional in `data_stores`; prod disables all three, while dev disables only PayRex and retains the active Together.ai and Daily video secrets.
  - Remove the disabled PayRex secret from shared Lambda IAM resources and expose an empty runtime ARN while `PAYREX_ENABLED=false`, so disabled code paths perform no secret read.
  - Reconcile Terraform state immediately after the operation-specific Secrets Manager deletion calls so later plans do not recreate or attempt to manage scheduled-for-deletion objects.
  - A future full production launch must deliberately re-enable the required secret resources, populate new provider credentials, apply the resulting IAM/Lambda ARN wiring, and qualify each integration. During the seven-day recovery window, restoration is required instead of same-name recreation.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Removes four dormant per-secret charges without deleting any customer-managed KMS key or affecting the landing/waitlist runtime.
  - Trade-off: PayRex cannot process dev payments or webhooks until its secret is restored or recreated and the provider is re-enabled; prod CDS, PayRex, and Daily remain intentionally unavailable until provisioned as part of a later launch.
  - Safety boundary: This authorization covers only the four named Secrets Manager secrets in `ap-southeast-1`; it does not authorize KMS deletion, credential revocation at external providers, or any other environment mutation.
- Related files: `infra/modules/data_stores/main.tf`, `infra/modules/data_stores/variables.tf`, `infra/modules/data_stores/outputs.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/payrex-webhook.tf`, `infra/environments/dev/main.tf`, `infra/environments/prod/main.tf`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`

### ADR-20260913-02: Cost-optimize the idle dev CDS alarm set

- Date: 2026-09-13
- Status: Accepted — applied to dev on 2026-09-13; no staging/prod mutation
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Context: Live inventory found 20 `bayanhealth-dev-cds-*` metric alarms in `ap-southeast-1`, all `OK`, all without alarm actions, and no staging/prod alarms. The idle dev environment does not provide enough sustained traffic for anomaly baselines or latency SLO evaluation, assessment-first migration is complete, and warning-tier audit alarms duplicate retained critical thresholds. Metric-math and anomaly alarms increase monitored-metric units beyond the alarm-object count.
- Decision:
  - Add an `extended_alarm_set_enabled` CDS-operations control that defaults to enabled and may be disabled only when `environment == "dev"`.
  - Disable it in dev, removing exactly these eight live alarms on the next authorized dev apply: `bayanhealth-dev-cds-abnormal-gate-denial`, `bayanhealth-dev-cds-protected-latency-p95`, `bayanhealth-dev-cds-migration-unknown`, `bayanhealth-dev-cds-migration-quarantine`, `bayanhealth-dev-cds-migration-mismatch`, `bayanhealth-dev-cds-policy-activation-failure`, `bayanhealth-dev-cds-outbox-warning-count`, and `bayanhealth-dev-cds-outbox-warning-age`.
  - Preserve the definitions for staging/prod or a future traffic-bearing dev campaign. Protected environments cannot disable the extended set through this variable.
  - Retain the twelve core alarms covering denied-path and stale-publication invariants, red-flag reliability, KMS/manifest failures, stuck lock/recovery state, async exhaustion/queue age, audit DLQ depth, and critical audit-outbox count/age.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Removes approximately ten standard alarm-metric units from idle dev while preserving the core clinical-safety and audit-loss signals.
  - Trade-off: Dev loses continuous anomaly, latency-SLO, warning-tier, policy-activation, and migration alarm state until the extended set is re-enabled before a relevant qualification or migration campaign.
  - Operational boundary: This change does not add notification actions; the retained alarms remain console/dashboard indicators until an SNS action topic is deliberately wired.
- Related files: `infra/modules/cds_operations/monitoring.tf`, `infra/modules/cds_operations/variables.tf`, `infra/modules/cds_operations/outputs.tf`, `infra/environments/dev/main.tf`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`

### ADR-20260913-03: Add an authenticated intake questionnaire v2 without changing the public intake flow

- Date: 2026-09-13
- Status: Accepted and implemented in the repository; operational qualification remains pending
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Context: The authenticated patient intake needs a clearer, English-first four-step questionnaire with bounded structured clinical context while preserving the existing public one-time-link UI and all readable version-1 records. A new route or storage aggregate would split one booking's intake across competing transports and force migration without adding an access pattern.
- Decision:
  - The new four-step UI is authenticated-only and English-first. The public intake UI and its legacy request behavior remain unchanged.
  - Questionnaire v2 is an additive transport on the existing authenticated booking intake GET/PUT routes and the existing `BOOKING#<bookingId>/INTAKE` item. No route, key prefix, or GSI is added.
  - Authenticated responses may expose `questionnaireVersion` and `revision`; v2 saves send `questionnaireVersion: 2` and must send `expectedRevision`. Omitting the version remains a valid legacy request.
  - Version-aware strict replacement of the selected step and revision-fenced write semantics are implemented. Stale expected revisions are rejected rather than merged silently.
  - V2 adds bounded complaint tags, baseline measurements, structured medical history, symptom review, and reproductive-health context. These fields are clinical context only: they add no deterministic safety red-flag field and establish no new red-flag policy.
  - Existing version-1 submitted records remain readable with absent version/revision fields; no migration or rewrite is required.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Add separate v2 routes or a second intake item: rejected because the version belongs to the representation and write semantics, not to a new resource or access pattern.
  - Replace the public questionnaire at the same time: rejected because public compatibility is an explicit boundary of this task.
  - Backfill submitted version-1 records: rejected because absence already identifies the legacy shape and rewriting submitted clinical records adds risk without a read requirement.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: authenticated clients can adopt structured v2 fields incrementally while existing clients and submitted records continue to work.
  - Positive: optimistic revision fencing gives the implementation a defined way to prevent lost updates without adding storage indexes.
  - Trade-off: the backend and authenticated UI implementation is present, but operational qualification remains a separate evidence boundary.
  - Safety boundary: structured context does not change the existing safety screen or deterministic red-flag router; any future policy input requires a separate clinical-safety decision.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `architecture/DATA_MODEL.md`, `architecture/DECISIONS.md`
### ADR-20260913-04: Care Recovery Roadmap as a single-view-model ECG journey tracker

- Date: 2026-09-13
- Status: Accepted
- Context: The patient home Care Recovery Roadmap had been patched several times (wavy SVG, zigzag chart, ReUI tab stepper). The last version kept its steps in component state and let a clicked tab rewrite step statuses, forced step 1 to look active when nothing had happened, marked every step before the focused one complete, and rendered a separate "How care works here" explainer when there was no booking.
- Decision:
  - **One pure view model.** `buildJourney` (`src/lib/patient/careJourney.ts`) wraps the unchanged `deriveCareRoadmap` and returns `{ phase, steps, activeStepIndex, banner }`. The desktop chart's colour boundary, node styles, popovers, CTA bar and mobile stepper all read that object; none holds step state.
  - **Current-step rule.** The first step something is genuinely happening on; otherwise the step after the furthest one reached; `-1` when the journey has not started or every step is done. Skipped (`muted`) steps behind it draw as passed, with their summary still saying nothing was recorded.
  - **Zero state replaces the explainer card.** With no booking, all four steps render locked (including the first) and the CTA bar offers Consult Now.
  - **Layered chart.** Path and node positions share one 0–100 coordinate space and depend only on step count; animations change opacity or a mask transform only, and pause under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
  - **No ETAs.** Active-step copy says what happens next, never how long it takes (ADR-20260806-02).
- Alternatives considered:
  - Pulse only genuinely active steps and show no current step otherwise: rejected; a scheduled consult would leave the patient with no "you are here" and no next action.
  - Generic "usually takes a few hours" expectation-setters: rejected under ADR-20260806-02.
  - Switch layouts with a viewport hook: rejected; both trees render and CSS hides one at `md`, avoiding a server/client mismatch.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the CTA bar, line boundary and active node cannot disagree; a test re-renders with two data states and asserts all of them move together.
  - Trade-off: a scheduled consultation pulses as the current step although nobody is working on it yet; its copy ("Starts at your appointment time") states that plainly.
  - Trade-off: the `complete` phase is unreachable on the consult branch from real reads, because a follow-up is never recorded as done.
  - **Not qualified.** Unit and render tests only; not visually verified in a browser or against a deployed API.
- Related files: `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/lib/patient/careJourney.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/patient/components/homepage/CareRecoveryRoadmap.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/patient/components/homepage/journey/`

### ADR-20260914-01: Isolate staging for PHI-bearing beta testing

- Date: 2026-09-14
- Status: Accepted — applied to staging on 2026-09-14; 15 beta accounts independently verified
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Context: Staging served a separate Amplify frontend but read dev Terraform state and used dev's HTTP/WebSocket APIs, Cognito pool, and DynamoDB tables. That contradicted ADR-20260904-01's classification of staging as a PHI-bearing beta environment and made beta activity indistinguishable from synthetic development data.
- Decision:
  - Staging owns its Cognito pool, `app_core` and `app_audit_ai` tables, private buckets, HTTP and WebSocket APIs, Lambda fleet, three CDS KMS keys, assessment-first queues/backup/audit controls, notifications, provider-secret placeholders, and Amplify build configuration. It has no `terraform_remote_state` dependency on dev.
  - The existing staging Amplify app and `staging.bayanhealth.co` association remain in staging state and are rewired to staging-owned API/auth outputs.
  - Cognito deletion protection is active. DynamoDB PITR, CDS audit/reconciliation, and protected-environment alarm definitions remain enabled. Payments remain on the ledger path with PayRex disabled; unqualified request-response flags and model-upgrade presets remain disabled/unset.
  - The named beta cohort is provisioned by an idempotent staging-only operator script. Password material must arrive through `STAGING_BETA_ACCOUNT_PASSWORD`, never source or Terraform state. Cognito users are confirmed, email-verified, and single-role. Doctor profiles are approved and indexed using clearly synthetic staging licence identifiers; patient profiles carry only the supplied test display names.
  - Staging data remains PHI-bearing despite the test-address format. End-of-cycle deletion and closed Cloudflare Access remain required by ADR-20260904-01.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Keep staging on dev and only add users: rejected because identities, clinical records, audit evidence, and destructive testing would remain commingled with development.
  - Clone dev state/resources manually: rejected because long-lived AWS resources must remain Terraform-defined and manual cloning creates immediate drift.
  - Enable PayRex or an unqualified Together.ai upgrade preset with the new stack: rejected because backend isolation does not qualify providers or authorize payment traffic.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: beta accounts and records have an isolated authorization, data, audit, recovery, and teardown boundary.
  - Positive: staging can now provide real staging qualification evidence instead of re-labelling dev evidence.
  - Trade-off: staging incurs the full protected-environment fixed cost while active and requires its own Together.ai and Daily credentials before those integrations can work beyond their safe fallback/error paths.
  - Trade-off: the account's regional Lambda concurrency quota is currently 10, which AWS requires to remain entirely unreserved. Staging therefore uses account-level unreserved concurrency until the quota is raised; module validation forbids carrying that exception into production.
  - Trade-off: the first apply is large and rewires the existing frontend; a reviewed saved plan and post-apply identity/API verification are mandatory.
- Related files: `infra/environments/staging/main.tf`, `infra/environments/staging/variables.tf`, `infra/environments/staging/outputs.tf`, `.github/workflows/deploy-staging.yml`, `backend/scripts/seed-staging-beta-accounts.ts`, `backend/package.json`

### ADR-20260914-02: Disclose vitals, allergies, and red-flag screening pre-acceptance in the on-demand request pool

- Date: 2026-09-14
- Status: Accepted and implemented in the repository
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Supersedes: the "no full intake body" language of ADR-20260805-04 and the pre-acceptance review UI's own prior doc comment, which listed vitals, allergies, and red-flag screening as deliberately withheld
- Context: The doctor-facing pre-acceptance review (`TriageDetailsModal.tsx`) showed literally every field the request-pool endpoint disclosed — service type, channel, time, price, patient name, and a bounded chief-complaint excerpt — and nothing more, by design: `canReadIntake` (`backend/src/handlers/intake.ts`) restricts the full intake record to the assigned doctor, since a doctor who has not accepted is not yet the treating clinician. Product direction is that this is too little for a doctor to judge an on-demand case before claiming it, and asked for a more comprehensive pre-acceptance review carrying the patient's submitted intake detail.
- Decision:
  - `GET /v1/doctors/me/request-pool` (`OnDemandRequest`) additionally discloses `vitals`, `allergies`, and `safetyScreen` (the structured red-flag screening answers: `chestPain`, `dyspnea`, `feverDays`), each present only once the patient has submitted intake — the same gating already used for `reasonExcerpt`.
  - The full intake body otherwise remains gated to the assigned doctor: no `patientId`, date of birth, contact details, OLDCART detail, medical history, medications, or reproductive health pre-acceptance. `canReadIntake` and the authenticated `GET /v1/bookings/{bookingId}/intake` route are unchanged.
  - `TriageDetailsModal.tsx` renders the newly disclosed fields and its own doc comment and on-screen disclosure copy are updated to describe what is now shown rather than what is withheld.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Keep the pool response as-is and only restyle the modal's presentation of the existing fields: rejected — the product ask was for more clinical substance pre-acceptance, not a denser layout of the same four fields.
  - Widen `canReadIntake` so any consult-approved doctor can read the full intake record pre-acceptance: rejected — that would disclose OLDCART detail, medical history, medications, and reproductive health to doctors who never take the case, which is a materially larger disclosure than the product ask called for.
- Consequences:
