  - Positive: a doctor can now weigh the clinical shape of an on-demand case — not just its administrative shape — before deciding whether to accept it.
  - Trade-off: a doctor browsing the pool now learns vitals, allergies, and red-flag answers for patients they may never treat. Accepted on the same basis as `patientName`'s disclosure under ADR-20260808-02: the pool is readable only by consult-approved doctors and scoped to unclaimed requests.
  - Not qualified: no clinical or legal review of this disclosure boundary was performed as part of this change; it followed from product direction given during the UI request. Revisit before this reaches a real patient population if that review has not happened by then.
- Related files: `contracts/openapi.yaml`, `backend/src/contracts/openapi.generated.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/types/openapi.generated.ts`, `backend/src/lib/on-demand-pool.ts`, `backend/src/handlers/on-demand-pool.test.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/lib/api/requestPool.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/homepage/TriageDetailsModal.tsx`
### ADR-20260914-03: Separate live consultation support from deliberate post-consult documentation

- Date: 2026-09-14
- Status: Accepted and implemented in the repository
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Extends: ADR-20260703-01, ADR-20260704-01, and the consultation-media-layer specification
- Context: The consultation room and post-consult workspace had accumulated conflicting interaction models: a live SOAP scratchpad beside the call despite assessment-first post-consult gating, audio transmission beginning immediately on join, automatic Final ICD generation on workspace mount, and diagnostic-order controls before their operational path was ready. The combined doctor and patient redesign needs one explicit boundary between live encounter support and physician-authorized post-consult mutations.
- Decision:
  - The live consultation room provides video, chat, and a read-only structured intake reference. It does not host a SOAP/clinical-document scratchpad; physician documentation begins in the post-consult Assessment workspace after the canonical session ends.
  - Daily rooms and the client call object start with both local microphone and camera inactive. Each participant enables each track through its own explicit, accessible control. This amends the prior audio-first default in the consultation-media-layer requirements and `TELECONSULT_VIDEO_AND_AI_SCRIBE.md`; it does not change recording consent or provider qualification boundaries.
  - Confirming an Assessment unlocks eligible protected tools but does not itself generate Final ICD. Final ICD generation requires a fresh physician click in the confirmed Assessment card, remains bound to that Assessment version and the server-side gate, and is listed as missing by finish readiness until a current artifact exists.
  - Final ICD remains reviewed in the Assessment card rather than through the generic deliverable finalize/release controls. Finish readiness aggregates every current artifact revision for the other eligible output types so one array entry cannot hide an outstanding generated or finalized artifact.
  - Lab and imaging request controls remain visible as coming-soon capabilities but cannot be generated from this workspace until their operational path is implemented and separately qualified.
  - `Finish documentation` is a navigation/closure acknowledgment, not a clinical publication mutation. After Assessment confirmation it may proceed only through a warning that enumerates missing, generated-but-unreviewed, and finalized-but-unreleased eligible work; it does not bypass the server-side finalize or release transitions for any artifact.
  - The doctor command center may spotlight one active on-demand encounter, but every assigned ready encounter remains reachable in the queue because no server invariant limits a doctor to one accepted booking.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: no media or protected generation starts from page entry or remount; the physician's point-of-action remains explicit and operation-specific.
  - Positive: live call space is less cramped and the clinical workspace has one authoritative documentation phase.
  - Trade-off: physicians must explicitly synchronize the ICD code and may see the same spotlighted encounter in the command center and queue; this duplication is preferred to making accepted patients unreachable.
  - Not qualified: these repository changes do not constitute staging, Daily-provider, clinical-workflow, canary, or production evidence.
- Related files: `.kiro/specs/consultation-media-layer/requirements.md`, `.kiro/specs/consultation-media-layer/design.md`, `.kiro/specs/consultation-media-layer/tasks.md`, `architecture/TELECONSULT_VIDEO_AND_AI_SCRIBE.md`, `backend/src/lib/video-provider.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/media/components/ConsultationVideo.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/session/ConsultationRoom.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/AssessmentFirstWorkspace.tsx`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/consultation/components/postConsultation/workspacePhase.ts`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/src/features/doctor/components/homepage/DoctorPatientQueue.tsx`
### ADR-20260914-04: Activate demo-grade CDS policy for the current staging doctor canary

- Date: 2026-09-14
- Status: Accepted — applied and read-smoked in staging on 2026-09-14 for the five current doctor accounts; authenticated write-generation, provider, clinical, and production qualification remain unproven
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Extends: ADR-20260914-01; adds bounded post-apply CDS runtime evidence without changing the staging isolation design
- Context: The isolated staging stack had a completed synthetic consultation with canonical session, Assessment, Head, and Gate records, but all three post-consult reads returned concealed `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` responses because `CDS#POLICY#staging / ACTIVE` did not exist. The authorized operation was to make the demo-grade policy available only to every doctor account that existed in staging at execution time, without exposing it to patients, administrators, future doctors, or unrelated infrastructure changes.
- Decision:
  - Staging policy revision `1` is active in `canary` mode with ruleset `demo-ruleset-v1`, catalog `demo-catalog-f0c847751f235b25`, activation time `2026-09-14T11:29:24.161Z`, and actor `ben_bayanhealth_20260914_staging_canary`.
  - The allowlist is the lowercase SHA-256 Cognito-subject digests of the five enabled, confirmed users then present in staging's `doctor` group: `34f02ed28f5418c88d432c184bfcb09777e881d024269b702d7e95d36bd8262f`, `dfad09be75cea0627bd6c2a63b0fada1dd16d7638be782a75192e50c838a654c`, `5c3b1b6d8cff5ca34554f6078c8b3cf9c6d61faa8183b00bab941aa2a812fadf`, `9a5c282fd1c29f5bb28097922b1fa69a4caca64e30f53237094515afbe3b7adc`, and `7a454ffc04082c7fef121981d52aeff66ce00dbec996fbca7e5b7bda455aefa8`. Patients, administrators, and later-created doctors remain excluded unless a separately authorized Terraform change adds them.
  - Terraform applied only the CDS Lambda environment variable `CDS_GENERATION_CANARY_DIGESTS`: `0` resources added, `1` changed, and `0` destroyed. A full plan containing 23 unrelated Lambda/configuration changes was rejected. The deployed CDS code remained unchanged at `CodeSha256` `MyHDQRGVtHQJP6nyUnPJZRTN/p8XVug9gVGY0VlS1CA=`.
  - Readiness after activation reported zero blockers and two warnings: actors outside the five-entry canary receive `503`, and `J06.9` has no patient-education article so the generic deterministic fallback remains available.
  - A read-only direct Lambda smoke against synthetic consultation `con_mu13mqa2oab7m7lsbj0` returned `200` for Assessment, Current, and History, replacing the original three policy-missing `404` responses. Assessment remained unconfirmed with lock reason `clinical_input_changed`; Current and History correctly returned empty output collections.
  - Rollback requires its own operation-specific authorization: activate policy revision `2` as `disabled`, remove the five Terraform allowlist digests, review the resulting scoped plan, and apply only that configuration removal. Existing immutable components and audit records are retained.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Activate the policy globally with `generationMode: enabled`: rejected because demo-grade content and unqualified generation must not be exposed to every staging actor.
  - Configure the Lambda directly with AWS CLI: rejected because long-lived Lambda configuration is Terraform-managed and an out-of-band mutation would create drift.
  - Apply the full or ordinary targeted Terraform plan: rejected because local/deployed bundles differed and the plans included unrelated code/configuration changes outside the authorized operation.
  - Confirm the Assessment or generate an artifact through a fabricated direct-Lambda identity: rejected because that would create clinical records without a genuine authenticated physician interaction.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: the original post-consult policy lookup failure is resolved, and protected generation is configuration-reachable for exactly the five current staging doctors.
  - Positive: the activation is bounded, auditable, Terraform-defined, and reversible without deleting immutable policy or audit history.
  - Trade-off: each future staging doctor remains denied until their digest is added through a separately reviewed and authorized Terraform operation.
  - Not qualified: no API Gateway or browser smoke with a genuine JWT ran because `STAGING_BETA_ACCOUNT_PASSWORD` was unavailable locally. No Assessment-confirmation or generation write was performed, so this is not full end-to-end clinical-workflow, representative Together.ai, staging-wide, canary-observation, production, or GA evidence. Python infrastructure tests also did not run because the local Python 3.13 environment lacks `pytest`; no dependency was installed.
- Related files: `backend/scripts/seed-cds-demo-policy.ts`, `infra/environments/staging/main.tf`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`
### ADR-20260914-05: Replace the unavailable staging serverless CDS model with Qwen3.6-Plus

- Date: 2026-09-14
- Status: Accepted — applied to staging on 2026-09-14; configuration verified, protected-generation output not yet re-smoked
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Extends: ADR-20260914-04; changes only the Together.ai model preset used by its five-doctor synthetic canary
- Context: Live staging generation reached an `ALLOW` gate decision with a `ROUTINE` protected-generation red-flag evaluation, but Plan, Prescription, and Patient Education all recorded provider errors and published deterministic templates. A minimal non-clinical request using staging's credential and exact active slug returned Together HTTP `400`, `invalid_request_error`, code `model_not_available`: `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-Turbo` remained listed in the model catalog but was no longer callable through the serverless completions API and required a dedicated endpoint. The credential itself authenticated successfully and the canary policy was not the failing boundary.
- Decision:
  - Set both staging Terraform inputs, `cds_soap_model_preset` and `cds_patient_model_preset`, to the existing `qwen3.6-plus` profile. That profile selects `Qwen/Qwen3.6-Plus`, requires streaming, disables reasoning output, allows 4096 SOAP tokens, and uses a 26-second provider timeout below the 30-second API Gateway/Lambda ceiling.
  - Preserve policy revision `1`, `generationMode: canary`, and the existing five-doctor subject-digest allowlist. This model change does not authorize patients, administrators, future doctors, or global generation.
  - Reject the first targeted Terraform plan because it also proposed local CDS and Post-Confirmation bundle/hash changes. Apply the regenerated exact saved plan only after temporary planning guards reduced it to the two model environment variables on `bayanhealth-staging-cds`.
  - The applied result was `0` resources added, `1` changed, and `0` destroyed. Live Lambda verification reported `LastUpdateStatus: Successful`, both presets as `qwen3.6-plus`, the unchanged five-entry canary allowlist, and unchanged deployed code hash `MyHDQRGVtHQJP6nyUnPJZRTN/p8XVug9gVGY0VlS1CA=`. Temporary guards and the saved plan were removed immediately.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Keep Qwen2.5-7B and rely on deterministic templates: rejected because the provider explicitly rejects that slug on the serverless API, so it cannot produce AI output.
  - Provision a dedicated Qwen2.5 endpoint: rejected because it introduces a new long-lived provider resource and cost outside the bounded staging fix.
  - Switch to `kimi-k2.6`: not selected because the requested staging value was `qwen3.6-plus`; both remain unqualified candidates.
  - Apply the first targeted plan: rejected because changing Lambda code bundles was outside the authorized model-value operation.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: staging no longer selects a model known to fail every serverless completion request.
  - Positive: the fix is Terraform-managed and changed no code, policy record, canary membership, IAM, API route, or other resource.
  - Trade-off: Qwen3.6-Plus has measured latency near 17 seconds and a 26-second timeout, leaving less headroom under the hard 30-second request ceiling than Kimi K2.6.
  - Not qualified: the configuration and Together model selection are verified, but no authenticated Assessment-confirmation or protected-generation write was performed after the switch. An optional minimal direct probe did not return reliable evidence before the local command timed out. This is not representative provider, clinical-workflow, staging-wide, canary-observation, production, or GA qualification; source must be observed as `llm` on a separately authorized synthetic generation before claiming the AI path works end to end.
- Related files: `infra/environments/staging/main.tf`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`, `backend/src/lib/cds/profiles.ts`
### ADR-20260914-06: Add Jerry and Williams to the staging patient cohort through a scoped operator run

- Date: 2026-09-14
- Status: Accepted — two staging patient accounts created and independently verified
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Extends: ADR-20260914-01; adds two named synthetic beta patients without changing environment isolation or role design
- Context: The staging beta-account source listed nineteen identities but did not include Jerry or Williams. The existing operator always iterated over the entire cohort, which would reset attributes and passwords for every beta account when only two new patients were authorized. Neither requested email existed in staging Cognito before the operation.
- Decision:
  - Add `testpatient.jerry@example.com` with display name and slug `Jerry`/`jerry`, and `testpatient.williams@example.com` with display name and slug `Williams`/`williams`, both with role `patient`, to the authoritative staging cohort. The source cohort now contains twenty-one entries; this operation independently verifies only the two additions rather than retroactively re-verifying every prior entry.
  - Add `--accounts=<comma-separated slugs>` to the staging operator. Omitted selection preserves the deployment workflow's full-cohort behavior; an explicit selection rejects empty, duplicate, or unknown slugs and limits every identity/profile mutation to those entries.
  - Execute the operator with `--accounts=jerry,williams` using password material supplied only at the point of action and not stored in source, Terraform state, logs, or this evidence record. Invitation delivery remains suppressed.
  - Verify both users through a separate `--verify-only` run and direct Cognito reads. Each user is enabled, `CONFIRMED`, email-verified, has the requested display name, belongs to exactly the `patient` group, and has a strongly read staging patient profile.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Run the existing unscoped cohort operator: rejected because it would unnecessarily update every beta identity, password, role membership, and profile outside the two-account authorization.
  - Create the accounts only with ad hoc Cognito CLI calls: rejected because the authoritative cohort and required application profiles would drift from the deployed identities.
  - Store the supplied password in repository configuration: rejected because staging credentials must remain out of source and Terraform state.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: Jerry and Williams can authenticate as isolated staging patients, and their identities remain reproducible through the standard operator.
  - Positive: future bounded cohort repairs can select named accounts without mutating the rest of the cohort.
  - Trade-off: the operator still verifies expected group presence rather than enforcing exclusivity for arbitrary pre-existing users; exact single-role membership was therefore checked directly for these two newly created identities.
  - Not qualified: identity, group, and profile persistence were verified, but no browser login or authenticated patient API workflow was run. This is not clinical-workflow, staging-wide cohort, production, or GA evidence.
- Related files: `backend/scripts/seed-staging-beta-accounts.ts`, `architecture/SESSION_HANDOFF.md`
### ADR-20260914-07: Publish the complete `$default` WebSocket invoke URL

- Date: 2026-09-14
- Status: Accepted — applied and rebuilt successfully in staging on 2026-09-14 (Amplify job 11)
- Owner: Lead Engineer
- Context: The HTTP API module created the WebSocket stage as `$default`, but exported only the API host. API Gateway does not route a WebSocket handshake on that bare host to the `$default` stage: live staging returned `403 {"message":"Forbidden"}` without invoking the `$connect` Lambda. The identical handshake at `/$default` invoked Lambda and returned its expected application response. Amplify consumed the incomplete output as `NEXT_PUBLIC_WS_URL`, so every browser realtime attempt failed before token validation and fell back to HTTP.
- Decision:
  - `module.http_api.websocket_api_endpoint` is the complete browser invoke URL and includes the literal `/$default` stage path.
  - Amplify, environment outputs, CI, local examples, and operator documentation consume or show that complete URL; clients do not reconstruct stage paths.
  - `WEBSOCKET_API_ENDPOINT` remains the HTTPS management endpoint and already included `/$default`; no Lambda configuration or authorization change is needed.
- Alternatives considered:
  - Append `/$default` only in the frontend: rejected because Terraform's public output would remain unusable and every non-browser client would need to rediscover the same missing stage.
  - Rename the stage to `dev`/`staging`: rejected because it changes invoke and management URLs without addressing the incorrect output contract.
  - Widen Lambda invocation permission: rejected because the existing API/stage/route wildcard is correct and requests to the complete invoke URL already reach Lambda.
- Consequences:
  - Positive: newly applied Amplify environment variables produce a WebSocket URL that reaches `$connect`; opaque token validation and HTTP fallback behavior remain unchanged.
  - Trade-off: `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` values are build-time inputs, so an environment needs a Terraform apply followed by an Amplify rebuild before an existing browser bundle is corrected.
  - Evidence boundary: a saved staging plan containing only two in-place Amplify environment-variable changes was applied (`0` added, `2` changed, `0` destroyed), and both live app and branch configuration values were read back as the complete `/$default` URL. Staging Amplify RELEASE job `11` then completed with BUILD, DEPLOY, and VERIFY all `SUCCEED`, so the corrected build-time value is deployed to the staging browser bundle.
- Related files: `infra/modules/http_api/outputs.tf`, `infra/modules/http_api/main.tf`, `infra/modules/amplify_hosting/main.tf`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `frontend/bayan-health-mvp/.env.example`, `architecture/ENVIRONMENT_SETUP.md`, `architecture/LOCAL_FRONTEND_DEV_SERVER.md`
